**What this rule forecloses, explicitly, so it cannot be quietly reintroduced later:** no future implementation may add a "best-effort" or "degrade gracefully" branch to `put_started_once`/`write_raw_results_once` (or any other Category 1/2 write) that proceeds without a resolved hold state under sustained contention, on the reasoning that "audit execution availability matters more here." That trade-off was considered (§19.13, prior revision) and Product Strategy's decision forecloses it — the write must fail, the caller must receive a distinguishable error (§19.15), and the audit's execution orchestration (outside this workstream's scope) is responsible for whatever retry/alerting/operational response that failure triggers upstream.

**Operational consequence, restated plainly rather than left implicit:** sustained DynamoDB throttling or contention on a specific audit's `LegalHold` item is now a real, if narrow, way an audit *execution* can fail — a change to Phase 1's operational risk profile that did not exist before this workstream, since `put_started_once` previously could not fail for hold-related reasons at all (hold-checking did not exist). This is accepted, not hidden: it is the direct, necessary consequence of treating legal hold as a constitutional evidence-integrity control that must never silently degrade, exactly as Product Strategy's Decision 1 reasoning states for the S3 marker mechanism, applied here to the fail-closed posture as a whole.

### 19.15 Governed Error Classification for Retry Exhaustion

Every failure mode this amendment introduces must be distinguishable by an operator or caller from an ordinary AWS API failure — confirmed sufficient using this codebase's **existing, already-established pattern**: a distinct `StorageError` subclass or a distinct string `code` attribute on a common `StorageError` base, exactly as `DuplicateRunIdError`, `ConditionalWriteError`, and `CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING` already do elsewhere in this same codebase. No new error-handling architecture, exception hierarchy, or status-code scheme is introduced — this confirms sufficiency of the existing pattern rather than inventing a new one.

| Code | Raised when | Distinguishable from |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `HOLD_STATE_CONCURRENCY_EXCEEDED` (§19.4) | The `LegalHold` `hold_version` `ConditionCheck` fails on every attempt up to the bounded retry limit — a genuine, sustained hold-state race, not a one-off collision. | An ordinary DynamoDB throttling error (`ProvisionedThroughputExceededException`/`ThrottlingException`, surfaced via the existing `storage_error_from_dynamodb_client_error` path, unrelated to hold contention specifically) and a genuine duplicate-write condition on the governed record's own item (surfaced as the write path's existing, unchanged error — `DuplicateRunIdError`, `ConditionalWriteError`, etc. — per §19.4 step 4's explicit precedence order, never conflated with a hold-version retry). |
| `HOLD_MARKER_ESTABLISHMENT_FAILED` (§19.5.6) | The canary marker's `PutObject` or its `HeadObject` read-back fails on every attempt up to the bounded retry limit. | An ordinary S3 error unrelated to the marker (e.g., a failure writing the evidence object itself, which retains its own existing error path and is never reported as a marker failure) and `HOLD_STATE_CONCURRENCY_EXCEEDED` above (a DynamoDB-side condition failure, not an S3-side write failure — these are always distinguishable by which store's operation actually failed). |

Both codes are raised **before** any partial write of the governed record/object is attempted or could be observed by a caller as having succeeded — consistent with §19.14's "no partial governed record may ever be represented as successfully persisted." An operator or an upstream caller inspecting either code knows immediately, without needing to inspect logs or infer from a generic timeout, that the failure is a legal-hold-coordination failure specifically, not an unrelated AWS service degradation.

### 19.16 Phase 4 Transaction Item and Byte Budget (`put_records_once`, `put_lineage_page_once`)

**Correction (Product Strategy — session-continuation verification found the prior "5-item transaction" characterization inaccurate):** §18.1 item 8's "five record kinds" describes five *kinds* of record `put_records_once` writes, not a fixed five-item `TransactWriteItems` call. Direct re-read of `AggregationOrchestrator._build_persisted_records` confirms the actual per-call item count is **`4 + 3N`**, where N is the number of distinct endpoints represented in the aggregation (1 audit lineage manifest + N endpoint lineage manifests + 1 audit aggregate + 1 audit failure-classification + N endpoint aggregates + N endpoint failure-classifications + 1 aggregate-set-completion marker). Even the simplest realistic case (N=1) is 7 items, not 5. §19.4 step 3 Item B and §19.7 item 8 are corrected accordingly; §19.10's item (e) description is corrected to match.

#### 19.16.1 Transaction Item Budget

DynamoDB's `TransactWriteItems` hard cap is 100 items total, counting every item type in the request (`Put`, `ConditionCheck`, or otherwise) — this figure should be independently reconfirmed against current AWS documentation at implementation time, not treated as fixed by this document alone.

Transaction splitting across multiple `TransactWriteItems` calls is **not authorized** (Product Strategy decision): it would weaken aggregate-set atomicity and complicate the deterministic retry and completion semantics `aggregate_set_exists`'s all-or-nothing correctness argument depends on. Instead, one of the 100 slots is permanently reserved for the appended `LegalHold.hold_version` `ConditionCheck` (§19.4 step 3 Item A):

- Maximum governed record items (existing `Put`s, item 8's `4 + 3N`): **99** (`MAX_AGGREGATE_RECORDS` in `aggregation/constants.py`, corrected from its prior value of 100).
- One appended `LegalHold` `ConditionCheck`.
- Maximum total transaction items: **100**.

Solving `4 + 3N ≤ 99` gives **N ≤ 31** — the maximum number of distinct endpoints one aggregation call may cover under the current record shape. 31 endpoints produce 97 governed items (98 total, within budget); 32 endpoints produce 100 governed items (101 total, over budget).

This limit is checked deterministically, client-side, in `put_records_once`, before any AWS request is made — never discovered via an AWS-side rejection. If a future requirement needs more than 31 endpoints in one aggregate transaction, that is its own, separately reviewed architecture decision (e.g., revisiting the no-split constraint above) — not a silent adjustment to this limit, and not something this correction authorizes.

**Error wording:** the rejection error must not suggest an unauthorized remediation. It must not read as "reduce endpoint count or split the aggregation" (the latter clause implies transaction splitting, which this section explicitly forecloses). Approved wording: *"Aggregate set exceeds the governed atomic-transaction limit. Reduce the configured endpoint scope or obtain a separately reviewed architecture decision."*

#### 19.16.2 Post-Augmentation Per-Item Size Validation

`custody_expires_at`, `ttl_disposal_at` (when applicable), and `evidence_class` are merged onto every governed item as part of this workstream's correction (§18.1) — this increases each item's persisted size relative to its pre-amendment shape. The existing per-item size guard (`MAX_AGGREGATE_ITEM_BYTES` in `aggregation/constants.py`) must be evaluated **after** these governance attributes are merged, never before. A pre-augmentation check is insufficient by construction: an item just under the ceiling before augmentation can be pushed over it by the added attributes, and a check performed before augmentation would never observe that.

This requirement applies identically to every item passed to `put_records_once` (all `4 + 3N` governed items) and to the single item passed to `put_lineage_page_once` — both write paths receive the same three governance attributes and are subject to the same post-augmentation per-item guard. Any item exceeding `MAX_AGGREGATE_ITEM_BYTES` after augmentation is rejected deterministically, before any AWS request is made.

#### 19.16.3 Deterministic Conservative Transaction-Size Measurement Contract

The transaction-level byte guard (`MAX_AGGREGATE_TRANSACTION_BYTES` in `aggregation/constants.py`) is evaluated over the **final wire-format transaction actions**, not over the pre-encoding Python record dicts, via one deterministic sequence:

1. Governance fields (`custody_expires_at`, `ttl_disposal_at` where applicable, `evidence_class`) are merged onto every governed record first.
2. Each augmented record is encoded into DynamoDB `AttributeValue` wire format.
3. The final `Put` transact-items are constructed from the encoded items.
4. The `LegalHold.hold_version` `ConditionCheck` transact-item is constructed.
5. The complete final transact-item list — every `Put` and the appended `ConditionCheck` together — is measured through one canonical, deterministic serialization (the same serialization used for every measurement, so repeated evaluation of the same input always yields the same result).
6. If that measurement exceeds `MAX_AGGREGATE_TRANSACTION_BYTES`, the transaction is rejected before any AWS request is made.

**This measurement is an RCP-owned conservative preflight guard, not a reproduction of AWS's internal transaction-size accounting.** It may intentionally count request-structure overhead (attribute names, type descriptors, the `ConditionCheck`'s own structure) that AWS's own internal accounting may compute differently, and may therefore reject a transaction AWS itself would have accepted. This is acceptable: it fails safely, in the direction of rejecting early rather than discovering a violation only after submission. This document does not claim byte-for-byte equivalence with AWS's internal accounting, and no future implementation may claim this measurement is exact proof of AWS acceptance. Operational confirmation against real AWS behavior remains part of the later deployment and acceptance gate — out of scope for this document and for A1.3c's own test suite (§19.16.4), which proves RCP's own guard behavior only.

`MAX_AGGREGATE_TRANSACTION_BYTES`'s existing value (3.8 MB) is not changed by this correction — the governance attributes' and appended `ConditionCheck`'s worst-case combined contribution is small relative to the existing margin below DynamoDB's total-request-size limit — but this document does not assert that margin is sufficient by arithmetic alone; §19.16.4's boundary tests are the authoritative proof, to be run against the corrected measurement sequence above, not against the pre-amendment calculation.

#### 19.16.4 Required Test Coverage

In addition to §19.11's existing categories, extended to Phase 4's variable-length transaction:

- **31-endpoint boundary**: 97 governed items + 1 appended `ConditionCheck` = 98 total — must succeed (subject to the byte guard).
- **32-endpoint rejection**: 100 governed items — must be rejected deterministically before any AWS request; no `transact_write_items` call occurs.
- **Post-augmentation per-item rejection**: an item just below `MAX_AGGREGATE_ITEM_BYTES` before governance-attribute augmentation but above it afterward — must be rejected, with no transaction call made; covering both `put_records_once` and `put_lineage_page_once`.
- **Transaction byte-boundary — below**: a final, fully-constructed transact-item list (post-augmentation, post-encoding, including the appended `ConditionCheck`) just under `MAX_AGGREGATE_TRANSACTION_BYTES` — accepted.
- **Transaction byte-boundary — above**: the symmetric case just over the limit — rejected, with no AWS request made.
- **Governance-attribute inclusion in the transaction measurement**: a record set under budget before governance-attribute merge but over budget only after — must be rejected, proving the measurement is not evaluated against stale, pre-merge sizes.
- **Appended-`ConditionCheck` inclusion in the transaction measurement**: the measurement must include the `ConditionCheck` transact-item's own serialized contribution, not only the `Put` items.
- **Determinism**: repeated evaluation of the identical input transaction produces the identical measurement and the identical accept/reject outcome.
- **Variable cancellation-reason indexing**: parameterized across multiple N (e.g., N=1, N=10, N=31), proving the existing, unmodified `HoldCoordinatedTransactionRunner`'s `CancellationReasons[:len(governed_items)]` slicing correctly isolates governed-item failures from the appended hold-check failure at every size — no change to `hold_coordination.py` is required, since it was never hardcoded to a fixed governed-item count.
- **Governed-condition vs. hold-condition precedence**: both fail in the same attempt — the governed failure must win, per §19.4 step 4's existing, unmodified precedence rule, never masked as a hold-version retry.

This test suite proves RCP's own preflight-guard behavior deterministically. It does not, and is not represented as, proof of AWS's internal `TransactWriteItems` byte-accounting implementation (§19.16.3).

#### 19.16.5 Custody-Period Configuration Source for `aggregate_metadata`

**Correction (Product Strategy — A1.3c.1 pre-implementation verification found no custody-period configuration channel existed for Phase 4's evidence class):** §18.1 and this section both require every governed Phase 4 DynamoDB row to carry `evidence_class = aggregate_metadata` and, per Category 1's custody-field requirement, `custody_expires_at`/`ttl_disposal_at`. Neither this document nor the companion ADR's Decision 5 (prior to its own A1.3c.1 amendment) ever named a configuration source `custody_expires_at` could be computed from for this specific evidence class — the only custody-period environment variable in the codebase, `CUSTODY_PERIOD_DAYS_RAW_EVIDENCE`, is scoped exclusively to RunMetadata (A1.3b/A1.LH3). This is now corrected:

- **Runtime environment variable**: `CUSTODY_PERIOD_DAYS_AGGREGATE_METADATA`, read by `AggregationRepository`'s governed write paths (`put_records_once`, `put_lineage_page_once`) at write time.
- **Stack configuration source**: `custom.custodyPeriodDays.aggregate_metadata.${stage}`, per the companion ADR's amended Decision 5.
- `custody_expires_at` is computed **independently, at each write's own time**, from this value — the same "never copied, never chained, computed fresh from the shared policy" discipline already established for every other custody-bearing write path in this document (§18.1's Category 2 clarification, §19.4 step 2). It is never derived from, defaulted to, or borrowed from `raw_evidence`'s or any other evidence class's configured value, per ADR Non-Negotiable Invariant 26.
- **Fail-closed, mirroring the established `raw_evidence` pattern** (`packages/storage/dynamodb_client.py::_resolve_custody_period_days_env`): if `CUSTODY_PERIOD_DAYS_AGGREGATE_METADATA` is unset, empty, non-numeric, zero, negative, or otherwise not a positive integer, the governed write must raise `CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING` — this exact code, not an equivalent or substitute — **before** any hold-state read, any transact-item construction, or any AWS request — never silently omitting `custody_expires_at`, never substituting a default, and never proceeding with a partially-governed record.
- No Phase 4 governed record (any of the `4 + 3N` items from `put_records_once`, or a `put_lineage_page_once` item) may ever be persisted missing `custody_expires_at` or `evidence_class` — both must be present on every write that succeeds, with no partial-success path.
- **No duration value is selected by this correction.** `CUSTODY_PERIOD_DAYS_AGGREGATE_METADATA` and `custom.custodyPeriodDays.aggregate_metadata.${stage}` are named and specified here as the authoritative channel; the numeric value itself remains a separate, lower-priority Product Strategy decision, per AC-A1-5 and the companion ADR's Decision 5 — unchanged by this correction.

#### 19.16.6 Implementation Consequences — Required in A1.3c.1

**Correction (Product Strategy — the prior revision of this subsection incorrectly deferred Lambda-environment wiring; corrected below):** the code-level fail-closed read (§19.16.5) is necessary but not sufficient on its own — without the Lambda `environment:` binding below, `AggregationRepository` can read `os.environ.get("CUSTODY_PERIOD_DAYS_AGGREGATE_METADATA")` in code, but no deployed Phase 4 Lambda process would ever actually receive that variable, and every invocation would fail closed permanently regardless of whether a stage value is later supplied — an unintended, permanent outage rather than an intentional, correctable gate. The environment binding is therefore **required, in-scope A1.3c.1 implementation work**, not deferred:

- Add `aggregate_metadata: {}` to `infra/serverless.yml`'s existing `custom.custodyPeriodDays` block, alongside the existing `raw_evidence`/`intelligence`/`report`/`certificate`/`retention_marker` keys, using the identical empty-mapping, no-fallback pattern already established for every other key in that block. **This key addition alone does not, by itself, cause `sls print`, packaging, or deployment to fail** — an empty mapping with no downstream reference is simply an unused configuration value, not a fail-closed gate.
- Bind the variable on the `auditAggregation` function only:

  ```yaml
  functions:
    auditAggregation:
      environment:
        CUSTODY_PERIOD_DAYS_AGGREGATE_METADATA: ${self:custom.custodyPeriodDays.aggregate_metadata.${self:provider.stage}}
  ```

  **This binding is what creates the fail-closed gate**, not the bare configuration key above: referencing `custom.custodyPeriodDays.aggregate_metadata.${self:provider.stage}` from a function's `environment:` block, while no stage-specific value has been supplied under that key, is what causes Serverless Framework's variable resolution to fail at package/deploy time — the same mechanism already proven for the four S3-backed evidence classes' `Days`/`NoncurrentDays` references.
- **Explicitly prohibited by this correction**: a provider-wide (`provider.environment`) binding; binding this variable to `coreEngineOrchestrator`, `scheduledExecution`, `auditFinalization`, or any function other than `auditAggregation`; any fallback or default value (e.g. `${self:custom.custodyPeriodDays.aggregate_metadata.${self:provider.stage}, 90}` or similar) that would silently resolve instead of failing; and any numeric duration value — this correction names the channel and requires the binding to exist, but selects no value.
- The referenced stage value (`custom.custodyPeriodDays.aggregate_metadata.${stage}`) remains absent for every stage (dev/staging/prod) until a separate, explicit Product Strategy decision supplies it — identical to every other custody-period value in this design.
- `sls print --stage <any>` must fail at variable-resolution time **specifically because `custom.custodyPeriodDays.aggregate_metadata.${self:provider.stage}` is referenced and unresolved** (via the `auditAggregation` environment binding above) — this failed render must not be represented as successful template validation, and must not be conflated with the bare configuration key's own (non-failing) presence.
- No deployment or activation is authorized by this correction.

Required test coverage:

- **Static**: the `aggregate_metadata` configuration key exists in `custom.custodyPeriodDays`.
- **Static**: the `CUSTODY_PERIOD_DAYS_AGGREGATE_METADATA` environment binding exists on `auditAggregation` and on no other function; no `provider.environment` binding exists for this variable.
- **Static**: no fallback or default value is present anywhere in the binding's variable reference.
- **Render**: `sls print --stage <any>` fails, and fails specifically because `custom.custodyPeriodDays.aggregate_metadata.${self:provider.stage}` is unresolved (not some unrelated failure) — the failure is asserted as intentional and fail-closed, never reported as successful rendering.
- **Runtime, missing/invalid configuration**: unset, empty, non-numeric, zero, negative — each raises `CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING` before any hold-state read, transaction construction, or AWS request, per §19.16.5.

Preserved, unchanged by this correction: no duration value is selected; no default is authorized anywhere in the binding; no fifth S3 `LifecycleConfiguration` rule is created (`aggregate_metadata` remains DynamoDB-only, §19.16.5); no provider-wide environment variable is added; no deployment or activation occurs; no A1.3d, migration, or issue #112 work enters scope.

## 20. Phase 5–7 Custody and Legal-Hold Coordination Architecture (A1.3d)

This section defines the architecture for wiring Phase 5 (Intelligence), Phase 6 (Report), and Phase 7 (Certificate) to A1.LH1 legal-hold coordination and custody-field computation, per companion ADR Decision 10, Decision 11, and Non-Negotiable Invariants 27–31. It is written from a read-only investigation verified directly against current repository code (`reliability_intelligence/`, `deterministic_reporting/`, `audit_platform_integrity/`, `operator_cli/main.py`) — every claim below traces to a specific file, not to inference from Phase 1–4's already-solved architecture. This section authorizes A1.3d.0 (this documentation) only. Implementation proceeds as four separately authorized subphases: **A1.3d.1** (shared authoritative custody-configuration foundation), **A1.3d.2** (Phase 5/Intelligence), **A1.3d.3** (Phase 6/Report), **A1.3d.4** (Phase 7/Certificate) — none of the four is authorized by this document. **Correction (A1.3d.2 read-only investigation)**: §20.4/§20.6's original constructor boundary made `hold_repository`/`custody_period_days` mandatory, which conflicted with `IntelligenceRepository`/`IntelligencePublisher` also being constructed by the read-only `retrieve intelligence-*` command. §20.4, §20.5, §20.6, and §20.12's A1.3d.2 entry are corrected accordingly, per new ADR Decision 11 and Invariant 31.

### 20.1 CLI-Only Invocation Boundary

Confirmed structurally: `IntelligenceRepository`, `ReportRepository`, and `CertificationRepository` (and their engines and publishers) are constructed exclusively in `operator_cli/main.py`. Zero references exist under `apps/`; no `functions:` entry exists in `infra/serverless.yml` for any of the three phases. Per ADR Decision 10 / Invariant 28, A1.3d does not change this. Every mechanism defined in this section — hold-version `ConditionCheck` coordination, write-time S3 tagging, custody-period resolution — operates entirely within the existing `operator_cli/main.py` → engine → repository/publisher call chain; none of it requires or implies a Lambda entry point.

### 20.2 Category 3 Exclusion — `IntelligenceJob`, `ReportJob`, `CertificationJob`

Per ADR Decision 8 (already naming these three record types as Category 3 examples) and new Invariant 27: these three record types must never receive `custody_expires_at`, `ttl_disposal_at`, `evidence_class`, an evidence-class S3 tag, or A1.LH1 hold-version transactional coordination of any kind. Their existing write methods (`put_intelligence_job_once`/`update_intelligence_job`, `put_report_job_once`/`update_report_job`, `write_certjob_pending`/`update_certjob_in_progress`/`update_certjob_complete`/`update_certjob_failed`) are unaffected by A1.3d and remain plain conditional/unconditional writes exactly as they exist today. Each of A1.3d.2/.3/.4 must extend that phase's existing `test_engine_no_phaseN_mutation.py` file (already proven, by name and existing purpose, to be the right home for this kind of boundary assertion) with a negative regression test proving the corresponding Job record never carries any of the five governance elements above, even after that phase's Metadata record and S3 artifact are correctly wired.

### 20.3 Authoritative Custody Configuration Source

Per ADR Decision 5 (A1.3d.0 amendment) and Invariant 29: one authoritative, checked-in, stage-indexed configuration source — `config/custody_periods.json` — covers all five evidentiary custody classes (`raw_evidence`, `aggregate_metadata`, `intelligence`, `report`, `certificate`) plus `retention_marker`'s separately governed operational-disposal duration. Fixed schema, no duration values present until a separate, later Product Strategy decision:

```json
{
  "evidentiary_classes": {
    "raw_evidence": {},
    "aggregate_metadata": {},
    "intelligence": {},
    "report": {},
    "certificate": {}
  },
  "operational_durations": {
    "retention_marker": {}
  }
}
```

An empty object under a class means no stage has an authorized value — the identical, already-proven "no default, no fallback" semantics `infra/serverless.yml`'s existing `custom.custodyPeriodDays.<class>: {}` empty-mapping pattern established for every prior evidence class (A1.2, A1.3c.1). A duration is introduced later, one stage at a time, by adding an explicitly authorized positive-integer property — for example (explanatory only; this exact value is never introduced by this document or by A1.3d.1's implementation):

```json
{
  "evidentiary_classes": {
    "intelligence": {
      "dev": 30
    }
  }
}
```

The `evidentiary_classes`/`operational_durations` nesting is the schema's own structural separation — `retention_marker` is never reachable through `evidentiary_classes`, and no evidentiary class is ever reachable through `operational_durations`; no consumer may read across this boundary. The production `config/custody_periods.json` file introduced by A1.3d.1 contains no duration values.

**Fixed lookup paths**, used identically by every consumer:

```text
evidentiary_classes.<evidence_class>.<stage>
```
```text
operational_durations.retention_marker.<stage>
```

**Two consumers, deriving from one file, never from each other:**

- **Serverless/infrastructure**: **all six** of `infra/serverless.yml`'s existing `custom.custodyPeriodDays.<class>` mappings — `raw_evidence`, `aggregate_metadata`, `intelligence`, `report`, `certificate`, `retention_marker` — migrate from empty inline mappings (`{}`) to external-file references, resolved via Serverless Framework's native `${file(...)}` syntax. This is a source-only migration, in-scope, shared foundational work owned entirely by A1.3d.1 (§20.12), not a permanent second authority for any class:

  ```yaml
  custodyPeriodDays:
    raw_evidence: ${file(../config/custody_periods.json):evidentiary_classes.raw_evidence.${self:provider.stage}}
    aggregate_metadata: ${file(../config/custody_periods.json):evidentiary_classes.aggregate_metadata.${self:provider.stage}}
    intelligence: ${file(../config/custody_periods.json):evidentiary_classes.intelligence.${self:provider.stage}}
    report: ${file(../config/custody_periods.json):evidentiary_classes.report.${self:provider.stage}}
    certificate: ${file(../config/custody_periods.json):evidentiary_classes.certificate.${self:provider.stage}}
    retention_marker: ${file(../config/custody_periods.json):operational_durations.retention_marker.${self:provider.stage}}
  ```

  The four already-provisioned (A1.2) S3-backed `LifecycleConfiguration` rules (`raw_evidence`, `intelligence`, `report`, `certificate` — see §20.9) and the separately governed `retention-marker-disposal` rule continue consuming `custom.custodyPeriodDays.*` exactly as today — `infra/resources/s3.yml` itself is structurally unchanged, since only the value each `custom.custodyPeriodDays.<class>` key resolves to changes, not what consumes it. `infra/serverless.yml`'s `auditAggregation` Lambda `environment:` binding for `aggregate_metadata` remains the **only** Lambda function binding this migration touches or requires — no new `environment:` block is added to any function, and no other function gains a custody-period binding. Missing or invalid stage properties in the external file continue to block Serverless variable resolution at package/deploy time, exactly as the prior inline-empty-mapping pattern did — this migration changes the value's source, not the fail-closed rendering gate itself.
- **CLI/runtime**: Phase 5/6/7's three write-capable commands resolve their class's value via `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader.resolve(evidence_class, stage)` (§20.4) reading `evidentiary_classes.<evidence_class>.<stage>` from the same file — never through a standalone environment variable.

Serverless and runtime consumers use different mechanisms (native `${file(...)}` variable resolution versus a Python JSON read) but must derive the same scalar from the same authoritative file and fixed schema, apply equivalent positive-integer validation, and provide no fallback or cross-class reuse. Missing or invalid stage/class values preserve fail-closed behavior on both sides: Serverless-side rendering/packaging/deployment fails at variable-resolution time (mechanism unchanged from A1.2/A1.3c.1, now sourced externally instead of inline); CLI-side resolution raises `CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING` before any AWS-client construction (§20.4).

### 20.4 Command-Scoped CLI Resolution and the Three Construction Modes

Per Invariant 30 and Invariant 31 (Decision 11). Custody-period configuration is resolved **only** by the three write-capable commands — `rcp generate intelligence`, `rcp generate report`, `rcp certify audit` — never added to `StageConfigLoader.REQUIRED_FIELDS` (which would incorrectly gate every other CLI command — retrieval, client/audit management, scheduling, config download/init — on a value none of them need).

**Correction to the prior revision**: `IntelligenceRepository`/`IntelligencePublisher`, `ReportRepository`/`ReportPublisher`, and `CertificationRepository`/`CertificationPublisher` are each constructed at two independent `operator_cli/main.py` dispatch branches — confirmed directly: `retrieve intelligence-*` (`main.py:226-230`) versus `generate intelligence` (`main.py:335-341`); `retrieve report-*` (`main.py:260-262`) versus the `generate report` construction block in `operator_cli/main.py` — currently the `ReportRepository` and `ReportPublisher` construction at lines 423-424; `retrieve cert-*` (`main.py:288-294`) versus `certify audit` (`main.py:487-493`). **Two construction modes apply universally to Phases 5–7: read-only retrieval and write-capable generation. Phase 5 additionally supports a write-free dry-run mode** — confirmed only for `rcp generate intelligence --dry-run` (§20.5); no dry-run flag or mode exists for `rcp generate report` or `rcp certify audit`, and this document does not imply otherwise. Concretely:

- **Phase 5 (Intelligence)**: retrieval, dry-run, and write-capable generation — three modes.
- **Phase 6 (Report)**: retrieval and write-capable generation — two modes.
- **Phase 7 (Certificate)**: retrieval and write-capable generation — two modes.

Each mode has different configuration-resolution and dependency obligations:

#### Read-only retrieval

- Must not resolve that phase's custody period.
- May construct the repository or publisher without `hold_repository` or `custody_period_days` (both `None`, their constructor default per Invariant 31) — this is exactly how current retrieval behavior already constructs these classes; nothing about retrieval's construction call changes.
- Must not perform a governed write.
- Must remain available while that phase's custody-period configuration is entirely absent from `config/custody_periods.json` — retrieval must never fail, and must never be made to depend on, that class's configured value.

#### Dry-run generation (Phase 5 only — Phase 6/7 have no dry-run mode)

- Must not resolve the `intelligence` custody period.
- May construct the AWS clients its existing read-only query (`list_phase4_aggregate_records`) requires — client construction itself is not gated by `--dry-run` today and this correction does not change that.
- Must not invoke `put_intelligence_metadata_once`, `update_intelligence_metadata`, or `IntelligencePublisher.write_artifact`.
- May use `IntelligenceRepository`/`IntelligencePublisher` instances constructed without `hold_repository`/`custody_period_days` — the identical governance-dependency-free construction retrieval uses (§20.5 gives this mode's full detail).
- Any future code change that caused `--dry-run` to reach a governed write must fail closed at the write method itself (Invariant 31's write-entry governance preflight below) rather than proceed ungoverned — the dry-run exemption is a property of the current, verified-write-free code path, never a construction-time bypass of the write guard.

#### Write-capable generation

The command-scoped order (unchanged from the previously locked flow):

1. Validate command, stage, and confirm non-dry-run mode (for Phase 5; Phase 6/7 have no dry-run branch to confirm against).
2. Resolve that phase's evidence class exactly once through `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader`.
3. Fail closed (`CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING`) before `AwsClientFactory` construction if resolution fails.
4. Construct the required AWS clients.
5. Construct exactly one `HoldRepository`.
6. Inject that same `HoldRepository` instance into the repository and publisher; inject the resolved `custody_period_days` integer into the repository only.
7. Perform governed writes.

Only this construction path may supply non-`None` values for `hold_repository`/`custody_period_days`.

**Locked contracts** (A1.3d.1 introduces exactly these, no alternative naming):

- Authoritative file: `config/custody_periods.json`.
- Loader module: `src/release_confidence_platform/config/custody_period_config.py`.
- Loader class: `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader`.
- Resolver method: `resolve(evidence_class: str, stage: str) -> int`.

`CustodyPeriodConfigLoader` is composed alongside `StageConfigLoader`, not merged into it — `StageConfigLoader.load()` and its `REQUIRED_FIELDS` are unchanged. `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader.resolve(evidence_class, stage)` reads `config/custody_periods.json` at `evidentiary_classes.<evidence_class>.<stage>` and validates the resolved value.

**Exception contract**: `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader` operates in the configuration layer, before any storage or AWS-client construction — it must raise `ConfigError("<sanitized message>", "CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING")`, never `StorageError` (a storage-layer exception this loader never reaches). The identical reason code applies to every one of the following conditions, with no distinguishing sub-code. This is distinct from, and does not replace, the repository-owned `StorageError("...", "CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING")` write-entry governance preflight defined below — the loader's `ConfigError` is raised at CLI-resolution time, before construction; the write-entry governance preflight is a repository/publisher's own defensive re-check of its already-injected state, raised only if a future wiring defect ever invokes a governed write method on an instance that was never given a valid dependency:

- `config/custody_periods.json` is missing.
- The file's contents are malformed JSON.
- The parsed root is not a JSON object.
- `evidentiary_classes` (or the requested class's nested object) is missing.
- The requested `evidence_class` is not a recognized key.
- The requested `stage` property is absent under that class.
- The resolved value is JSON `null`.
- The resolved value is a Boolean — **rejected explicitly**, even though Python's `bool` is a subclass of `int` and would otherwise pass a naive `isinstance(value, int)` check.
- The resolved value is a string.
- The resolved value is a float (including a float with an integral value, e.g. `30.0`).
- The resolved value is zero.
- The resolved value is negative.

Every condition above fails closed, before `AwsClientFactory` construction, with no fallback.

**Locked dependency-construction flow** — applies to the write-capable construction mode only (above); retrieval and (for Phase 5) dry-run never enter this flow's configuration-resolution or `HoldRepository`-construction steps. Resolution happens exactly once, entirely within `operator_cli/main.py`'s write-capable dispatch branch, strictly before any AWS-client construction; the resolved value is then injected as a typed dependency, never re-resolved:

```text
CLI argument/stage validation
→ resolve custody_period_days exactly once
→ construct AWS clients
→ construct one HoldRepository
→ inject typed dependencies into repository/publisher
→ construct engine
→ execute
```

**Exact resolution sequence**, each write-capable command:

1. Argument and stage validation (existing, unchanged).
2. **Custody-period resolution, exactly once**:
   ```python
   custody_period_days = CustodyPeriodConfigLoader().resolve(
       "<evidence_class>",
       args.stage,
   )
   ```
   Fails closed here (`CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING`), before anything below — including before `AwsClientFactory` construction.
3. `AwsClientFactory` construction, and the underlying `boto3` client(s) it produces.
4. One `HoldRepository` construction (backed by the low-level DynamoDB client).
5. Repository and publisher construction: the same `HoldRepository` instance is injected into both; the resolved `custody_period_days` integer is injected into the repository constructor only, as a keyword-only parameter (§20.6–§20.8).
6. Engine construction, receiving only the already-constructed repository and publisher — the engine itself never sees `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader`, a stage string, a configuration path, or a raw/unvalidated duration.
7. Engine execution: Job-record creation, then any Category 1 or Category 2 write, each hold-coordinated per §20.6–§20.8.

No environment-variable fallback exists at any step. No `CUSTODY_PERIOD_DAYS_INTELLIGENCE`/`_REPORT`/`_CERTIFICATE` environment-variable-name constant is introduced anywhere — Invariant 30 forecloses this mechanism entirely for these three CLI-only paths, so no placeholder constant for it may exist even unused.

**Repository/publisher constructor boundary** (applies to all three phases, §20.6–§20.8; corrected per ADR Decision 11 / Invariant 31 — `hold_repository` and `custody_period_days` are optional, defaulting to `None`, because the same class is constructed by both the read-only/write-free mode and the write-capable mode above):

```python
Repository(
    ...existing_dependencies,
    hold_repository: HoldRepository | None = None,
    *,
    custody_period_days: int | None = None,
)

Publisher(
    ...existing_dependencies,
    hold_repository: HoldRepository | None = None,
)
```

This mirrors `AggregationRepository.__init__`'s existing, unmodified `hold_repository: HoldRepository | None = None` parameter (`aggregation/repository.py:148-153`, A1.3c.1) — Decision 11 generalizes an already-proven shape, not a new pattern. `custody_period_days` is keyword-only on the repository, `None` by default; the final parameter ordering/naming may otherwise follow each class's existing positional conventions. The publisher receives `hold_repository` only — never the duration — because S3 Lifecycle expiration is owned by the infrastructure rule itself (§20.9), not by the publisher; the publisher's only custody-related responsibility is reading current hold state and computing `rcp-legal-hold`/`rcp-evidence-class` tags per call. Only the write-capable construction path (above) supplies non-`None` values for either parameter; the read-only and write-free construction paths leave both at their `None` default, unchanged from how those paths already construct these classes today.

**Repository boundary — must never**: import `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader`; call `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader.resolve` (or any equivalent); read stage configuration; read an environment variable for these three CLI-only classes; accept a custody duration through a caller-supplied item/update dictionary; or substitute a default, sentinel, or fallback value in place of a `None` `custody_period_days` or `hold_repository` — a `None` dependency must reach the write-entry governance preflight below unmodified, never be silently replaced. A repository **must** validate a non-`None` injected `custody_period_days` (not merely "may") — see the write-entry governance preflight's third check below; this validates an already-resolved dependency, it never re-resolves configuration or creates an alternative source.

**Write-entry governance preflight** (ADR Invariant 31) — mandatory on every governed write method regardless of how many construction paths a class has, and regardless of whether that method also has its own caller-input-validation step (§20.7's forbidden-field check is one such step; see the precedence rule below). The preflight verifies, in this order:

1. Required hold coordination is present (`hold_repository is not None`).
2. Required custody duration is present (`custody_period_days is not None`) — repositories only; publisher methods that consume no duration validate hold coordination only (check 1).
3. The custody duration is a positive, non-Boolean integer.

```python
if self._hold_repository is None:
    raise StorageError(
        "Hold coordination is not configured", "HOLD_COORDINATION_NOT_CONFIGURED"
    )
if self._custody_period_days is None:
    raise StorageError(
        "Custody period configuration was not provided to this repository instance",
        "CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING",
    )
if isinstance(self._custody_period_days, bool) or not isinstance(
    self._custody_period_days, int
) or self._custody_period_days <= 0:
    raise StorageError(
        "Custody period configuration is invalid", "CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING"
    )
```

Check 3 rejects a Boolean explicitly (checked before the `int` check, since `bool` is an `int` subclass), a string, a float, zero, a negative integer, and any other non-integer value — identical in substance to `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader`'s own value-classification rules above, re-applied here because this preflight is a repository-owned re-check of an already-injected value, not a call back into the loader. This mirrors `AggregationRepository.put_lineage_page_once`'s existing, unmodified guard (`aggregation/repository.py:222-225`) exactly, extended with the equivalent `custody_period_days` presence-and-validity checks. It applies to `put_intelligence_metadata_once`, `update_intelligence_metadata`, `IntelligencePublisher.write_artifact` (check 1 only — publishers never receive a duration), and their Phase 6/Phase 7 equivalents (§20.6–§20.8) identically. Under the locked write-capable dispatch flow (steps 1–7 above), no branch of this preflight fires in correct operation — every write-capable construction path supplies both dependencies, already valid, before any write method is reached. It exists as a fail-closed backstop against a future wiring or configuration defect, not as an expected runtime branch. All three raised codes are `StorageError`, not `ConfigError` — this preflight is the repository/publisher's own storage-layer precondition check, distinct from `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader.resolve`'s configuration-layer `ConfigError` above; both layers share the identical `CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING` reason-code string for a missing-or-invalid duration, so operator-facing behavior for that code is identical regardless of which layer detects the failure.

**Preflight precedence rule**: every governed write must complete a deterministic entry preflight before any hold-state read, transaction construction, S3 publication, or other AWS mutation. Pure validation of caller-supplied input may precede governance-dependency validation, but neither step may perform an AWS call. Locked order:

1. Reject caller-supplied forbidden governance fields, where such a restriction exists for that write method (§20.7's `regenerate_report_metadata` forbidden-field check is the only current example).
2. Reject missing hold coordination (`HOLD_COORDINATION_NOT_CONFIGURED`).
3. Reject missing or invalid custody duration (`CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING`).
4. Only then read hold state, or construct/submit an AWS write.

Where no caller-field restriction exists for a given write method (`put_intelligence_metadata_once`, `update_intelligence_metadata`, every `write_artifact`, `write_cert_metadata_complete`), the write-entry governance preflight (steps 2–3 above) is that method's first executable action.

### 20.5 Intelligence Dry-Run Exemption

`rcp generate intelligence --dry-run` (`IntelligenceEngine.generate(..., dry_run=True)`, confirmed at `engine.py:252-262` to return via `_dry_run_pipeline` before any of steps 4 onward — no `IntelligenceJob`, `IntelligenceMetadata`, or S3 write occurs) is the dry-run construction mode defined in §20.4: exempt from custody-period resolution because there is no governed write to compute custody fields for, and free to construct `IntelligenceRepository`/`IntelligencePublisher` with `hold_repository`/`custody_period_days` left at their `None` default (Invariant 31) — identical to how the retrieval mode already constructs these classes. **This exemption is conditional, not permanent**: it holds only while `--dry-run` remains provably write-free. If a future change causes `--dry-run` to persist any governed record or artifact, custody-period resolution (§20.4) becomes required for that path immediately, and the exemption ceases to apply without requiring a further architecture amendment — the underlying rule (§20.4's sequence) already covers it; only the current code's write-free dry-run path is what creates the exemption. Independently of this exemption, the write-entry governance preflight (§20.4) means that if a future defect caused the dry-run path to reach `put_intelligence_metadata_once`, `update_intelligence_metadata`, or `write_artifact` while still constructed without governance dependencies, the write method itself would fail closed with `HOLD_COORDINATION_NOT_CONFIGURED`/`CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING` rather than silently performing an ungoverned write.

### 20.6 Phase 5 (Intelligence) — CREATE and Regeneration Contract

`IntelligenceMetadata` is Category 2 (evidence-derived artifact, pointing at the Category 1 `intelligence/` S3 artifact) per existing Decision 8/TD §18.1 classification — unchanged. `IntelligenceJob` is Category 3 (§20.2) — excluded.

- **CREATE** (`existing is None`, `engine.py:297-312`): `put_intelligence_metadata_once` becomes a hold-coordinated `TransactWriteItems` call — the existing conditional `Put` (`attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK)`, unchanged) plus the appended `LegalHold.hold_version` `ConditionCheck`, following A1.LH1's already-proven, unmodified `HoldCoordinatedTransactionRunner` pattern.
- **Regeneration** (`existing is not None`, `engine.py:313-329`): `update_intelligence_metadata`'s existing unconditioned full-item `put_item` becomes a hold-coordinated `TransactWriteItems` call — the `Put` itself remains unconditioned (preserving the existing "entire item is replaced" force-regeneration semantics; no `attribute_not_exists`/expected-state condition is added), with the appended `ConditionCheck` as the only condition in the transaction. Directly analogous to Phase 4's `put_lineage_page_once`-style "governed `Put` + appended `ConditionCheck`" shape, adapted here to an unconditioned `Put`.
- **Construction**: `IntelligenceRepository`'s constructor accepts optional `hold_repository: HoldRepository | None = None` and keyword-only `custody_period_days: int | None = None` (§20.4/Invariant 31) — both `None` when constructed by the retrieval or dry-run mode, both required non-`None` when constructed by the write-capable `generate intelligence` mode. `put_intelligence_metadata_once` and `update_intelligence_metadata` each perform the write-entry governance preflight (§20.4) as their first action, before the conditional/unconditioned `Put` logic described below.
- **Custody fields**, both cases: `custody_period_days` is resolved once by the command-scoped CLI boundary before AWS-client construction (§20.4) and injected into `IntelligenceRepository`'s constructor. The repository never invokes `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader` and has no stage or configuration dependency. Within each `HoldCoordinatedTransactionRunner` attempt, it computes fresh `custody_expires_at`/`ttl_disposal_at`/`evidence_class = "intelligence"` from the injected duration, that attempt's clock, and that attempt's fresh hold state — never copied or chained from a prior generation, matching every other Category 2 regeneration rule already established (§18.4/§19.9). Both CREATE and regeneration write the **entire item** via `Put` (never a partial `UpdateExpression`); when the fresh hold-state read observes an active hold, `ttl_disposal_at` is simply omitted from the new item being written — since the whole item is replaced, omission alone is sufficient and correct, unlike Phase 6's partial-update contract (§20.7), which requires an explicit `REMOVE` because it does not replace the whole item.
- **S3 leg — ownership boundary**: `IntelligencePublisher.write_artifact` (`publisher.py:27-53`) owns exactly: a `HoldRepository`-backed, `ConsistentRead: true` hold-state read immediately before `put_object` (mirroring A1.LH3's raw-evidence pattern exactly); a fixed `rcp-evidence-class=intelligence` tag; a freshly computed `rcp-legal-hold=true|false` tag based on that read; and fail-closed behavior (the write-entry governance preflight, §20.4) when `hold_repository` is absent or hold state cannot be determined. It does **not** own, and must not reimplement, canary-marker establishment, PLACE/RELEASE reconciliation, marker disposal, or custody sweeping — those remain exclusively `RetentionService`/`CustodySweepClient`'s responsibility (§20.9), already generically covering the `intelligence/` S3 prefix. The object write itself, and its existing "write S3 before updating COMPLETE" ordering (`engine.py:487-489`), are otherwise unchanged.
- `IntelligenceJob` remains entirely outside this contract (§20.2).

### 20.7 Phase 6 (Report) — CREATE, Regeneration, and Ordinary-Transition Contract (Corrected — Explicit CREATE Boundary, Phase 7 Consumer-Contract Preservation)

**Correction to the prior revision of this section**, made ahead of A1.3d.3 implementation and verified directly against current repository code (`deterministic_reporting/repository.py`, `publisher.py`, `engine.py`, `identity.py`; `audit_platform_integrity/repository.py`, `engine.py`, `domains.py`): the prior revision (a) cited a stale construction-site line range for the write-capable `generate report` command, corrected below and throughout this section; (b) described only `regenerate_report_metadata`'s regeneration contract and folded ReportMetadata CREATE into generic "every governed write" language instead of giving `put_report_metadata_once` its own named contract, mirroring the explicit treatment §20.6 already gives Phase 5's CREATE; and (c) was silent on Phase 7's status as an existing, already-implemented, read-only consumer of Phase 6 outputs — a prior read-only investigation's "no Phase 7 coupling" conclusion is rejected by Product Strategy and corrected in §20.7.7 below. This section defines three distinct Phase 6 persistence responsibilities — CREATE (§20.7.1), regeneration (§20.7.2), and ordinary status/field transitions (§20.7.3) — classifies every current `engine.py` call site into one of them (§20.7.4), and adds the CREATE acceptance-coverage requirement (§20.7.5), the canonical report S3 key contract (§20.7.6), the Phase 7 consumer-contract preservation requirement (§20.7.7), the seven retrieval variants (§20.7.8), the Phase 6 error matrix (§20.7.9), the self-contained production/test file inventory (§20.7.10), and the preserved issuance-governance scope boundary (§20.7.11). This correction is documentation-only: it authorizes A1.3d.3 documentation, not A1.3d.3 implementation, A1.3d.4, or A2 work.

#### 20.7.1 ReportMetadata CREATE Contract (`put_report_metadata_once`)

Per ADR Decision 11/Invariant 31 and the already-locked, generically applicable Non-Negotiable Invariants 9, 11, 12, 14, 17, and 18 — this is an application of existing invariants to a Phase 6-specific write method, not a new invariant. `put_report_metadata_once` (`deterministic_reporting/repository.py:212-221`, currently a plain first-write-wins conditional `put_item` with no hold coordination) is the Category 2 CREATE write for `ReportMetadata` and must become a hold-coordinated `TransactWriteItems` call, structurally identical to the already-merged (A1.3d.2) `IntelligenceRepository.put_intelligence_metadata_once` (`reliability_intelligence/repository.py:340-399`), with `evidence_class` fixed to `"report"`. A1.3d.3 must implement it so that, in this order, it:

1. Performs the write-entry governance preflight (§20.4/Invariant 31) as its first action — `HOLD_COORDINATION_NOT_CONFIGURED` (`StorageError`) if `self._hold_repository is None`; `CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING` (`StorageError`) if `self._custody_period_days` is `None`, a Boolean, a non-integer, zero, or negative — before any hold-state read, transaction-item construction, or AWS mutation.
2. Preserves the existing SK-namespace guard, `_assert_phase6_sk(item.get("SK", ""))` (`repository.py:34-47`), unchanged.
3. Preserves the record's existing first-write-wins behavior and its existing `ConditionExpression`, `attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK)` (`repository.py:253`), unchanged, as the governed `Put`'s own condition inside the transaction — the "governed record's own condition," distinct from and additional to the appended hold `ConditionCheck` (item 8 below).
4. Derives `(client_id, audit_id)` structurally from the item's own `PK`/`SK` — never from a caller-supplied string parameter — via a dedicated helper mirroring `_parse_phase5_metadata_identity` (`reliability_intelligence/repository.py:82-99`) exactly: `PK` must start with `CLIENT#`, `SK` must start with `AUDIT#`, and `audit_id` is the SK segment up to (not including) the next `#`; a mismatch on either raises `StorageError("...", "STORAGE_ERROR")` before any hold-state read. `ReportMetadata`'s SK shape (`AUDIT#{audit_id}#EXEC#{...}#CFG#{...}#AGG#{...}#INTEL#{...}#RPT#{...}#META`, `repository.py:91-95`) has the identical `AUDIT#{audit_id}#...` prefix Phase 5's parser already handles, so this is a direct mirror, not a new parsing shape.
5. Creates a non-mutating shallow copy of the caller's item — `base_item = dict(item)` — mirroring `reliability_intelligence/repository.py:365`'s comment exactly: **`sanitize()` must never be called on a full persistence-bound item**, because `sanitization/sanitizer.py`'s `STRUCTURAL_IDENTIFIER_KEYS` (`sanitizer.py:33-45`) does not include `PK`/`SK`, so sanitizing the full item risks corrupting the composite key via the same phone/email regex false-positive failure mode already locked for Phase 5. The caller's original `item` object must never be mutated.
6. Within each `HoldCoordinatedTransactionRunner` attempt (`evidence_retention/hold_coordination.py`), computes fresh governance fields from `base_item`, that attempt's own hold-state read, and the injected `custody_period_days` — never copied or chained from a prior attempt or a prior generation: `custody_expires_at = now + custody_period_days * 86400` (fresh clock every attempt); `evidence_class = "report"` (fixed); `ttl_disposal_at` computed via `compute_ttl_disposal_at(hold_state, custody_expires_at)` (`hold_coordination.py:107-118`) — present in the item being written when the fresh read observes no active hold, **omitted** when it observes an active hold. Omission alone is correct here (unlike §20.7.2's regeneration contract) because CREATE writes the entire item via `Put`, never a partial update.
7. Appends the `LegalHold.hold_version` `ConditionCheck` transact-item, built via `build_hold_version_condition_check_item` (`hold_coordination.py:74-96`), as the transaction's final item — the identical mechanism used by every other governed write, no bespoke variant.
8. Executes via the unmodified `HoldCoordinatedTransactionRunner.run(...)` (`hold_coordination.py:161-224`), preserving its existing dual-failure precedence: a governed-condition failure (the record's own `attribute_not_exists` collision) is surfaced immediately via `on_governed_condition_failed`, regardless of whether the hold `ConditionCheck` also failed in the same attempt, and is never retried or masked as a hold-version race; only a hold-`ConditionCheck`-only failure triggers the bounded retry (`MAX_HOLD_COORDINATION_RETRY_ATTEMPTS = 3`, `evidence_retention/constants.py:183`), whose exhaustion raises `HoldStateConcurrencyExceededError` (`HOLD_STATE_CONCURRENCY_EXCEEDED`).
9. Maps the governed CREATE collision to the existing `ConditionalWriteError` (`repository.py:50-52`, code `CONDITIONAL_WRITE_FAILED`) via `on_governed_condition_failed`, exactly as `IntelligenceRepository.put_intelligence_metadata_once` already does (`reliability_intelligence/repository.py:389-399`) — unchanged from the pre-A1.3d.3 collision-handling contract `ReportingEngine.generate`'s idempotency check (`engine.py:149-157`) already relies on.

#### 20.7.2 ReportMetadata Regeneration Contract (`regenerate_report_metadata`)

Regeneration must not be inferred from arbitrary `update_report_metadata_fields` dictionary contents (the current code's mechanism, `engine.py:289-297`, where the same method is reused for regen-PENDING and every later ordinary status transition). A1.3d.3 introduces a **dedicated, explicitly-typed regeneration operation**, `ReportRepository.regenerate_report_metadata(key, updates, *, client_id, audit_id)` — confirmed by direct grep to not exist anywhere in current code — used only at the regen-detection branch point, distinct from `update_report_metadata_fields`, which remains used for ordinary status transitions (PENDING→IN_PROGRESS, →COMPLETE, →FAILED) exactly as today (§20.7.3).

`custody_expires_at`, `ttl_disposal_at`, and `evidence_class` are **repository-owned governance fields** on this operation. `regenerate_report_metadata` must **reject** — never silently override — any attempt by the caller-supplied `updates` dict to set these three keys, mirroring the existing internal-programming-contract guard `AggregationRepository.update_job` already establishes for `AggregationJob`'s own Category 3 boundary (`aggregation/repository.py`'s `_RETENTION_GOVERNED_FIELD_NAMES` set, checked via `forbidden = _RETENTION_GOVERNED_FIELD_NAMES & updates.keys()`, raising `AssertionError` before any further work if non-empty):

```python
_REPORT_REGENERATION_GOVERNED_FIELD_NAMES = frozenset(
    {"custody_expires_at", "ttl_disposal_at", "evidence_class"}
)

def regenerate_report_metadata(self, key, updates, *, client_id, audit_id):
    forbidden = _REPORT_REGENERATION_GOVERNED_FIELD_NAMES & updates.keys()
    if forbidden:
        raise AssertionError(
            f"regenerate_report_metadata must never accept caller-supplied "
            f"governance fields {sorted(forbidden)!r}; custody_expires_at, "
            f"ttl_disposal_at, and evidence_class are computed internally, "
            f"never caller-controlled."
        )
    # ... hold-coordinated TransactWriteItems construction follows
```

This forbidden-field check is the **first** action `regenerate_report_metadata` performs, followed immediately by the write-entry governance preflight (§20.4/Invariant 31) — both occur before any hold-state read, before any transaction construction, and before any AWS request of any kind. `ReportRepository`'s constructor accepts optional `hold_repository: HoldRepository | None = None` and keyword-only `custody_period_days: int | None = None` (§20.4/Invariant 31), because `ReportRepository` is, like `IntelligenceRepository`, constructed at both a read-only retrieval dispatch branch (`retrieve report-*`, `main.py:260-262`) and the write-capable `generate report` construction block in `operator_cli/main.py` — currently the `ReportRepository` and `ReportPublisher` construction at lines 423-424 — confirmed by the same A1.3d.2 investigation that identified this pattern for Phase 5. Both dependencies are `None` when constructed by the retrieval branch, both required non-`None` when constructed by the write-capable branch. `custody_period_days` is resolved once by the command-scoped CLI boundary before AWS-client construction (§20.4) and injected into `ReportRepository`'s constructor. The repository never invokes `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader` and has no stage or configuration dependency. Within each `HoldCoordinatedTransactionRunner` attempt, it computes fresh `custody_expires_at`/`ttl_disposal_at`/`evidence_class` from the injected duration, that attempt's clock, and that attempt's fresh hold state — never accepted as, or merged from, caller input. `ReportPublisher`'s constructor accepts optional `hold_repository: HoldRepository | None = None` for the identical reason (`retrieve report-*` also constructs `ReportPublisher`, `main.py:262`); `write_artifact` performs the write-entry governance preflight for `hold_repository` before its hold-state read. This paragraph records the same construction-mode finding Phase 5 already made — A1.3d.3 (Phase 6's own separately authorized implementation subphase) is responsible for verifying this against Phase 6's code at implementation time and is not itself authorized by this correction.

The dedicated regeneration operation's hold-coordinated `TransactWriteItems` call must, explicitly:

- `SET` a freshly-computed, repository-owned `custody_expires_at`.
- `SET` a fixed, repository-owned `evidence_class = "report"`.
- `SET ttl_disposal_at` to the newly-computed custody timestamp **when the fresh hold-state read observes no active hold**.
- **`REMOVE ttl_disposal_at`** (an explicit `UpdateExpression REMOVE`, not merely omitting it from the `SET` clause) **when the fresh hold-state read observes an active hold** — omission alone is insufficient and incorrect, since it would silently preserve whatever stale `ttl_disposal_at` value the prior generation left in place, exactly the failure mode Decision 3/Invariant 9 exist to prevent.
- Append the `LegalHold.hold_version` `ConditionCheck` as the transaction's final item.
- Preserve the existing partial-update semantics for every other, non-governance field in the caller-supplied `updates` dict (`report_job_id`, `status`, `generation_count`, `updated_at`) — unaffected by this contract.
- Use A1.LH1 coordination (`HoldCoordinatedTransactionRunner`, unmodified) — the identical mechanism §20.7.1's CREATE contract uses.
- Preserve report identity and every field required by the Phase 7 consumer contract (§20.7.7) — `report_id`, `report_version`, `intelligence_version`, `aggregate_set_hash`, `endpoint_count`, `s3_artifact_ref` — none of which this contract touches or is permitted to rename.

`ReportJob` remains entirely outside this contract (§20.2). `ReportPublisher.write_artifact` gains the identical write-time tagging pattern described for Phase 5 in §20.6, with `evidence_class=report` (full contract: §20.7.6).

#### 20.7.3 Ordinary Status/Field Transitions (`update_report_metadata_fields`)

`update_report_metadata_fields` (`repository.py:223-237`) remains the exclusive owner of every ordinary `ReportMetadata` status/field transition — PENDING→IN_PROGRESS (`engine.py:319`), →FAILED (`engine.py:390`), →COMPLETE (`engine.py:435`) — and must:

- Remain a plain, unconditioned `UpdateExpression="SET ..."` call (`repository.py:260-270`), with **no** `ConditionExpression` and **no** hold-version `ConditionCheck` — these transitions never compute or touch a custody field, so they fall outside Category 1/2 hold coordination entirely (Decision 8/§18.1: they are ordinary field updates on an already-governed record, not themselves a governed write).
- Preserve whatever `custody_expires_at`, `ttl_disposal_at`, and `evidence_class` values CREATE or the most recent regeneration already set — never recompute, restore, or otherwise touch any of the three.
- Remain structurally distinct from `regenerate_report_metadata` (§20.7.2) — the current code's conflation of regen-PENDING (`engine.py:289-297`) into this method is exactly the defect §20.7.2's dedicated operation exists to correct; A1.3d.3 must move the regen-PENDING call site to `regenerate_report_metadata` and leave every other call site on `update_report_metadata_fields` unchanged.
- Preserve current IN_PROGRESS/FAILED/COMPLETE behavior exactly — same fields, same unconditioned semantics, same lack of hold coordination.

**Enforceable forbidden-field guard.** "Never accept a caller-supplied governance value" is not self-enforcing merely because current call sites happen not to supply one — A1.3d.3 must make `update_report_metadata_fields` reject such input programmatically, as its **first executable action**, before `_assert_phase6_sk`, before any `UpdateExpression`/`ExpressionAttributeNames`/`ExpressionAttributeValues` construction, and before any AWS call:

```python
_FORBIDDEN_REPORT_METADATA_UPDATE_FIELDS = frozenset({
    "custody_expires_at",
    "ttl_disposal_at",
    "evidence_class",
})

def update_report_metadata_fields(self, key, updates):
    forbidden = _FORBIDDEN_REPORT_METADATA_UPDATE_FIELDS.intersection(updates)
    if forbidden:
        raise AssertionError(
            "ReportMetadata governance fields are repository-owned"
        )
    # ... existing _assert_phase6_sk + self._update_item(key, updates) follows, unchanged
```

An equivalent implementation is acceptable only if it preserves every one of: exception type `AssertionError`; a deterministic, sanitized (no field value echoed) message; the check as the method's first executable action; zero SK validation or AWS work performed after rejection; and the caller's `updates` dict left unmutated whether the call rejects or proceeds. This is **repository-ownership enforcement, not legal-hold coordination** — `update_report_metadata_fields` still never resolves custody configuration, never reads hold state, and never performs A1.LH1 coordination; it only refuses to let a caller inject a value into the three fields CREATE/regeneration exclusively own.

**Required negative tests** (ownership: `tests/unit/deterministic_reporting/test_repository.py`, alongside `update_report_metadata_fields`'s existing coverage):

- `test_update_report_metadata_fields_rejects_custody_expires_at` — single forbidden field, `custody_expires_at`, raises `AssertionError`.
- `test_update_report_metadata_fields_rejects_ttl_disposal_at` — single forbidden field, `ttl_disposal_at`.
- `test_update_report_metadata_fields_rejects_evidence_class` — single forbidden field, `evidence_class`.
- `test_update_report_metadata_fields_rejects_all_forbidden_fields_together` — all three present simultaneously, still a single `AssertionError`.
- `test_update_report_metadata_fields_rejects_forbidden_field_mixed_with_ordinary_fields` — a forbidden field present alongside permitted fields (e.g. `status`, `updated_at`) still raises, and no permitted field is applied.
- `test_update_report_metadata_fields_forbidden_field_exception_type_and_message` — asserts the exact exception type `AssertionError` and the exact deterministic message text, with no forbidden field name or value echoed into the message.
- `test_update_report_metadata_fields_forbidden_field_rejection_performs_zero_dynamodb_activity` — a spy/mock DynamoDB client records zero calls (`update_item` or otherwise) when rejection occurs.
- `test_update_report_metadata_fields_forbidden_field_rejection_preserves_caller_dict` — the caller's `updates` dict is unchanged (identity and contents) after a rejected call.
- `test_update_report_metadata_fields_permitted_fields_unaffected` — a call using only permitted ordinary fields (`status`, `report_job_id`, `generation_count`, `updated_at`, `failure_stage`, `failure_reason_code`, `completed_at`, `report_id`, `composite_score`, `score_label`, `endpoint_count`, `s3_artifact_ref`, `aggregate_set_hash`) succeeds exactly as it does today, proving the guard does not regress the existing IN_PROGRESS/FAILED/COMPLETE call sites (`engine.py:319,390,435`).

#### 20.7.4 Call-Site Classification — `engine.py` (Re-Verified)

Every current `ReportRepository` write call site in `deterministic_reporting/engine.py`, re-verified directly against the file:

| Line(s) | Call | Record | Category |
|---|---|---|---|
| 264 | `self.repository.put_report_job_once(job_item)` | `ReportJob` CREATE | Category 3 — excluded (§20.2) |
| 286 | `self.repository.put_report_metadata_once(meta_item)` | `ReportMetadata` CREATE | **CREATE** (§20.7.1) |
| 289–297 | `self.repository.update_report_metadata_fields(meta_key, {...})` (the `else:` branch, `existing is not None`) | `ReportMetadata` regeneration (regen-PENDING) | **Regeneration** — must migrate to `regenerate_report_metadata` (§20.7.2) |
| 318 | `self.repository.update_report_job(job_key, in_progress_updates)` | `ReportJob` IN_PROGRESS | Category 3 — excluded (§20.2) |
| 319 | `self.repository.update_report_metadata_fields(meta_key, in_progress_updates)` | `ReportMetadata` IN_PROGRESS | **Ordinary transition** (§20.7.3) |
| 389 | `self.repository.update_report_job(job_key, fail_updates)` | `ReportJob` FAILED | Category 3 — excluded (§20.2) |
| 390 | `self.repository.update_report_metadata_fields(meta_key, fail_updates)` | `ReportMetadata` FAILED | **Ordinary transition** (§20.7.3) |
| 434 | `self.repository.update_report_job(job_key, job_complete_updates)` | `ReportJob` COMPLETE | Category 3 — excluded (§20.2) |
| 435 | `self.repository.update_report_metadata_fields(meta_key, meta_complete_updates)` | `ReportMetadata` COMPLETE | **Ordinary transition** (§20.7.3) |

The only call site A1.3d.3 relocates is line 289 (regen-PENDING), from `update_report_metadata_fields` to `regenerate_report_metadata`. Every other call site's method and semantics are unchanged.

#### 20.7.5 Required CREATE Acceptance Coverage (Future A1.3d.3 Test Requirements)

Documentation of required test coverage — not actual test files, and not itself an authorization of A1.3d.3 implementation. A1.3d.3's own test plan must include, at minimum, for `put_report_metadata_once`:

- Unheld CREATE succeeds and sets `ttl_disposal_at = custody_expires_at`.
- Active-hold CREATE succeeds and omits `ttl_disposal_at`.
- Released-hold CREATE succeeds and sets `ttl_disposal_at = custody_expires_at`.
- A `PLACE` race (hold placed between the read and commit of a CREATE attempt) is detected by the hold `ConditionCheck` and triggers a retry with a fresh read.
- A `RELEASE` race (hold released between the read and commit) is likewise detected and triggers a retry with a fresh read.
- Bounded retry exhaustion raises `HoldStateConcurrencyExceededError` (`HOLD_STATE_CONCURRENCY_EXCEEDED`) after `MAX_HOLD_COORDINATION_RETRY_ATTEMPTS` attempts, with zero fallback to an unconditioned write.
- A CREATE collision (the record's own `attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK)` condition fails) raises `ConditionalWriteError` (`CONDITIONAL_WRITE_FAILED`).
- A CREATE collision and a hold-version race occurring in the same attempt still surfaces `ConditionalWriteError`, never `HoldStateConcurrencyExceededError` — proving the governed-condition-wins precedence (§20.7.1 item 8) holds for Phase 6, not only Phase 5.
- Conditional-error precedence is proven directly: a test forcing both the governed `Put` condition and the hold `ConditionCheck` to fail in the same `TransactionCanceledException` and asserting only `ConditionalWriteError` is raised.
- Fresh time, fresh custody fields, a fresh TTL omit/include decision, a fresh item, and fresh transaction actions are proven on each retry attempt (not cached or reused from a prior attempt) — e.g., by asserting the clock-derived `custody_expires_at` differs between two forced retry attempts.
- Caller-item immutability: the `item` dict passed by the caller is unchanged (identity and contents) after the call, whether the call succeeds, retries, or raises.
- Repository-owned governance-field override: a caller-supplied `custody_expires_at`/`ttl_disposal_at`/`evidence_class` present in the input `item` is silently replaced by the repository-computed value — CREATE's contract does not reject caller-supplied governance fields the way regeneration's forbidden-field check does (§20.7.2), because CREATE's `base_item = dict(item)` merge order (`{**base_item, **governance_fields}`, mirroring `reliability_intelligence/repository.py:371-374`) always applies governance fields last.
- Sanitizer-sensitive identifier preservation: a `PK`/`SK`/`client_id`/`audit_id` value that would trigger the phone/email regex false positive is proven to survive a CREATE write unmodified, proving `sanitize()` is never called on the full item.
- Malformed identity: an item whose `PK`/`SK` do not match the expected `CLIENT#{client_id}`/`AUDIT#{audit_id}#...` shape raises `StorageError("...", "STORAGE_ERROR")` before any hold-state read (zero calls to `HoldRepository.get_legal_hold`).
- Missing hold dependency: a `ReportRepository` constructed with `hold_repository=None` raises `HOLD_COORDINATION_NOT_CONFIGURED` on `put_report_metadata_once`, with zero `TransactWriteItems` construction and zero AWS calls.
- Missing/invalid duration: a `ReportRepository` constructed with `custody_period_days=None`, `True`/`False`, a string, a float, zero, or a negative integer each raises `CUSTODY_PERIOD_CONFIG_MISSING` on `put_report_metadata_once`, with zero AWS calls.
- DynamoDB item/transaction validation failure (e.g., a `ParamValidationError` from a malformed transact-item shape) is mapped through the existing `storage_error_from_dynamodb_request_error`/`storage_error_from_dynamodb_client_error` path, preserving the existing `STORAGE_ERROR`/`STORAGE_CONFIG_ERROR` classification — no new reason code is introduced for this case.

#### 20.7.6 Canonical Report S3 Key Contract and `ReportPublisher` Boundary

**Key structure.** `identity.build_s3_key` (`deterministic_reporting/identity.py:23-47`) produces a canonical **nine-segment** key — one segment longer than Phase 5's eight-segment `intelligence/` key (`reliability_intelligence/identity.py:15-33`):

```text
reports/{client_id}/{audit_id}/{audit_execution_id}/{aggregation_version}
  /{intelligence_version}/{report_version}/{report_job_id}/artifact.json
```

Split on `/`, this is exactly 9 parts: `parts[0] == "reports"`, `parts[1] == client_id`, `parts[2] == audit_id`, `parts[3] == audit_execution_id`, `parts[4] == aggregation_version`, `parts[5] == intelligence_version`, `parts[6] == report_version`, `parts[7] == report_job_id`, `parts[8] == "artifact.json"`. A future key parser (needed by `ReportPublisher.write_artifact`'s hold-state read, exactly as Phase 5's `_parse_intelligence_key_identity` is needed by `IntelligencePublisher.write_artifact`, `reliability_intelligence/publisher.py:41-63`) **cannot be a straight copy of Phase 5's 8-segment parser** — it must independently:

- Validate `len(parts) == 9` (not 8).
- Validate the fixed `parts[0] == "reports"` prefix.
- Validate the fixed `parts[8] == "artifact.json"` filename.
- Extract `client_id = parts[1]`, `audit_id = parts[2]` as trusted, structurally-derived identity — never accepted as separate caller-supplied parameters to the parser.
- Reject a malformed or ambiguous key (wrong segment count, wrong prefix, wrong filename, or an empty `client_id`/`audit_id` segment) **before** any hold-state read or S3 call, mirroring `_parse_intelligence_key_identity`'s own ordering (`publisher.py:47-63`) exactly.
- On rejection, raise `StorageError` with the already-locked, reused `STORAGE_ERROR` code and a fixed, static message template — never echoing the caller-supplied key value, the client/audit identifiers, or any other identifier, mirroring `_parse_intelligence_key_identity`'s existing message text exactly (a fixed template describing the expected shape, not the actual failing value).

**`ReportPublisher.write_artifact` boundary.** Currently (`publisher.py:27-51`) a plain `put_object` with only `ContentType` — no hold read, no tagging. A1.3d.3 must bring it to parity with `IntelligencePublisher.write_artifact` (`reliability_intelligence/publisher.py:90-140`), owning exactly:

- The mandatory hold-dependency preflight (check 1 of §20.4's write-entry governance preflight only — publishers never receive a duration): `HOLD_COORDINATION_NOT_CONFIGURED` if `self._hold_repository is None`, raised before any hold-state read or S3 call.
- A strongly consistent (`ConsistentRead: true`) hold-state read immediately before publication — `self._hold_repository.get_legal_hold(client_id, audit_id, consistent_read=True)` — per Invariant 17.
- A fixed `rcp-evidence-class=report` object tag (`EVIDENCE_CLASS_TAG_KEY` from `evidence_retention/constants.py`, value `"report"`).
- A freshly computed `rcp-legal-hold=true|false` object tag from that read (`LEGAL_HOLD_TAG_KEY`/`LEGAL_HOLD_TAG_VALUE_TRUE`/`LEGAL_HOLD_TAG_VALUE_FALSE`).
- Preservation of hold-error identity: an `StorageError` already raised by the hold-state read (e.g. a propagated `HOLD_COORDINATION_NOT_CONFIGURED`) is re-raised unchanged, never re-wrapped; only a genuine, non-`StorageError` failure during the hold read is wrapped as a fresh `StorageError("...", "STORAGE_ERROR")`.
- Genuine S3-error mapping: any `put_object` failure is wrapped as `StorageError("...", "S3_WRITE_FAILED")` — the existing, already-used code, unchanged.

It must **not** implement: canary-marker establishment; `PLACE`/`RELEASE` reconciliation; sweeping; or marker disposal. Those remain exclusively `RetentionService`/`CustodySweepClient`'s responsibility. `S3_EVIDENCE_CLASS_PREFIXES` (`evidence_retention/constants.py:119-124`) already includes `"reports"`, and both `CustodySweepClient`'s sweep and `reconcile_versions()` (A1.LH2) already iterate over it generically (§20.9) — **existing A1.LH2 reconciliation remains authoritative for the `reports` prefix; no reconciliation-prefix change is required by A1.3d.3.**

#### 20.7.7 Phase 7 Consumer-Contract Preservation (Existing Read-Only Consumer)

**Correction, superseding the prior investigation's rejected conclusion**: Phase 7 (`audit_platform_integrity/`) is not a hypothetical future integration — it is an **existing, already-implemented, read-only consumer of `ReportMetadata` and the Phase 6 S3 report artifact**, confirmed directly against current code. A1.3d.3 must preserve this contract exactly; this is behavior preservation, not new integration work.

**The exact consumed boundary:**

- **`ReportMetadata.status == COMPLETE` prerequisite behavior.** `CertificationEngine.certify` step 2 (`audit_platform_integrity/engine.py:223-255`) calls `self.repository.get_report_metadata(...)` (`engine.py:227-235`, implemented at `audit_platform_integrity/repository.py:89-113` — an independent, Phase-7-owned read-only copy of the identical key-construction logic `ReportRepository.get_report_metadata` uses) and raises `CertificationGateError` (`engine.py:253-255`) when the record is absent or `status != "COMPLETE"` (`engine.py:237`). No `CertificationJob` is created if this gate fails.
- **`s3_artifact_ref`.** Read via `self.repository.read_report_artifact(report_metadata["s3_artifact_ref"])` (`engine.py:349`, implemented at `audit_platform_integrity/repository.py:114-136`) — a direct S3 `GetObject` on the exact key stored in `ReportMetadata.s3_artifact_ref`; the method's own docstring states "Phase 7 must use this key directly and never construct it independently" (`repository.py:118-119`).
