## 22. Disposal-Recorder Identity, Event, Recovery, and IAM Architecture Correction (A1.4a.0 Amendment)

**Status: documentation-only correction.** This section authorizes no code, test, or infrastructure change. It corrects the not-yet-implemented `evidenceDisposalRecorder`'s target design — described across §5.1 (platform-position diagram), §6 (component table), §10.2/§10.4 (validation flow and persistence-flow sequences), §12 (Security), and §13 (Reliability) — to the rigor Product Strategy's disposition requires before A1.4a implementation may be authorized. Companion ADR: Decision 13, Non-Negotiable Invariants 38–51.

**Revision history:** Round 1 of this section (Product Strategy disposition: "Approved with Concerns — High Risk") is preserved below with its Round 2 corrections applied in place, per the twelve required corrections in Product Strategy's Round 2 disposition ("REJECT — High Risk"). Round 2 does not reopen the underlying architecture (event-driven disposal recording via DynamoDB Streams and S3 EventBridge notifications, ADR Decision 4); it corrects rigor and factual precision within that architecture. **Round 3 applies twelve further targeted corrections to Round 2's text, per Product Strategy's "RETURN FOR CORRECTION — High Risk" disposition on Round 2:** the recovery-bucket IAM grant (`s3:ListBucket` added, `s3:ResourceAccount` condition added — **field name corrected to `s3:ResourceAccount` throughout by Round 4, item 5; Round 3 originally, and incorrectly, used `aws:ResourceAccount`** — §22.11, item 1); failure-plane observability (named CloudWatch metrics replace "no native metric known" claims for every plane, §22.8/§22.9.4, item 2); the S3 EventBridge schema-version field name (`detail["event-version"]`, not `detail.version`) and its semver-style MAJOR/MINOR validation, plus rule-level `account` matching (§22.5/§22.5.2/§22.5.3, item 3); Lifecycle provenance corroboration (`detail.requester == "s3.amazonaws.com"` added to the provenance rule, §22.5/§22.5.2/§22.5.3, item 4); the `DisposalRecord` source-identity field count (seven, not six) and source-kind-conditional field validation, plus the `source_kind_version`/`disposal_id_scheme` naming reconciliation (§7.3/§22.2/§22.3, item 5); the S3-key identity QA-matrix test correction (§22.13 item 1, item 6); the redrive command's pre-processing envelope/provenance validation (§22.9.3, item 7 — **this validation sequence is itself further corrected by Round 4, item 3, below**); partial-batch/same-shard-blocking and `ReportBatchItemFailures`/`BisectBatchOnFunctionError` interaction semantics (§22.10/§22.13 item 12, item 8); the recovery bucket's Lifecycle-duration configuration source (`operational_durations.disposal_recovery`, not an evidentiary class, §22.9.2, item 9); the infrastructure inventory (dropping the `MetadataTableStreamArn` Output, de-duplicating `infra/serverless.yml`, naming the recovery-bucket notification rule's file/logical ID, adding the DLQ `QueuePolicy`, correcting `evidence-retention-dlq.yml`'s modified-status, §22.4/§22.14, item 10); production/test file ownership (`commands.py`, `operator_cli/main.py`/`result.py`, and new configuration-loader/CLI-composition tests, §22.14, item 11); and the recovery-artifact classification (contains governed payload data; not "never evidence," §22.9.2, item 12). None of the twelve reopened the architecture Round 2 already locked (native S3 `OnFailure` destination and dedicated recovery bucket; the `"Permanently Deleted"` literal; canonical-JSON deterministic identity; redrive included in A1.4a scope; the batch/retry numeric values; `StartingPosition: LATEST`).

**Round 4 (this revision) applies eight further targeted corrections, per Product Strategy's "RETURN FOR CORRECTION — High Risk" disposition on Round 3:** (1) explicit opt-in enablement of the event-source mapping's `EventCount` metrics category, via `MetricsConfig: { Metrics: ["EventCount"] }` on the `AWS::Lambda::EventSourceMapping` resource, is added, since `OnFailureDestinationDeliveredEventCount`/`DroppedEventCount` do not emit without it (§22.8, §22.10, §22.14); the unprefixed alias `DestinationDeliveredEventCount` (as distinct from `OnFailureDestinationDeliveredEventCount`) is removed everywhere it appeared as if it were a supported name — it is not. (2) the partial-batch/bisection interaction is restated to match AWS's exact documented contract precisely: when `ReportBatchItemFailures` and `BisectBatchOnFunctionError` are both enabled, the function's own returned lowest-failed-sequence-number becomes the checkpoint/retry boundary directly, not a separate mechanism that only engages when no partial response is returned (§22.10, §22.13 item 13). (3) the redrive command's pre-processing validation sequence (§22.9.3) is corrected: the not-implementable "validate the bucket the `GetObject` response was actually served from" check is removed and replaced with a construction-time guarantee — the bucket name passed to `GetObject` is always the one hardcoded/configured recovery bucket, never operator-suppliable — and the remaining validation points are corrected to the actual documented native DynamoDB-event-source-mapping S3-failure-destination object structure (`aws/lambda/{esm-uuid}/...` key convention, `requestContext.functionArn`, envelope `version`, `DDBStreamBatchInfo.streamArn`, `payload`, and per-record `eventSourceARN`). (4) `dynamodb:ListStreams` is restored to `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole` as a separately-scoped statement (Resource `"*"`, since it cannot be scoped to a single stream ARN), matching AWS's own documented `AWSLambdaDynamoDBExecutionRole` action set (§22.11). (5) every occurrence of `aws:ResourceAccount` is corrected to the actual documented condition key, `s3:ResourceAccount` (§22.11, §22.13, §22.14). (6) the redrive-actor IAM contradiction is resolved by removing the second, separate IAM principal for the redrive actor — the redrive command operates under the operator's own existing, pre-configured CLI credentials, the same credential-resolution mechanism every other `rcp` command uses, never a new deployable IAM role (§22.9.3, §22.11, §22.14). (7) an EventBridge `FailedInvocations`-based alarm, an explicit `RetryPolicy`, and required infrastructure-test coverage are added for the recovery bucket's own `Object Created` notification rule (§22.9.2, §22.13, §22.14). (8) §22.14's "New tests" list is corrected to move `tests/unit/test_handler_import_smoke.py` and `tests/unit/test_infra_configuration.py` into "Modified tests," since both already exist today. None of the eight reopens the architecture Round 3 already locked (native S3 `OnFailure` destination and dedicated recovery bucket; the `"Permanently Deleted"` literal; canonical-JSON deterministic identity; the batch/retry numeric values — batch size 25, `MaximumRetryAttempts: 3`, `MaximumRecordAgeInSeconds: 72000`, `ParallelizationFactor: 1`, EventBridge retry 10/3600, Lambda async retry 2/21600; `StartingPosition: LATEST`; redrive included in A1.4a scope; the source-identity field count and naming reconciliation). Each subsection below is marked, where materially rewritten, with which round's item(s) it corrects; subsections not so marked are unchanged by the marked round.

**Round 5 applies three further targeted corrections, per Product Strategy's "RETURN FOR CORRECTION — High Risk" disposition on Round 4 — one substantial implementation-blocking gap, two internal-consistency defects; none reopens any AWS-contract correction Round 4 already made (EventCount metrics enablement, the corrected bisection-boundary contract, the corrected native-destination redrive-validation fields, restored `dynamodb:ListStreams`, the `s3:ResourceAccount` condition-key correction, the redrive-actor credential model, the recovery-bucket-notification observability, or the test-inventory reclassification):** **(1)** a deployment-to-CLI identity configuration contract is defined for §22.9.3's redrive-validation checks (new §22.9.5) — the four values those checks depend on (the configured recovery-bucket name, `evidenceDisposalRecorder`'s own event-source-mapping UUID, its own function ARN, `MetadataTable`'s own stream ARN) had no defined resolution path into `StageConfig`; this is corrected with four new `StageConfig` fields, `REQUIRED_FIELDS`/`ENV_OVERRIDES` entries, the CloudFormation Outputs each value requires, and a new `validate_disposal_recorder_config` fail-closed validator mirroring `validate_scheduler_config`'s existing shape, required to resolve before the redrive command's `GetObject` call. This explicitly and narrowly supersedes Round 3 item 10(a)'s "no `MetadataTableStreamArn` Output" instruction for one new, external consumer only (the operator CLI's own configuration) — Round 3's original reasoning for its own, same-stack consumer (the event-source-mapping's `EventSourceArn` property) is unchanged and not reopened; the Output is reinstated in `infra/resources/dynamodb.yml` for the new reason alone. **(2)** §22.9.2's recovery-bucket-notification paragraph is corrected: a leftover claim that this design achieves its alerting surface "without introducing a second alarm resource" directly contradicted Round 4 item 7's own `EvidenceDisposalRecoveryNotificationFailedInvocationsAlarm` addition — corrected to state plainly that this notification path has its own dedicated, genuinely-new second alarm, covering a failure mode distinct from `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQAlarm`. **(3)** every remaining "seven-point envelope/provenance validation" cross-reference — leftover Round 2/3 phrasing predating Round 4's correction of the actual sequence to a construction-time guarantee plus six numbered checks — is corrected to "the construction-time recovery-bucket guarantee plus the six numbered runtime envelope/provenance checks," at every remaining §22.14 inventory-entry occurrence. This is a documentation-only correction: no code, test, or configuration change is made by this note, and it does not authorize A1.4a implementation. No custody-duration numeric value is introduced by this amendment. Full corrected text: Technical Design §22 (revised in place — new §22.9.5; §7.3 unaffected by this round).

**Round 6 applies three further targeted corrections, per Product Strategy's "RETURN FOR CORRECTION — High Risk" disposition on Round 5 — none reopens anything Round 5 established (the four new `StageConfig` fields and `RCP_*` env-var names, the `validate_scheduler_config`-mirroring shape, the reinstated `MetadataTableStreamArn` Output, or the redrive-credential model):** **(1)** §22.9.3's recovery-bucket trust-boundary narrative is reconciled with §22.9.5's own, already-correct description of `disposal_recovery_bucket_name` — prior text asserted an operator "literally cannot supply or override" the bucket name and that "no environment-variable override exists," directly contradicted by §22.9.5's own `RCP_DISPOSAL_RECOVERY_BUCKET_NAME` override; corrected to state the bucket name is not a CLI argument (`--recovery-object-key` remains the only one) but is resolved from trusted, deployment-configurable `StageConfig`, with narrowly-scoped operator IAM — not the absence of a configuration path — as the actual enforcement boundary (§22.9.3, §22.9.5, §22.11). **(2)** `validate_disposal_recorder_config()` is strengthened from placeholder/emptiness-only detection to real structural validation — a syntactically valid S3 bucket name, a canonical UUID, a well-formed Lambda function ARN, a well-formed DynamoDB Streams ARN, cross-field region/account consistency, and stream table-identity agreement with `audit_metadata_table` — collected into the same single `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR`, so §22.9.5's "malformed configuration fails before `AwsClientFactory` construction" claim is now actually true for structural malformation, not only emptiness (§22.9.5, §22.13, §22.14). **(3)** CloudFormation Output ownership for the two new Outputs is corrected from "added in `infra/serverless.yml`" (not a valid merge shape in this codebase — `infra/serverless.yml` never contains `Outputs:` directly, only imported `resources/*.yml` fragments do) to a new fragment file, `infra/resources/evidence-disposal-recorder-outputs.yml`, added to `infra/serverless.yml`'s `resources:` import list as one new line (§22.9.5, §22.14). This is a documentation-only correction: no code, test, or configuration change is made by this revision, and it does not authorize A1.4a implementation. No custody-duration numeric value is introduced by this revision. Full corrected text: Technical Design §22 (revised in place; §7.3 unaffected by this round).

This correction originates from a read-only investigation of `DisposalRepository` (`evidence_retention/disposal_repository.py`, A1.1, implemented), `evidence_retention/identity.py::generate_disposal_id`, `evidence_retention/constants.py`, the not-yet-implemented `evidence_retention/disposal_recorder.py`, and the infrastructure `evidenceDisposalRecorder` must eventually run under (`infra/serverless.yml`, `infra/resources/dynamodb.yml`, `infra/resources/s3.yml`, `infra/resources/evidence-retention-dlq.yml`, `infra/resources/phase4-aggregation-iam.yml`), followed by thirteen Round 1 corrective requirements and twelve further Round 2 corrective requirements from Product Strategy. Confirmed directly: **no `evidence_retention/disposal_recorder.py` exists in this repository today** (confirmed by directory listing, consistent with §5.1/§6's own labeling of it as future scope), and no `evidenceDisposalRecorder` function block exists in `infra/serverless.yml`'s four current functions (`coreEngineOrchestrator`, `scheduledExecution`, `auditFinalization`, `auditAggregation`). §5.1's platform-position diagram, §10.2/§10.4's sequence diagrams, and §13's Reliability bullets both describe and assume the recorder's target behavior; it is specifically that description — not any already-merged A1.1 code — that this section corrects.

### 22.2 Deterministic, Versioned Disposal Identity (ADR Decision 13, Invariant 38)

**Confirmed defect:** `evidence_retention/identity.py::generate_disposal_id()` (lines 23–25) is `f"{DISPOSAL_ID_PREFIX}{uuid.uuid4().hex}"` — fully random on every call, with `DISPOSAL_ID_PREFIX = "disp_"` (`constants.py:29`). Direct grep across `src/` and `tests/` confirms no caller of this function exists anywhere in the repository besides its own dedicated tests (`tests/unit/evidence_retention/test_models.py`). §10.4's existing sequence diagrams (`generate disposal_id` as an unqualified step) and §13's Reliability section ("a conditional put... makes redelivery idempotent") both assume the conditional put alone is sufficient to make at-least-once redelivery safe — that assumption requires the *same* disposal event to compute the *same* `disposal_id` on every delivery, which a `uuid.uuid4()`-based generator structurally cannot provide: two invocations processing the identical redelivered event would attempt two different `disposal_id` values, and the conditional put (`attribute_not_exists(PK) AND attribute_not_exists(SK)`) would succeed twice, producing two `DisposalRecord`s for one disposal.

**Correction:** `evidenceDisposalRecorder` computes `disposal_id` as `disp_v1_{sha256_hex}`, where `sha256_hex` is the lowercase hex digest of a versioned, canonical, deterministic serialization of the disposing event's own source-identity fields — never Lambda invocation time, event delivery order, or any other non-reproducible input. `generate_disposal_id()` (`identity.py:23-25`) is not repurposed and is not modified by this correction — its existing random form remains available, unused by the recorder, under its existing name; the recorder computes its identity via new, dedicated logic in `disposal_recorder.py`, not by extending `identity.py`'s existing generator to accept parameters it was never designed around. The `disp_` prefix convention (`DISPOSAL_ID_PREFIX`) is preserved; `v1` is a literal version segment, not a computed value, so a future change to the canonicalization scheme can introduce `disp_v2_...` without ambiguity against `v1`-generated identifiers already persisted. This same `v1` value is also persisted independently as `DisposalRecord.disposal_id_scheme` (§7.3, ADR Invariant 49) so a future canonicalization-version audit does not depend on parsing it back out of the `disposal_id` string.

**Correction (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 7 — unambiguous identity encoding):** Round 1's `\x1f`-delimited-string encoding (below, struck through in substance, retained here only as the historical record of what is corrected) is rejected: an S3 key can contain arbitrary bytes, including `\x1f` itself, so "not expected to occur under normal operation" is not a sufficient guarantee against a delimiter-collision producing two structurally different events with the same hash input. Two encodings were considered — canonical JSON (an exact, fixed field list, fixed key order, `json.dumps(..., sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"))`-style deterministic serialization) and a length-prefixed encoding (`len(field1)|field1|len(field2)|field2|...`, unambiguous regardless of field content). **Canonical JSON is selected** as simpler to specify precisely and to verify by inspection (a length-prefixed encoding is equally unambiguous but harder to eyeball-verify against a raw event payload during an investigation) — a real choice, not a default, flagged for confirmation before A1.4a implementation adopts it verbatim. The contract: a fixed Python `dict` with an explicit, versioned field list (below), serialized via `json.dumps(fields, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"))` before hashing; no floating-point-valued field is ever included; no field is ever omitted, renamed, or reordered within a given `disposal_id_scheme` value — any such change requires a new `disposal_id_scheme` value, exactly as `v1`→`v2` would require for `disposal_id` itself. **Naming correction (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 5):** Round 2 named this hash-input field `source_kind_version`, a second, independently-tracked name for the identical versioning concept `DisposalRecord.disposal_id_scheme` (§7.3) already persists. This is corrected: the hash-input field below is named `disposal_id_scheme`, not `source_kind_version` — one name for one concept, used identically in the canonicalization contract and the persisted schema, eliminating the redundant duplicate identifier entirely (§7.3's naming-reconciliation note).

**Correction (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 7 — raw key hashed, not percent-decoded):** Round 1's `canonical_key = percent_decode(detail.object.key)` step (below) is premature and is removed from the identity computation. Whether `detail.object.key` arrives percent-encoded, and under what conditions, requires staging-time confirmation — the same category of AWS-fact-confirmation-required item already flagged elsewhere in this document (§22.5). The identity hash therefore uses `detail.object.key` **exactly as supplied by the event, in whatever encoding it arrives in** — decoding, where needed, is a separate concern, handled entirely within §22.12's dedicated key parser, used only to extract `client_id`/`audit_id`/`evidence_class`. Identity-hash input and human-meaningful-field extraction must never share a decode step whose exact behavior is not yet confirmed.

**Canonicalization contract — DynamoDB TTL path**, over the DynamoDB Streams record's own fields (never a value derived from processing them), serialized as canonical JSON before hashing:

```
fields = {
    "source_kind":         "dynamodb_ttl_remove",
    "disposal_id_scheme": "v1",
    "stream_identity":     record.eventSourceARN,  # the expected MetadataTable stream ARN
                                                     # (§22.4's exact-match rule already requires
                                                     # this to match a configured expectation
                                                     # before this point is reached)
    "event_id":            record.eventID,
}
hash_input = json.dumps(fields, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")).encode("utf-8")
disposal_id = "disp_v1_" + sha256(hash_input).hexdigest()
```

These same four field values (`source_kind`, `stream_identity` as `source_stream_identity`, `event_id` as `source_event_id`) are also persisted verbatim on `DisposalRecord` (§7.3, ADR Invariant 49) — the identity hash's own inputs, not re-derived, so §22.3's duplicate-conflict verification can compare source identity directly.

**Canonicalization contract — S3 Lifecycle path**, over the EventBridge event's `detail` object's content fields — deliberately **excluding** the envelope's own top-level `id` (the EventBridge-assigned delivery ID, distinct from any S3-native request ID): a redelivery of the *same* underlying S3 permanent deletion could, in principle, arrive under a *different* EventBridge `id` on retry, so hashing the envelope ID would defeat determinism across exactly the redelivery scenario this mechanism exists to make safe. Hashing the deletion's own content fields, not the delivery envelope's identity, is what makes this deterministic; `key` below is the **raw, undecoded** value from the event, per the correction above:

```
fields = {
    "source_kind":         "s3_lifecycle_delete",
    "disposal_id_scheme": "v1",
    "bucket":               detail.bucket.name,
    "key":                  detail.object.key,          # raw, exactly as received — NOT percent-decoded
    "version_id":           detail.object["version-id"],
    "reason":               detail.reason,
    "deletion_type":        detail["deletion-type"],
}
hash_input = json.dumps(fields, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")).encode("utf-8")
disposal_id = "disp_v1_" + sha256(hash_input).hexdigest()
```

These same field values (`source_kind`, `bucket` as `source_bucket`, `key` as `source_object_key`, `version_id` as `source_object_version_id`) are also persisted verbatim on `DisposalRecord` (§7.3, ADR Invariant 49) for the identical reason given for the DynamoDB path above.

**(A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 7 — superseded):** the paragraph immediately above this one, in Round 1, specified `\x1f` (ASCII Unit Separator) as the field delimiter for a delimiter-joined string, reasoned as "not expected to occur under normal operation" in any of the hashed fields. This reasoning is rejected — an S3 key can contain arbitrary bytes, and "not expected" is not a structural guarantee — and the encoding itself is replaced with the canonical-JSON contract specified above. The choice of canonical JSON over a length-prefixed alternative, and the exact field list/order within each `source_kind`'s canonicalization contract, remain a judgment call made by this correction — Product Strategy's own instruction invited a "well-reasoned exact format" rather than dictating one — and is flagged for confirmation before A1.4a implementation adopts it verbatim; the versioned `v1` prefix (both in `disposal_id` and in the independently persisted `disposal_id_scheme` field) exists precisely so a confirmed change to this contract does not require reinterpreting already-persisted identifiers.

### 22.3 Duplicate-Conflict Verification, Not Duplicate Assumption (ADR Decision 13, Invariant 39)

**Confirmed defect:** §10.5's existing text — "a conditional put (`attribute_not_exists`) on `DisposalRecord` guards against duplicate records if an event is redelivered" — treats a `ConditionalCheckFailedException` as self-evidently a duplicate. It is only proof that a record already exists at the computed key; under §22.2's deterministic identity, two *different* disposal facts could in principle compute colliding `disposal_id` values (a sha256 collision, or — more realistically — a defect in the canonicalization contract itself failing to fully distinguish two genuinely different events), and silently treating every conflict as "already recorded, nothing to do" would mask that case entirely.

**Correction:** on `ConditionalCheckFailedException` from `DisposalRepository.put_disposal_record`, `evidenceDisposalRecorder` performs a strongly consistent read-back via `DisposalRepository.get_disposal_record(client_id, audit_id, disposal_id, consistent_read=True)` and compares the existing record against the new attempt's own freshly computed values, field by field:

- `record_type`, `client_id`, `audit_id`, `disposal_id` (the identity fields the read was keyed on, compared anyway as a structural sanity check on the read-back itself);
- **the persisted source-event identity (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 6 — new comparison surface, not present in Round 1's text)**: `disposal_id_scheme`, `source_kind`, and whichever of `source_stream_identity`/`source_event_id` (DynamoDB path) or `source_bucket`/`source_object_key`/`source_object_version_id` (S3 path) applies to `source_kind` — §7.3's new fields, ADR Invariant 49. Round 1's text compared only disposal facts (below); comparing disposal facts alone does not satisfy "compare source identity and immutable facts," since two structurally different disposing events could in principle produce matching disposal facts (e.g., the same `evidence_class`/`disposal_mechanism`/`disposed_identity_ref` recomputed from two different, non-identical source events) without a source-identity comparison to distinguish them;
- every existing immutable disposal fact: `evidence_class`, `disposal_mechanism`, `disposed_identity_ref`, `disposed_at`, `source_created_at`, `custody_period_days_applied`.

`recorded_at` is excluded from the comparison — the only field excluded — because it is processing-time and expected to differ between the original write and this verification attempt. An exact match on every other compared field is a **confirmed idempotent duplicate**: acknowledge, no new write, no retry, no DLQ delivery. Any single-field mismatch — including a source-identity mismatch, not only a disposal-fact mismatch — is a **`disposal_id` collision with unequal immutable content** — a distinct integrity failure (§22.7 category (f)), routed to the recorder's own failure/retry path (never silently accepted, never resolved by a second, overwriting `PutItem`, which `DisposalRepository.put_disposal_record`'s conditional expression would in any case reject).

**Required change to `DisposalRepository` (A1.4a implementation scope, not made by this correction):** `get_disposal_record` (`disposal_repository.py:90-97`) has no `consistent_read` parameter today — confirmed by direct re-read; unlike `HoldRepository.get_legal_hold` (`hold_repository.py:136-163`), which already accepts `consistent_read: bool = False` and threads it through `_get_item(key, consistent_read=True)` (`hold_repository.py:384-391`), `DisposalRepository`'s `_get_item` (`disposal_repository.py:154-156`) has no equivalent parameter or code path. A1.4a implementation must add `consistent_read: bool = False` to both `get_disposal_record` and the internal `_get_item` helper, mirroring `HoldRepository`'s existing pattern exactly (default `False` so any other future caller of `get_disposal_record` remains behaviorally unchanged; `ConsistentRead=True` passed explicitly only when requested) — `evidenceDisposalRecorder`'s duplicate-conflict verification is the first caller requiring `consistent_read=True`, for the same reasoning already established for `rcp retention hold status` (§21.3): this read may immediately follow a conflicting write by another concurrent invocation, and an eventually-consistent read has no transactional backstop to catch staleness on this path. **A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 6 additionally requires:** `models.py`'s `DisposalRecord` model gains the seven new fields §7.3 now specifies (count corrected A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 5) (`disposal_id_scheme`, `source_kind`, `source_stream_identity`, `source_event_id`, `source_bucket`, `source_object_key`, `source_object_version_id`), and `disposal_repository.py`'s `put_disposal_record` write path is extended to persist them — both are reclassified from omitted to modified production files (§22.14).

### 22.4 DynamoDB Streams TTL Provenance Rule (ADR Decision 13, Invariant 40)

**Confirmed defect:** §10.2's existing validation flow — "only `REMOVE` events with `userIdentity.principalId == "dynamodb.amazonaws.com"` are processed" — checks one field (`principalId`) and one event name, but does not check `userIdentity.type`, does not check that the record's stream/table identity is the one `evidenceDisposalRecorder` is actually meant to consume, does not require `OLD_IMAGE` to be present, and does not require `OLD_IMAGE` to represent a record the recorder actually recognizes as governed. A DynamoDB Streams event-source mapping delivers **every** stream record type (`INSERT`, `MODIFY`, `REMOVE` alike, per `StreamViewType: NEW_AND_OLD_IMAGES`, `dynamodb.yml:43-44`) to the same Lambda; an incomplete filter risks either missing a genuine TTL disposal or, worse, attempting to construct a `DisposalRecord` from a record shape that does not actually describe one.

**Corrected exact-match rule — every element required, no partial match accepted (ADR Invariant 40):**

1. **Expected stream identity.** `record.eventSourceARN` must equal `evidenceDisposalRecorder`'s configured expectation for `MetadataTable`'s stream (supplied at deployment time — **corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 10(a):** `evidenceDisposalRecorder`'s event-source-mapping configuration lives in the same `infra/serverless.yml`/`infra/resources/` template set as `MetadataTable` itself, so **the event-source-mapping resource's own `EventSourceArn` property specifically** is resolved via a direct, same-stack `Fn::GetAtt: [MetadataTable, StreamArn]` reference at the point of use, never via a CloudFormation Output — this reasoning, scoped to this one same-stack consumer, is unchanged and not reopened. **Partially superseded for a different consumer (A1.4a.0 Round 5, item 1):** Round 3 additionally stated, as a general rule, that no `MetadataTableStreamArn` Output is added at all. That general statement is corrected: §22.9.3's redrive-validation checks 4 and 6 require the operator CLI — a process running entirely outside CloudFormation, for which a same-stack `Fn::GetAtt` reference is structurally unavailable — to independently know `MetadataTable`'s own stream ARN as a `StageConfig` value (`metadata_table_stream_arn`, §22.9.5). For that different, external consumer, the `MetadataTableStreamArn` Output **is** reinstated in `infra/resources/dynamodb.yml` (§22.9.5, §22.14). The event-source-mapping's own `EventSourceArn` property continues to resolve via the same-stack `Fn::GetAtt` reference above and never consumes this Output — the Output exists solely to serve the CLI's own configuration, a purpose Round 3 never considered.).
2. **`eventName == "REMOVE"`** exactly — `INSERT`/`MODIFY` records are never candidates for disposal recording. The DynamoDB Streams event-source mapping's own `FilterCriteria` (§22.10) is configured to filter to `REMOVE` only *before* invoking the Lambda at all, as a cost-reduction measure — but this element is still independently re-checked here, in the handler, as defense-in-depth; the mapping-level filter is never trusted alone.
3. **DynamoDB service-origin identity** — `userIdentity.type == "Service"` **and** `userIdentity.principalId == "dynamodb.amazonaws.com"`, both required (the existing text checks only the second).
4. **`OLD_IMAGE` present.** A `REMOVE` event without an `OLD_IMAGE` (should not occur given `NEW_AND_OLD_IMAGES`, but a malformed or truncated event is not structurally impossible) cannot supply the fields a `DisposalRecord` requires.
5. **`OLD_IMAGE` represents a valid governed record** — carries `PK`/`SK`, an `evidence_class` value that is a member of the governed Category 1/2 set (§18.1), and a usable creation-timestamp-equivalent field for `source_created_at`. A `REMOVE` event whose `OLD_IMAGE` lacks any of these (e.g., a hypothetical future application-initiated delete of a Category 3–6 record that happened to carry `userIdentity.principalId == "dynamodb.amazonaws.com"` through some indirect path, or a record shape the recorder does not recognize) fails element 5.

**Disposition on failure (corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 5 — the disposition boundary below replaces Round 1's grouping of elements 1–3 together):**

- **Element 1 (wrong stream identity) failing is not valid-but-irrelevant.** It is **unknown/incompatible source** (§22.7 category (d)) — fail closed. A stream-identity mismatch means this recorder received an event it structurally should never see at all (a source-identity problem, not "a real event this mechanism doesn't act on"); Round 1 grouped this with elements 2–3 as a uniform "valid-but-irrelevant" outcome, which Product Strategy rejected — a wrong source must never be acknowledged merely because doing so is operationally convenient.
- **Element 2 (`eventName != "REMOVE"`, i.e. `INSERT`/`MODIFY`) failing is valid-but-irrelevant** (§22.7 category (b)) — one of exactly four cases in the corrected, narrowed valid-but-irrelevant set (§22.7). This is a real, correctly-delivered stream event on the expected stream that this mechanism was never meant to act on.
- **Element 3 (non-service-origin `userIdentity` on a genuine `REMOVE`, i.e. a non-TTL application delete) failing is valid-but-irrelevant** (§22.7 category (b)) — the second of the four cases: a real application-initiated delete on the expected stream, not a TTL disposal.
- **Elements 4–5 failing, *given* elements 1–3 already passed** (a genuine TTL-driven `REMOVE` on the expected stream, but with a payload shape the recorder cannot parse or validate), is a **malformed target-provenance event** (§22.7 category (c)) — the event looked exactly like a case this mechanism should handle, and did not parse, which is a materially different, higher-concern condition than a correctly-recognized irrelevant event, and different again from element 1's outright wrong-source case.

No DynamoDB Streams record carries an explicit schema-version field the way the EventBridge "Object Deleted" envelope does (§22.5's `detail["event-version"]`); this asymmetry between the two paths is stated honestly here rather than papered over by inventing an equivalent check that does not exist for DynamoDB Streams records.

### 22.5 S3 EventBridge Envelope, Provenance, and Permanent-Disposal Semantics (ADR Decision 13, Invariant 41)

**Confirmed defect:** ADR Decision 4's existing text ("S3 Event Notifications are configured for `s3:LifecycleExpiration:*` events... routed through EventBridge") and §10.2/§10.4's matching language describe an S3-native notification *event-type naming convention* (`s3:LifecycleExpiration:Delete`, `:DeleteMarkerCreated`) that applies to S3's direct SQS/SNS/Lambda notification configuration, not to the EventBridge-routed configuration this design actually specifies. `infra/resources/s3.yml`'s `RawResultsBucket.NotificationConfiguration.EventBridgeConfiguration: {}` (`s3.yml:114-115`) enables **all** S3-originated EventBridge events for the bucket, delivered under EventBridge's own, differently-shaped event envelope — there is no `s3:LifecycleExpiration:*` sub-type concept in that envelope at all. The recorder's actual event-source rule (not yet defined — A1.4a implementation scope) must filter on the EventBridge envelope's own fields, not on the S3-native naming this document's prior text borrowed.

**Corrected S3 provenance and permanence rule (ADR Invariant 41), standard AWS platform facts stated here as this correction's basis but requiring confirmation against a real staging-environment event capture at implementation time, not treated as repository-verified the way DynamoDB Streams' documented shape is:**

**Schema-version field name, corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 3):** Round 2's text below, and every other occurrence in this document and the companion ADR, used `detail.version` as the schema-version field name. This is corrected: the actual field is `detail["event-version"]` — a hyphenated key nested under `detail`, distinct from the EventBridge envelope's own top-level `version` field (the envelope-level field describing the EventBridge event envelope schema itself, not the S3-specific "Object Deleted" `detail` payload's schema). Every occurrence of `detail.version` describing the S3 "Object Deleted" schema-version check, across this document and the companion ADR, is corrected to `detail["event-version"]` by this Round 3 amendment.

- **Envelope:** `source == "aws.s3"`, `detail-type == "Object Deleted"`, `account` equal to the expected AWS account ID (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 4 — new check, not present in Round 1's text; now also enforced at the EventBridge rule's own `EventPattern`, §22.5.2, A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 3), `detail["event-version"]` (the EventBridge "Object Deleted" event's own `detail`-level schema-version field, A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 4; field name corrected A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 3) validated per the semver-style compatibility rule below, top-level `id` present but **excluded from the identity hash** (§22.2) — it is an EventBridge delivery ID, not a content-derived identity.
- **Schema-version compatibility rule, corrected from equality to semver-style compatibility (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 3):** Round 2 validated `detail["event-version"]` as "equal to a supported value" — an exact-match check that would reject a future, backward-compatible minor-version bump from AWS. This is corrected: the handler parses `detail["event-version"]` as `{MAJOR}.{MINOR}` and validates (1) the **MAJOR** component matches a specific, configured supported major version exactly (a major-version change may alter the envelope shape in a breaking way and must not be silently accepted), and (2) the **MINOR** component is **greater than or equal to** a configured minimum supported minor version (a higher minor version is presumed forward-compatible/additive per semver convention and is accepted; a lower minor version than this handler was built against is not, since it may lack a field this handler already depends on). A version string that does not parse as `{MAJOR}.{MINOR}`, or whose MAJOR does not match, or whose MINOR is below the configured minimum, fails this check and routes to **unknown/incompatible source or schema** (§22.7 category (d)) — never silently accepted, never treated as valid-but-irrelevant.
- **`detail` object:** `bucket.name` (must equal the expected `RawResultsBucket` name), `object.key`, `object.version-id` (presence required, A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 4), `object.sequencer` (not used by this design), `reason` (e.g., `"Lifecycle Expiration"`), `deletion-type` (e.g., `"Permanently Deleted"` versus `"Delete Marker Created"`), `requester` (new, A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 4 — see the provenance rule below).
- **Reason filter, reclassified (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 5):** only `detail.reason == "Lifecycle Expiration"` is in scope for this mechanism. Round 1 classified any other or absent `reason` value as valid-but-irrelevant; this is corrected — the narrowed, exhaustive valid-but-irrelevant set (§22.7) does not include "any S3 delete for a reason other than Lifecycle Expiration" as one of its four cases, so an event with correct envelope/bucket/account/schema provenance but an unexpected `reason` value now fails closed as **unknown/incompatible source or schema** (§22.7 category (d)) instead — this mechanism cannot confidently attest to a deletion it cannot positively identify as Lifecycle-driven, and a wrong source must never be acknowledged merely because doing so is operationally convenient.
- **Permanence filter (§22.5.1 below):** only `detail.deletion-type == "Permanently Deleted"` is recordable.
- **Provenance rule, strengthened — `reason` and `requester` together, corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 4):** Round 2's honesty statement — "no field beyond `detail.reason == "Lifecycle Expiration"` itself is known to independently corroborate genuine Lifecycle-service provenance" — is **removed; it is no longer accurate.** AWS's S3 EventBridge "Object Deleted" event schema, for a lifecycle-driven deletion, exposes `detail.requester == "s3.amazonaws.com"` — the AWS-service-origin identity for the deletion, corroborating that the deletion was S3-Lifecycle-service-initiated rather than some other deletion path (an application `DeleteObject` call, an operator console action, a different AWS service) that happened to produce a matching `reason`/`deletion-type` combination. **The corrected provenance rule requires both fields together:** `detail.reason == "Lifecycle Expiration"` **and** `detail.requester == "s3.amazonaws.com"`. An event with `reason == "Lifecycle Expiration"` but a `requester` value other than `"s3.amazonaws.com"` (or a missing `requester` field) fails this corroboration check and routes to **unknown/incompatible source or schema** (§22.7 category (d)) — a `reason` value alone is no longer trusted as sufficient attestation now that a corroborating field is known. This check is required in handler-side validation (§22.5.3) and, where EventBridge's own pattern language supports matching on it — confirmed: EventBridge content-based filtering matches on any field nested under `detail`, `requester` included, identically to how `reason`/`deletion-type` are already matched above — is also added to the EventBridge rule's own `EventPattern` (§22.5.2), per this design's established two-layer, defense-in-depth discipline (the handler-side check is retained even though the rule now also filters on it — the rule-level filter is never trusted alone, exactly as for every other rule-level check in this design). Reference basis: AWS's S3 EventBridge notification event schema documentation (`ev-events.html`).

#### 22.5.1 Permanent-Disposal Semantics — Why `"Delete Marker Created"` Must Never Be Recorded

**The specific defect this closes, stated precisely rather than left implicit:** `RawResultsBucket` has `VersioningConfiguration.Status: Enabled` (confirmed, `s3.yml`). For a versioned bucket, an S3 `DELETE` request that does not specify an explicit `VersionId` does not remove any object content — it inserts a new **delete marker** as the current version, while every prior version (including the one a naive reader might assume was "deleted") remains physically present and independently addressable by its own version ID. AWS's own EventBridge "Object Deleted" notification surfaces this distinction directly via `detail.deletion-type`: `"Permanently Deleted"` for an actual version removal (which is what S3 Lifecycle's `NoncurrentVersionExpiration`/`Expiration` actions perform when they act on a specific, already-identified version), versus `"Delete Marker Created"` for the marker-insertion case. Recording a `DisposalRecord` for a `"Delete Marker Created"` event would assert, in a compliance-facing durable record, that evidence was disposed when the underlying object version in fact still exists — a false disposal fact, more severe in kind than simply missing a real disposal, since it actively misinforms a future compliance query rather than merely lagging one.

**Why this is a real, not merely theoretical, risk for this specific mechanism:** S3 Lifecycle's own `Expiration` action, for a versioned bucket, does not delete the current version outright — its documented behavior is to insert a delete marker as the new current version while the previously-current version becomes noncurrent (subsequently handled by `NoncurrentVersionExpiration`, per this design's existing two-action Lifecycle rule shape, ADR Decision 1). This means a **single** Lifecycle evaluation cycle acting on one object can legitimately produce *two* EventBridge "Object Deleted" notifications with *different* `deletion-type` values in short succession: one `"Delete Marker Created"` (for the `Expiration` action's marker insertion — never recordable) and, on a later cycle once `NoncurrentDays` elapses for the now-noncurrent version, one `"Permanently Deleted"` (for the actual version removal — recordable). A recorder that filtered only on event *presence*, without this exact `deletion-type` check, would produce a `DisposalRecord` up to one full Lifecycle cycle before the evidence was actually, permanently gone.

**AWS-fact confirmation required at implementation/staging time, stated honestly rather than asserted as verified:** the exact string values `"Permanently Deleted"` and `"Delete Marker Created"`, and whether any third `deletion-type` value exists for a case not covered by these two, are AWS platform facts this correction states as its design basis but does not claim to have verified against a live AWS account or current AWS documentation as part of this read-only, repository-scoped investigation. A1.4a implementation must capture a real event from a staging-environment `RawResultsBucket` Lifecycle expiration before relying on these exact string values in a fail-open way; until then, the recorder's provenance rule (§22.4's DynamoDB-side rule and this rule's S3-side counterpart) must treat any `deletion-type` value other than the exact literal `"Permanently Deleted"` as **not recordable** — an unrecognized (neither `"Permanently Deleted"` nor `"Delete Marker Created"`) value must be treated as **unknown/incompatible** (§22.7 category (d), fail closed) rather than silently folded into valid-but-irrelevant, since an unrecognized value could, in principle, represent a real permanent deletion under a renamed or newly introduced AWS value this correction did not anticipate — silently discarding it risks under-recording a genuine disposal, which this design's fail-closed posture does not tolerate.

#### 22.5.2 EventBridge Rule-Level `EventPattern` — Layer One (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 4)

The EventBridge rule that delivers `RawResultsBucket`'s S3-originated events to `evidenceDisposalRecorder` filters at the rule level, before the Lambda is ever invoked. This is the first of two filtering layers this correction requires (§22.5.3 is the second); it exists to reduce invocation volume and cost, and is never trusted as the recorder's own correctness boundary — every element it filters on is independently re-checked in the handler (§22.5.3).

```
{
  "account": ["${aws:accountId}"],
  "source": ["aws.s3"],
  "detail-type": ["Object Deleted"],
  "detail": {
    "bucket": { "name": ["${self:custom.rawResultsBucketName}"] },
    "reason": ["Lifecycle Expiration"],
    "requester": ["s3.amazonaws.com"],
    "deletion-type": ["Permanently Deleted"],
    "object": {
      "key": [
        { "prefix": "raw-results/" },
        { "prefix": "intelligence/" },
        { "prefix": "reports/" },
        { "prefix": "integrity/" }
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

**`account` matching added at the rule level (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 3):** Round 2's rule-level `EventPattern` filtered on `bucket.name`/`reason`/`deletion-type`/`object.key` but left `account` matching to handler-side validation only (§22.5.3) — EventBridge's own pattern-matching language supports matching the envelope's top-level `account` field directly (identical in kind to the `source`/`detail-type` top-level matches already present), so this is corrected to add a top-level `account` match to the rule itself. **The existing handler-side `account` re-validation (§22.5.3) is retained, not removed** — this design's own established two-layer, defense-in-depth principle applies here exactly as it does to every other rule-level check: the rule-level filter reduces invocation volume/cost, and the handler never trusts it alone.

**`requester` matching added at the rule level (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 4):** per the corrected provenance rule above (§22.5), `detail.requester == "s3.amazonaws.com"` is added to the rule's `detail` object alongside `reason`/`deletion-type` — EventBridge matches any field nested under `detail` identically regardless of which specific key it is, so this requires no different matching capability than `reason`/`deletion-type` already use. Handler-side re-validation of `requester` (§22.5.3) is retained per the same two-layer principle.

**Whether EventBridge's own pattern language can prefix-match `detail.object.key`, stated plainly:** yes — EventBridge content-based filtering supports a `prefix` matcher, used above as an inclusive `anyOf`-shaped match across the four evidence-class prefixes (an object-key array of `{ "prefix": ... }` matchers is itself already a logical OR under EventBridge's pattern-matching semantics). This is used deliberately as an **inclusive allow-list** of the four known-good prefixes, rather than an exclusion (`anything-but`) of `retention-markers/` and the recovery bucket — an allow-list of exactly four prefixes structurally excludes everything else, including `retention-markers/` and any future unrecognized prefix, without depending on whether EventBridge's `anything-but` operator supports a `prefix` sub-matcher (a detail this design does not need to rely on either way). The recovery bucket (§22.9.1) never matches this rule at all, independent of any key-prefix filtering, because its `bucket.name` differs structurally from `${self:custom.rawResultsBucketName}` — this is the same bucket-level exclusion Invariant 45 describes as structural rather than filter-dependent. This exact pattern shape is a standard, current EventBridge capability stated here as this correction's design basis, flagged, per this document's established convention, for confirmation against a real deployed rule at implementation time. Note that `detail["event-version"]`'s semver-style MAJOR/MINOR compatibility check (§22.5) is **not** replicated at the rule level — EventBridge's pattern language matches on exact/prefix/numeric-range values, not an application-level semver comparison, so schema-version compatibility remains a handler-side-only check (§22.5.3), never expressed at the rule level.

#### 22.5.3 Handler-Side Defense-in-Depth Validation — Layer Two (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 4)

The rule-level `EventPattern` above is never trusted alone. `evidenceDisposalRecorder`'s own handler independently re-validates every element the rule filters on, plus additional checks the rule cannot express, before any event may reach disposition (a):

1. **Repeats every rule-level check:** `source`, `detail-type`, `account` (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 3 — now also filtered at the rule level, §22.5.2; retained here as defense-in-depth per this design's two-layer principle), `bucket.name`, `reason`, `requester` (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 4 — now also filtered at the rule level, §22.5.2), `deletion-type`, and prefix membership (via §22.6's own dedicated prefix check, not a re-implementation of the rule's `prefix` matcher).
2. **Expected AWS account ID** — the event envelope's own `account` field, checked against the recorder's configured expectation. **Corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 3):** the EventBridge rule's `EventPattern` (§22.5.2) now also filters on `account` at the rule level; this handler-side re-check is retained regardless, per this design's established two-layer, defense-in-depth discipline — the rule-level filter is never trusted alone.
3. **Supported event schema version, field name and comparison corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 3)** — `detail["event-version"]` (not `detail.version` — corrected field name, §22.5), validated via the semver-style compatibility rule §22.5 specifies: MAJOR must match a specific supported major version exactly; MINOR must be greater than or equal to a configured minimum supported minor version. A version that fails either check is unknown/incompatible (§22.7 category (d)), never silently accepted.
4. **`version-id` presence** — `detail.object["version-id"]` must be present; its absence is a malformed target-provenance event (§22.7 category (c)), not silently tolerated.
5. **Lifecycle-service provenance, strengthened (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 4 — corrects Round 2's honesty statement, which is no longer accurate and is removed):** `detail.reason == "Lifecycle Expiration"` **and** `detail.requester == "s3.amazonaws.com"` are both required together — §22.5's corrected provenance rule. `requester` is now also filtered at the rule level (§22.5.2); this handler-side re-check is retained per the same two-layer discipline as every other check in this list. An event satisfying `reason` alone, without a matching `requester`, is not recordable and routes to unknown/incompatible source or schema (§22.7 category (d)).

Any failure at this layer resolves per §22.4/§22.5/§22.6/§22.7's disposition rules exactly as if the rule-level filter had not been applied at all — the handler's own validation is the authoritative boundary, and the rule-level filter is treated purely as a cost optimization.

### 22.6 Evidence-Prefix Restriction (ADR Decision 13, Invariant 42)

**Confirmed defect:** §10.4's existing S3 disposal-recording sequence ("extract client_id/audit_id/evidence_class from S3 key path segments") does not state which prefixes are in scope, and does not exclude `retention-markers/` — the operational, non-evidentiary prefix ADR Invariant 22 / §19.5.8 already establish as governance metadata, never subject to any evidence-class Lifecycle rule and never enumerated by `CustodySweepClient`'s own evidence-retagging sweep. `retention-markers/` **does** have its own, separately governed S3 Lifecycle rule (§19.5.8) and **will** produce `Object Deleted` EventBridge notifications when that rule expires a marker — a recorder with no prefix restriction would attempt to construct a `DisposalRecord` for marker disposal, misclassifying operational bookkeeping as evidence disposal.

**Corrected rule:** a recordable S3 event's `detail.object.key` must resolve, by prefix, to exactly one of the four authoritative evidence-class prefixes confirmed in `constants.py::S3_EVIDENCE_CLASS_PREFIXES` (`raw-results/`, `intelligence/`, `reports/`, `integrity/`). An event whose key falls under `retention-markers/` — the one explicitly recognized, non-evidentiary exclusion — is **valid-but-irrelevant** (§22.7 category (b)): acknowledged, no `DisposalRecord` written, no retry, no dead-letter-queue delivery.

**Corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 5): an unrecognized prefix is not valid-but-irrelevant.** Round 1's text grouped "`retention-markers/` or unrecognized prefix" together as a single valid-but-irrelevant outcome; Product Strategy rejected this. The narrowed, exhaustive valid-but-irrelevant set (§22.7) contains exactly four cases, and "any other, unrecognized S3 prefix" is not one of them. An event whose key falls under any prefix other than the four evidence-class prefixes and `retention-markers/` must fail closed as **unknown/incompatible** (§22.7 category (d)) — an unrecognized prefix is structurally different from `retention-markers/`, which this mechanism explicitly knows about and explicitly excludes; an unrecognized prefix is one this mechanism has no basis to confidently characterize at all, and must not be silently folded into the same handling as a known, deliberate exclusion merely because both result in "no `DisposalRecord`" today. The dedicated recovery bucket (§22.9.1) never reaches this prefix check in the first place — it is a structurally separate bucket, excluded at the EventBridge rule's own bucket-name constraint (§22.5.2) before any key-prefix evaluation occurs.

The same restriction applies, structurally, to the DynamoDB side via §22.4's requirement that `OLD_IMAGE.evidence_class` be a member of the governed Category 1/2 set — `LegalHold`/`LegalHoldEvent` (Category 4) never carry `ttl_disposal_at` in the first place (ADR Invariant 8), so a `REMOVE` event for either is not TTL-driven and is excluded by §22.4's provenance rule before evidence-class scoping is even reached.

### 22.7 Event-Disposition Taxonomy (ADR Decision 13, Invariant 43)

**Confirmed defect:** §10.5's existing error-handling paragraph describes a two-outcome model — a disposal-recording attempt either succeeds, or it "fails" and is "logged... does not block... subsequent events," with a conditional put separately described as guarding against duplicates. This conflates at least six operationally and governance-distinct outcomes into "succeeded" or "failed, logged, move on," which is insufficiently precise for a mechanism whose entire purpose is producing a compliance-facing durable record.

**Every event `evidenceDisposalRecorder` processes is classified into exactly one of the following six dispositions, in this fixed evaluation order, before any write is attempted:**

**Corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 5): valid-but-irrelevant is exactly four cases, not an open-ended "a real event this mechanism doesn't act on."** Round 1's table listed a broad, illustrative set of triggers under (b), including "wrong stream," "non-`Lifecycle Expiration` reason," and "unrecognized prefix." Product Strategy rejected this breadth: a wrong source is not the same kind of outcome as a real, correctly-scoped event this mechanism deliberately does not act on, and folding both into one disposition risks silently acknowledging events that should instead raise concern. The table below replaces Round 1's (b) row with the narrowed, exhaustive four-case set.

| # | Disposition | Trigger | Required handling |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| (a) | Recordable governed disposal | Passes §22.4's DynamoDB rule or §22.5's S3 rule in full, including §22.5.3's handler-side defense-in-depth checks and §22.6's prefix restriction | Compute `disposal_id` (§22.2), attempt `DisposalRepository.put_disposal_record` |
| (b) | Valid-but-irrelevant (exactly four cases — A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 5) | **Exactly:** (i) DynamoDB `INSERT`/`MODIFY`; (ii) a `REMOVE` with non-DynamoDB-service `userIdentity` (a non-TTL application delete); (iii) `detail.deletion-type == "Delete Marker Created"`; (iv) the explicitly recognized `retention-markers/` prefix. No other trigger belongs in this disposition. | Acknowledge; no `DisposalRecord`, no retry, no DLQ delivery |
| (c) | Malformed target-provenance event | An event that structurally matches a recordable shape (correct stream/bucket/account/schema, correct event type) but whose fields do not parse or validate (missing `OLD_IMAGE` fields, unparseable S3 key, missing required `detail` fields, missing `version-id`) | Fail for retry, subject to §22.8's failure-plane routing and §22.9's payload preservation where applicable |
| (d) | Unknown/incompatible source or schema (broadened — A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 5) | An event shape the recorder does not recognize or does not trust at all, **including:** a wrong stream/table identity (DynamoDB, §22.4 element 1); a wrong bucket, account, or unsupported schema version (S3, §22.5/§22.5.3); a non-`"Lifecycle Expiration"` `reason` value (§22.5); any `deletion-type` value other than the exact literal `"Permanently Deleted"` or `"Delete Marker Created"` (§22.5.1); and any S3 key prefix other than the four evidence-class prefixes or `retention-markers/` (§22.6) | Fail closed — never silently treated as (b) |
| (e) | Confirmed idempotent duplicate | §22.3's read-back verification finds an exact match on every immutable compared field, including the persisted source-event identity (§22.3, ADR Invariant 49) | Acknowledge as success; no new write, no retry |
| (f) | `disposal_id` collision, unequal content | §22.3's read-back verification finds any mismatch, including a source-identity mismatch | Integrity failure — distinct handling from (e); never silently accepted or overwritten |

**The ordering is load-bearing:** (a) is only reached after (b)/(c)/(d) have been ruled out by §22.4/§22.5/§22.6's exact-match rules; (e)/(f) are only reached from within (a)'s own write attempt, on a conditional-write conflict. An implementation that collapses any two of these six into one code path (e.g., treating (c) and (d) identically, or treating (e) and (f) identically) does not satisfy this taxonomy — each disposition has a distinct required handling specified above, and §22.13's future QA matrix requires positive and negative test coverage proving all six are independently reachable and distinguishable. Category (c) versus (d) is a real, load-bearing distinction, not a matter of convenience: (c) is reserved for an event whose *source* is confirmed correct (right stream/bucket/account/schema/event-type) but whose *content* does not parse; (d) is reserved for an event whose source itself cannot be confirmed correct. An event with correct target provenance but missing required fields (e.g., a genuine TTL `REMOVE` on the expected stream with an incomplete `OLD_IMAGE`) remains category (c), not (d) — Product Strategy's own item 5 language ("fail closed as unknown/incompatible provenance or schema") is read here as describing the *fail-closed family* generally (not valid-but-irrelevant), not as collapsing (c) into (d) specifically; this reading is stated explicitly as a drafting clarification, not left ambiguous.

### 22.8 Separate Failure Planes (ADR Decision 13, Invariant 44)

**Confirmed defect:** §6's `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ` row and §13's "Dead-letter queue and failure destination on both event sources" bullet describe one SQS resource serving as "the" failure destination without separating the distinct AWS mechanisms that would each need to be independently configured to actually route a failure there. `infra/resources/evidence-retention-dlq.yml` today defines only the queue and its depth alarm — "A1.3/A1.4 wiring" is explicitly deferred (confirmed by the file's own header comment) — so no failure plane is wired yet, and describing "both event sources" as though there were exactly two configuration points undercounts the actual mechanisms involved.

**Four distinct planes, each requiring its own explicit configuration and its own automated observability signal — one DLQ resource existing does not, by itself, mean any of the four is correctly wired or independently observable.**

**Corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 2): plane (iii) no longer routes to `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ`.** A DynamoDB event-source mapping has exactly one `OnFailure` destination slot. §22.9's payload-preserving S3 recovery destination now occupies that slot for plane (iii) specifically — `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ` (SQS) is retained, but scoped to planes (i) and (ii) only, going forward. The ADR's original Decision 7 text, which assumed the DynamoDB and S3 paths could share the same SQS DLQ, is amended by this correction: it may no longer be read as authorizing `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ` as plane (iii)'s destination.

**Corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 2): every plane below now names a concrete, alarmable CloudWatch metric — the "no native metric is known" claims Round 2 stated for several planes are removed and replaced with named metrics, per ADR Non-Negotiable Invariant 44's own explicit requirement that discoverability solely through manual CloudWatch Logs inspection does not satisfy any plane. Reference basis: `docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/monitoring-metrics-types.html`, `docs.aws.amazon.com/eventbridge/latest/userguide/eb-monitoring.html` — cited as this correction's design basis, flagged, per this document's established convention, for staging-time confirmation, not treated as repository-verified.**

- **(i) EventBridge-to-Lambda delivery failure** (the EventBridge rule fails to invoke the Lambda at all — e.g., a permissions failure on the resource-based `AWS::Lambda::Permission`, §22.11, or the rule's own retry policy exhausting). Configuration: the EventBridge rule's Lambda target must define its own `RetryPolicy` (`MaximumRetryAttempts`, `MaximumEventAgeInSeconds` — concrete values selected at §22.10) and its own `DeadLetterConfig` pointing at `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ` — this is a property of the **EventBridge rule/target**, distinct from anything configured on the Lambda function itself. **Observability, corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 2):** EventBridge's own `InvocationsFailedToBeSentToDlq` metric — dimensioned by `RuleName`/`EventBusName` — fires specifically when EventBridge itself fails to deliver a failed-invocation record to this rule's own configured DLQ (`evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ`); a dedicated CloudWatch alarm on this metric (`Sum > 0` over a short evaluation period) is the named, plane-specific signal, in addition to the existing `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQAlarm` (queue-depth) which still fires on any message that *does* land via this plane.
- **(ii) Lambda asynchronous-invocation failure, S3/EventBridge path specifically** (the Lambda is successfully invoked but the invocation itself fails — an unhandled exception, timeout, or throttle on an asynchronous invoke). Configuration: the Lambda function's own `DestinationConfig.OnFailure` (or the equivalent `EventInvokeConfig` for an EventBridge-triggered async invocation) pointed at `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ` — a property of the **Lambda function's event-invoke configuration**, distinct from (i)'s rule-level `DeadLetterConfig`, even though both may ultimately target the same queue. **Observability, corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 2):** Lambda's own `DestinationDeliveryFailures` metric, dimensioned by `FunctionName`, fires when Lambda fails to deliver an asynchronous invocation's failure record to its configured destination (this plane's `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ`) — a dedicated CloudWatch alarm on this metric is the named, plane-specific signal. `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQAlarm` fires on any message landing via this plane too; additionally, Lambda's own function-level `Errors` and `Throttles` CloudWatch metrics (standard, already-documented Lambda metrics, not specific to this correction) provide an earlier, plane-specific signal of invocation trouble before a given event necessarily exhausts its async-retry budget and reaches the DLQ.
- **(iii) DynamoDB Streams event-source-mapping processing failure — now routed to the dedicated recovery bucket, not `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ`** (the poll-based Streams consumption path, governed by an entirely different retry/failure model than Lambda's asynchronous-invoke model — §19.16's transaction-item budget work and this design both already rely on this distinction elsewhere). Configuration: the event-source mapping's own `DestinationConfig.OnFailure` (§22.9.1's dedicated recovery bucket), `MaximumRetryAttempts`, `BisectBatchOnFunctionError`, and `MaximumRecordAgeInSeconds` (§22.10) — a property of the **event-source mapping**. **Observability, corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 2 — Round 2's "no metric known" claim is removed; metric name corrected and enablement requirement added, A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 1):**
  - **These two metrics are opt-in `EventCount`-category metrics and emit nothing unless explicitly enabled — stated here before the metrics themselves, since Round 3's text cited them as if they were always-on:** `OnFailureDestinationDeliveredEventCount` and `DroppedEventCount` both belong to the event-source mapping's `EventCount` metrics category (`docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/monitoring-metrics-types.html`), which AWS does not emit by default. The event-source mapping's own `MetricsConfig` property must explicitly enable this category — `MetricsConfig: { Metrics: ["EventCount"] }` on the `AWS::Lambda::EventSourceMapping` resource itself (§22.10, §22.14) — or neither metric exists to alarm on, silently defeating this plane's entire observability story while appearing correctly designed on paper. This enablement is a required, explicit configuration line, not an assumed default.
  - **`OnFailureDestinationDeliveredEventCount`** — the correct, sole, official AWS metric name for this signal; **there is no supported unprefixed alias** (Round 3's own text elsewhere cited an unprefixed `DestinationDeliveredEventCount` as if it were an equivalent or alternate name — this is corrected: that unprefixed name is not a real AWS metric name and is removed everywhere it appeared in this document and the companion ADR). An event-source-mapping-level metric confirming a batch was successfully delivered to the configured `OnFailure` destination (the recovery bucket). This is a **positive-path signal**: it confirms plane (iii)'s recovery mechanism itself worked as designed for a given failed batch, useful for correlating "a batch failed and was recoverably captured" rather than signaling a problem on its own.
  - **`DroppedEventCount`** — an event-source-mapping-level metric counting events dropped **without** either successful processing **or** successful delivery to the `OnFailure` destination. This is the actual **silent-loss signal** this design most needs to alarm on: a nonzero `DroppedEventCount` means a batch was lost by neither being processed nor recoverably captured — a dedicated CloudWatch alarm on `DroppedEventCount > 0` is the named, plane-specific (and, per its definition, effectively plane-(iv)-adjacent, see below) signal.
  - **Lambda's `DestinationDeliveryFailures` metric, same name as plane (ii)'s but a distinct metric instance, precision on the distinction stated honestly:** Lambda also emits `DestinationDeliveryFailures` (dimensioned by `FunctionName`) when it fails to deliver to an event-source mapping's own `OnFailure` destination — the identical metric *name* plane (ii) uses for async-invoke destination-delivery failure, but semantically a distinct failure mode (event-source-mapping destination delivery, not async-invocation destination delivery) for the same function. **Whether AWS exposes an additional CloudWatch dimension that structurally distinguishes an async-invoke-destination failure from an event-source-mapping-destination failure for the same `FunctionName`, this correction could not confirm** — stated honestly, per this document's established convention, rather than asserted; this is flagged explicitly for staging-time confirmation. In the interim, this specific ambiguity is operationally mitigated by `DroppedEventCount` above, which is unambiguously event-source-mapping-scoped and does not share this cross-plane dimension question.
- **(iv) Failure while writing to the failure destination itself.** For planes (i)/(ii), this is `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ` unavailable, throttled, or otherwise rejecting the `SendMessage` call — now covered by `InvocationsFailedToBeSentToDlq` (plane i) and `DestinationDeliveryFailures` (plane ii) above, both newly named in this correction. For plane (iii), this is the DynamoDB event-source mapping's own attempt to write to the recovery bucket (§22.9.1) failing — now covered by `DroppedEventCount` above (an event that is neither processed nor delivered to the `OnFailure` destination is, by that metric's own definition, exactly this failure mode) and, per the honest caveat above, potentially also reflected in the shared-name `DestinationDeliveryFailures` metric pending the dimension-distinguishability confirmation. This plane has no further fallback destination in this design for any of the three — if the failure destination itself cannot accept the message/object, the underlying AWS mechanism's own documented behavior applies. **Every plane now has a named, alarmable metric — no plane is left with only "rely on log inspection" as its observability story**, satisfying ADR Non-Negotiable Invariant 44 as originally intended; direct CloudWatch Logs inspection remains available as a secondary diagnostic tool, not the primary signal, for all four planes.

A design or future implementation that configures only `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ`'s existence and asserts "the DLQ handles failures" without separately wiring (i), (ii), and (iii) — including (iii)'s now-distinct routing to the recovery bucket rather than the DLQ, and without alarming on the named metrics above — does not satisfy this invariant, and neither does a design that configures all four planes without at least one automated, named observability signal per plane, per Non-Negotiable Invariant 44.

### 22.9 Payload-Preserving DynamoDB Recovery (ADR Decision 13, Invariant 45)

**The defect, precisely:** a DynamoDB Streams event-source mapping's `DestinationConfig.OnFailure` invocation record, for a batch that exhausts its retry budget, carries invocation metadata — request ID, DynamoDB batch information (shard ID, start/end sequence number, batch size, batch size in bytes), and response context. Native DynamoDB Streams (the `StreamViewType: NEW_AND_OLD_IMAGES` configuration this design already uses, `dynamodb.yml:43-44`) retains records for a fixed window before they age out of the stream; if a failed batch were referenced only by shard/sequence-number metadata, that metadata alone could not recover the batch's actual disposal data once the stream ages the underlying records out — the pointer would become a pointer to data that no longer exists anywhere the recorder can read it from.

**Corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 2, items 1/2): the failure-destination payload question is resolved differently than Round 1 assumed.** Round 1 characterized the `OnFailure` destination payload as metadata-only in all cases and, on that basis, adopted an application-level pre-processing capture (superseded — see §22.9.1's revision history below). This characterization is corrected: **AWS supports an S3 `OnFailure` destination for a DynamoDB Streams event-source mapping which — unlike an SQS or SNS destination — carries the complete failed invocation payload, including the original stream records.** This is stated here as this correction's design basis, a real, current AWS capability, and flagged, consistent with §22.5's own standard, for reconfirmation against AWS's then-current documentation at implementation time rather than treated as settled by this repository-scoped investigation. Because this native mechanism already supplies what Round 1's application-level capture was built to provide, this correction adopts it directly rather than duplicating its own equivalent — see §22.9.1.

#### 22.9.1 Selected Mechanism — Native S3 `OnFailure` Destination on a Dedicated Recovery Bucket (A1.4a.0 Round 2, items 1/2 — supersedes Round 1's pre-processing capture)

**Round 1's mechanism, and why it is rejected outright (item 1):** Round 1 adopted an unconditional, per-invocation, application-level capture of every DynamoDB Streams batch to a new prefix (`retention-recovery/dynamodb-streams-batches/`) on `RawResultsBucket`, performed as the first action of every invocation regardless of whether that invocation would go on to fail. Product Strategy rejected this outright, not as a refinement opportunity: it copied successful and entirely irrelevant `INSERT`/`MODIFY`/`REMOVE` traffic on every single invocation, creating a secondary, duplicate history of `MetadataTable`'s own content outside that content's existing, purpose-built custody boundary; it wrote a new S3 object on every retry of the same underlying batch, multiplying storage and introducing its own lifecycle/legal-hold ambiguity for content that was never evidence in the first place; and — the most serious defect — it still admitted payload loss in the narrow case where the capture write itself repeatedly failed, meaning the mechanism built specifically to guarantee recoverability could itself be the single point of failure that defeats recoverability. This mechanism, and the `retention-recovery/dynamodb-streams-batches/` prefix on `RawResultsBucket` it proposed, is removed from this design entirely — no trace of it survives in the corrected mechanism below beyond this historical record.

**Corrected mechanism (item 2):** the DynamoDB Streams event-source mapping's own native `DestinationConfig.OnFailure` is configured to point at a **dedicated, private S3 recovery bucket** — never `RawResultsBucket` — created specifically for this purpose. When a batch exhausts its retry budget (§22.10), AWS's own event-source-mapping infrastructure writes the complete, unmodified failed batch — every record's `eventID`, `eventName`, `userIdentity`, and full `OLD_IMAGE`/`NEW_IMAGE` — directly to this bucket, with no application code in the recorder's own invocation path ever executing a capture write. This is a fully AWS-managed delivery, not an application-level pattern this design must implement and can get wrong; the recorder's only remaining responsibility on this plane is normal per-record processing plus §22.10's batch/retry configuration.

**Same-account destination restriction, stated as an AWS constraint (item 2):** a DynamoDB event-source-mapping S3 `OnFailure` destination must be in the **same AWS account** as the event source mapping itself — cross-account S3 destinations are not a supported configuration for this mechanism. This constrains the recovery bucket to be provisioned in the same account as `MetadataTable` and `evidenceDisposalRecorder`, which this design already assumes throughout (no cross-account architecture exists anywhere else in this workstream), so this constraint does not require any design change beyond stating it explicitly.

#### 22.9.2 Recovery Bucket — Security, Lifecycle, Versioning, Access, Notification, and Recursion Isolation (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 2)

The dedicated recovery bucket (working name: `EvidenceDisposalRecoveryBucket`, a new `infra/resources/` resource, §22.14) is specified as follows, mirroring `RawResultsBucket`'s own existing security posture (`infra/resources/s3.yml`) as a starting precedent where applicable, and diverging where the recovery bucket's own narrower purpose and access/retention boundary (below) requires a different answer.

**Recovery-artifact classification, corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 12):** Round 2's text characterized this bucket's content with language such as "operational recovery data about governed records, not governed evidence in its own right" and "content that was never evidence in the first place" (§22.9.1's Round 1 rejection paragraph, retained above as historical record). **This materially understates what the content actually contains and is corrected:** the native `OnFailure`-destination object may contain a **complete copy of governed DynamoDB records' full content** — `OLD_IMAGE`/`NEW_IMAGE`, i.e., the actual field values of governed Category 1/2 metadata records (§18.1), not merely identifiers or pointers. This is real, compliance-relevant, governed payload data — copied from `MetadataTable`, precisely the same content `DisposalRecord`/`LegalHold` mechanisms exist to govern — **not** raw evidence file content from `RawResultsBucket` (that distinction is precise and retained: this bucket never receives S3 evidence-object content, only DynamoDB Streams record content), but it is not "never evidence" in any sense that implies unimportant or non-sensitive.

The corrected classification: this is a **restricted, purpose-limited operational recovery artifact that contains governed payload data**, with the following boundaries stated explicitly rather than left to an implied non-sensitivity claim:

- **Confidentiality boundary (who may access it) — reconfirmed from the IAM design, corrected for the redrive-credential model (§22.11, A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 6):** write access belongs exclusively to the DynamoDB event-source mapping's own AWS-managed delivery mechanism operating under `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole`'s credentials (`s3:PutObject` only, never `s3:GetObject`); read access belongs exclusively to the `rcp retention disposal-recorder redrive` command's own operator-CLI credential resolution (`s3:GetObject` only, held by the operator's own pre-existing AWS identity, per §22.9.3/§22.11/ADR Invariant 51) — never `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole`, and never a new IAM resource this correction provisions. No other principal or credential source in this design is granted any access to this bucket's content.
