   - **(a) ARN-region agreement (retained from Round 6):** the `region` segments extracted from `disposal_recorder_function_arn` and `metadata_table_stream_arn` must equal each other — catches the two-ARNs-disagree-with-each-other defect (e.g. a `staging` function ARN paired with a `prod` stream ARN), independent of whether either matches the configured stage.
   - **(b) `disposal_recorder_function_arn`'s region must equal `StageConfig.region`** — catches a function ARN pointing at a region other than the one the operator's own stage configuration declares, even if it happens to agree with the stream ARN's own (equally wrong) region.
   - **(c) `metadata_table_stream_arn`'s region must equal `StageConfig.region`** — the symmetric check for the stream ARN, catching the same "internally consistent but wrong-stage" failure mode from the other ARN's side.

   Checking (b) and (c) together, not merely (a), is what catches the "wrong-stage-region" case Round 6 could not: two ARNs that agree with each other (passing (a)) but both disagree with `StageConfig.region` (failing (b) and (c)) are now correctly rejected, distinct from — and never conflated with — the case where the two ARNs disagree with each other in the first place (failing (a), independent of (b)/(c)). Both resources are expected to be deployed in the same AWS account and region as this entire workstream (no cross-account or cross-region architecture exists anywhere else in this design), and now, specifically, in the same region the operator's own stage configuration declares; any of the three checks failing must fail closed rather than silently proceed with an internally inconsistent or wrong-stage configuration. **Account-ID consistency is unaffected by this correction and remains a pairwise-only check (below the corrected rule set), since no `StageConfig` field carries an authoritative account ID to bind it against** — `StageConfig`'s `REQUIRED_FIELDS` names no such field today, and introducing one is outside this round's narrow scope.

**6. Stream table-identity matching `StageConfig.audit_metadata_table`** — the `table_name` segment extracted from `metadata_table_stream_arn` must equal `StageConfig.audit_metadata_table`'s own value (the existing, already-required field naming `MetadataTable`'s actual deployed table name). This catches a stream ARN that is otherwise well-formed and same-account/same-region but points at some *other* table entirely — a configuration error the region/account check above cannot detect on its own, since a wrong-table stream ARN in the same account/region would still pass that check.

**Collection and raise — unchanged in shape from Round 5, now covering nine checks instead of two (corrected count, A1.4a.0 Round 7 — four field-structural checks; ARN-pairwise region agreement; ARN-pairwise account agreement; function-ARN-region-vs-`StageConfig.region`; stream-ARN-region-vs-`StageConfig.region`; and stream table-identity — Round 6's "six checks" count predates item 1's split of the single pairwise-region check into three independently-named checks):** every failing field or cross-field rule is collected — not raised on the first failure — into the same single `ConfigError(..., "DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR")`, naming every invalid field/rule together and instructing the operator to export the corresponding `RCP_*` environment variable(s) or correct `audit_metadata_table`, exactly mirroring `validate_scheduler_config`'s existing collect-then-raise shape and remediation-message style. Illustrative, not exhaustive:

```python
def validate_disposal_recorder_config(config: StageConfig) -> None:
    invalid: list[str] = []

    if _is_placeholder(config.disposal_recovery_bucket_name):
        invalid.append("disposal_recovery_bucket_name")
    elif not DISPOSAL_RECOVERY_BUCKET_NAME_PATTERN.fullmatch(config.disposal_recovery_bucket_name):
        invalid.append("disposal_recovery_bucket_name")

    if _is_placeholder(config.disposal_recorder_event_source_mapping_uuid):
        invalid.append("disposal_recorder_event_source_mapping_uuid")
    elif not DISPOSAL_RECORDER_EVENT_SOURCE_MAPPING_UUID_PATTERN.fullmatch(
        config.disposal_recorder_event_source_mapping_uuid
    ):
        invalid.append("disposal_recorder_event_source_mapping_uuid")

    function_arn_match = None
    if _is_placeholder(config.disposal_recorder_function_arn) or _has_placeholder_account(
        config.disposal_recorder_function_arn
    ):
        invalid.append("disposal_recorder_function_arn")
    else:
        function_arn_match = DISPOSAL_RECORDER_FUNCTION_ARN_PATTERN.fullmatch(
            config.disposal_recorder_function_arn
        )
        if function_arn_match is None:
            invalid.append("disposal_recorder_function_arn")

    stream_arn_match = None
    if _is_placeholder(config.metadata_table_stream_arn) or _has_placeholder_account(
        config.metadata_table_stream_arn
    ):
        invalid.append("metadata_table_stream_arn")
    else:
        stream_arn_match = METADATA_TABLE_STREAM_ARN_PATTERN.fullmatch(config.metadata_table_stream_arn)
        if stream_arn_match is None:
            invalid.append("metadata_table_stream_arn")

    if function_arn_match is not None and stream_arn_match is not None:
        if function_arn_match.group("region") != stream_arn_match.group("region"):
            invalid.append("disposal_recorder_function_arn/metadata_table_stream_arn (region mismatch)")
        if function_arn_match.group("account_id") != stream_arn_match.group("account_id"):
            invalid.append("disposal_recorder_function_arn/metadata_table_stream_arn (account mismatch)")

    # Corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 7, item 1: bind each ARN's own region to StageConfig.region directly —
    # two ARNs agreeing with each other is not sufficient if both disagree with the configured stage.
    if function_arn_match is not None and function_arn_match.group("region") != config.region:
        invalid.append("disposal_recorder_function_arn (region does not match StageConfig.region)")

    if stream_arn_match is not None and stream_arn_match.group("region") != config.region:
        invalid.append("metadata_table_stream_arn (region does not match StageConfig.region)")

    if stream_arn_match is not None and stream_arn_match.group("table_name") != config.audit_metadata_table:
        invalid.append("metadata_table_stream_arn (table segment does not match audit_metadata_table)")

    if invalid:
        raise ConfigError(
            "Stage disposal-recorder configuration contains placeholder, malformed, or "
            f"inconsistent deployed resources: {', '.join(invalid)}. Export "
            "RCP_DISPOSAL_RECOVERY_BUCKET_NAME, RCP_DISPOSAL_RECORDER_FUNCTION_ARN, "
            "RCP_DISPOSAL_RECORDER_EVENT_SOURCE_MAPPING_UUID, and/or RCP_METADATA_TABLE_STREAM_ARN "
            "from deployed disposal-recorder outputs, confirm both ARNs' own regions match the stage's "
            "configured region, and confirm audit_metadata_table matches the stream ARN's own table "
            "segment, before running rcp retention disposal-recorder redrive.",
            "DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR",
        )
```

This is illustrative of the required shape and check ordering, not literal implementation code this documentation-only correction authorizes writing — A1.4a implementation retains discretion over exact variable names and control flow, provided every one of the **nine checks** above is performed (count corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 8, item 5 — this trailing caveat still read "six checks" after Round 7 corrected the "Collection and raise" paragraph immediately above to "nine checks instead of two," an internal inconsistency within the same subsection; corrected here to match), every failure is collected rather than short-circuited, and the single-`ConfigError`/`DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR` contract is preserved exactly. **This nine-check count is specific to `validate_disposal_recorder_config` and must never be conflated with §22.9.3's own, separate "six numbered runtime envelope/provenance checks"** for the redrive command's recovered-object validation — a different validator, checking different things, whose own six-check count is correct and unaffected by this correction.

**Ordering — validated before any AWS call, mirroring `schedule_command`'s own existing structure exactly (confirmed by direct inspection, `operator_cli/services.py:232-236`: `stage_config.validate_scheduler_config()` is called before `AwsClientFactory(stage_config)` is constructed):** `dispatch_disposal_recorder_redrive`'s own construction path — reached from `operator_cli/main.py`'s `disposal-recorder redrive` dispatch branch (§22.14) — must call `stage_config.validate_disposal_recorder_config()` before constructing `AwsClientFactory`, and therefore before the redrive command's own `s3:GetObject` call and before any shared per-record processing (`disposal_recorder.py`) is ever invoked. This is the identical "resolve configuration before any AWS call" discipline §20.4's write-capable-generation ordering already establishes for Phase 5/6/7 (§20.4 step 3: "Fail closed... before `AwsClientFactory` construction if resolution fails") — stated directly here since this document had not previously cross-referenced §20.4 from §22. A malformed or placeholder stage configuration must short-circuit with `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR` before any AWS credential is ever exercised, exactly as `validate_scheduler_config`'s existing placement already does for `rcp audit schedule`.

**Exact modified/new files, added to §22.14's inventory (see §22.14 below for the full, reconciled entries):** `src/release_confidence_platform/config/stage_config.py` (modified — gains the four new fields, `REQUIRED_FIELDS`/`ENV_OVERRIDES` entries, `validate_disposal_recorder_config`, and, per Round 6 item 2, the four new structural regex constants — now additionally covering the region-vs-`StageConfig.region` binding, commercial-partition lock, bucket reserved-prefix/suffix rejection, and variable-precision stream-label shape, all A1.4a.0 Round 7); `config/stages/dev.json`/`staging.json`/`prod.json` (modified); `infra/resources/dynamodb.yml` (modified — reinstated); `infra/resources/evidence-disposal-recorder-outputs.yml` (**new, A1.4a.0 Round 6, item 3** — the two new Outputs above, their `Fn::GetAtt`/`Ref` logical-ID targets confirmed via the required isolated-harness two-pass render sequence per Round 7 item 5/Round 8 items 1/4 — never a direct render against the real, checked-in repository, which cannot succeed until custody-period configuration values are supplied — does not exist today); `infra/serverless.yml` (already a modified-file entry per Round 3/4/5; per Round 6 item 3, gains exactly one new `resources:` import-list line for the new fragment file above, not the two Outputs directly; **per Round 7 item 3, additionally gains one new `custom.disposalRecoveryBucketName` entry** in its existing `custom:` block, `${self:custom.resourcePrefix}-${self:provider.stage}-disposal-recovery`, mirroring `rawResultsBucketName`/`metadataTableName`'s existing entries); `tests/unit/config/test_stage_config.py` (new — no such file exists today, confirmed by direct inspection; `tests/unit/config/` today contains only `test_custody_period_config.py` and `__init__.py`; scope expanded per Round 6 item 2's structural/cross-field coverage, further expanded per Round 7's region-binding/partition/reserved-name/stream-label coverage).

**No CloudFormation-stack-discovery mechanism is introduced.** A runtime `describe_stacks`/`boto3.client("cloudformation")`-based dynamic lookup was considered as an alternative to a static, operator-populated configuration value and is explicitly rejected: it would require its own IAM permissions (a `cloudformation:DescribeStacks` grant this correction's IAM design, §22.11, does not authorize), its own failure semantics (stack mid-deployment, renamed, or operator credentials lacking stack-read access), and its own separately reviewed design — none of which this correction is authorized to introduce. This platform's existing static-configuration-plus-placeholder-validation pattern is retained exactly, extended with four new fields rather than replaced with a different paradigm.

### 22.10 Batch and Retry Contract for the DynamoDB Event-Source Mapping (ADR Decision 13, Invariant 46)

**Confirmed gap:** beyond ADR Decision 7's existing high-level statement ("a bounded `MaximumRetryAttempts`" and "`BisectBatchOnFunctionError: true`"), no part of this design specifies `StartingPosition`, `FilterCriteria`, batch size, `MaximumRecordAgeInSeconds`, `ParallelizationFactor`, whether `ReportBatchItemFailures` is used alongside bisection (and their precedence), a poison-record isolation/redrive procedure, or the EventBridge/Lambda-async retry parameters for planes (i)/(ii).

**Corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 8): every parameter below is explicitly selected, not left to an unstated default.**

- **`MetricsConfig: { Metrics: ["EventCount"] }` (new, A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 1) — explicit opt-in enablement of the event-source mapping's `EventCount` metrics category.** This is the exact `AWS::Lambda::EventSourceMapping` CloudFormation property that must be set for `OnFailureDestinationDeliveredEventCount` and `DroppedEventCount` (§22.8 plane (iii), §22.9.4) to emit at all — these are opt-in EventCount-category metrics, not always-on defaults (`docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/monitoring-metrics-types.html`); without this property, the alarms §22.8/§22.9.4 specify for plane (iii) would have no underlying metric data to alarm on, a silent observability gap this design must not leave unaddressed. **Serverless Framework syntax, flagged for confirmation:** whether Serverless Framework's own function-level `stream` event syntax exposes a first-class `metricsConfig` key for this property (a relatively recent AWS capability) is not confirmed by this repository-scoped correction; if the framework version this platform uses at implementation time does not yet support it natively, A1.4a implementation must apply `MetricsConfig` via `resources.extensions`, patching the rendered `AWS::Lambda::EventSourceMapping` logical ID's own `MetricsConfig` property directly — the CloudFormation property name and required value (`{ Metrics: ["EventCount"] }`) are fixed and not in question; only the exact Serverless-Framework-syntax path to set it is flagged for staging-time confirmation, per this document's established convention.

- **`StartingPosition`: `LATEST`, not `TRIM_HORIZON` (a new, explicit selection — Round 1 did not address this parameter at all).** `evidenceDisposalRecorder` must never silently initiate a backfill of pre-existing `MetadataTable` stream content the moment its event-source mapping is first created — this design's own scope is operational, forward-looking disposal recording, and a historical backfill of the pre-Workstream-A evidence backlog is a distinct, separately governed decision this ADR already treats as out of scope (ADR "Assumption Requiring Confirmation," and the forward-referenced, not-yet-authorized A1.4d historical-backfill subphase, if any such subphase is ever created). `TRIM_HORIZON` would risk processing old, already-superseded stream content — including `REMOVE` events for records that were TTL-disposed before this recorder ever existed — as if newly occurring, which this design was never meant to act on retroactively. `LATEST` ensures the recorder only ever sees stream activity from the point its event-source mapping is created forward.
- **`FilterCriteria`: scoped to `eventName == "REMOVE"` only, at the event-source-mapping level (a new, explicit selection).** DynamoDB Streams event-source-mapping filtering is a real, current AWS capability; the filter pattern `{ "eventName": ["REMOVE"] }` prevents `INSERT`/`MODIFY` records — the majority of ordinary write traffic against `MetadataTable` — from invoking the Lambda at all, reducing invocation volume and cost. This is defense-in-depth cost reduction only; §22.4's own handler-side `eventName == "REMOVE"` check is never removed or weakened on the assumption the mapping-level filter already handles it — the mapping-level filter is never trusted alone (§22.4).
- **Batch size: 25 (a new, explicit, justified selection — Round 1 left this as an unstated default).** Smaller than the DynamoDB Streams event-source-mapping's own default batch size, chosen to bound two things this design specifically cares about: the size of the single failed-batch object the native `OnFailure` destination writes to the recovery bucket (§22.9.1) when a batch is ultimately undeliverable, and the blast radius of `BisectBatchOnFunctionError`'s own repeated-halving behavior when a batch contains one poison record (fewer records to bisect through before isolating it). This trades a modest increase in per-record Lambda invocation overhead (more, smaller invocations rather than fewer, larger ones) for a smaller, easier-to-inspect recovery artifact and a faster poison-record isolation path — a reasoned choice, not AWS's own unstated default, and flagged for confirmation before A1.4a implementation adopts it verbatim.
- **`ParallelizationFactor`: 1 (default — a new, explicit selection with the reasoning stated even though the default is retained).** DynamoDB Streams processing is ordered strictly per shard; `ParallelizationFactor` increases per-shard concurrency at the cost of relaxing that strict ordering within a shard. No throughput requirement has been identified for this workload that would justify raising it above the default. This design states explicitly, for the benefit of any future tuning decision, that raising `ParallelizationFactor` above 1 would be **safe for this design's correctness specifically**, unlike most ordered-stream consumers: §22.2's deterministic `disposal_id` computation, together with §22.3's duplicate-conflict verification, makes reordering of records for the same underlying item within a shard harmless — two records for the same disposal event arriving out of relative order still compute the identical `disposal_id` and resolve correctly via the conditional put and duplicate-verification path, regardless of delivery order. This is stated now so a future throughput-driven tuning decision has this reasoning already available, not because a specific throughput need exists today.
- **Partial-batch-failure handling — the exact documented contract, restated precisely and simply (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 8 — precedence is new; corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 8; corrected again, A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 2, to match AWS's actual documented contract exactly, replacing Round 3's more elaborate "per-invocation, bisection re-engages later" framing):** `functionResponseTypes: ["ReportBatchItemFailures"]` and `BisectBatchOnFunctionError: true` (already specified by ADR Decision 7) are both enabled together. **The exact contract, stated as directly as AWS documents it — no invented nuance beyond this:** when both are enabled, the function's own returned lowest-failed-sequence-number **is** the checkpoint/bisection boundary directly — the returned sequence number **is** the boundary the mapping retries from for the remaining records. Bisection in this configuration is not a separate, alternate mechanism that only kicks in when the function fails to return a well-formed partial response; it is a boundary derived directly from what the function itself reports. Round 3's prior framing — describing bisection as not engaging "for that invocation" when a well-formed response exists, and only "re-engaging" on some later invocation that fails to produce one — stated a more elaborate, invocation-by-invocation alternation than AWS's documented behavior actually specifies, and is corrected to this simpler, direct statement: the function's returned sequence number is the boundary. Reference basis: `docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/services-ddb-batchfailurereporting.html`, this correction's authoritative basis for this exact restatement (not itself flagged for further softening — Product Strategy's own instruction is applied directly here, not re-hedged).
- **Bounded `MaximumRetryAttempts`: 3.** Not asserted merely by analogy to another subsystem's unrelated retry count — the workload/failure-characteristic reasoning specific to this path (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 8): the transient failures this specific write path can encounter are DynamoDB read/write throttling (on `DisposalRepository`'s own `PutItem`/`GetItem` calls, §22.3) and S3 throttling (on the recovery bucket's own delivery, or on the redrive actor's read), both of which are typically transient and resolve within a small number of retries under normal AWS throttling/backoff behavior. Failures that are *not* transient in this way — a malformed event (§22.7 category (c)/(d)), a code defect, or a genuine `disposal_id` collision (§22.7 category (f)) — are deterministic and would not be helped by a larger retry count; more retries would only delay, not prevent, their eventual routing to the failure destination. 3 is therefore a reasoned number for this path's own failure shape, not an unexamined reuse of a value chosen for a structurally different mechanism elsewhere in this design (§19.4's hold-version-race retry, §19.5.6's marker-establishment retry, which this correction no longer cites as its sole justification).
- **Bounded `MaximumRecordAgeInSeconds`:** set well within DynamoDB Streams' own fixed 24-hour record-retention window (confirmed: native DynamoDB Streams retention is fixed at 24 hours, not independently configurable, distinct from the longer, configurable retention Kinesis Data Streams for DynamoDB offers as a separate, not-adopted capability, §22.9.1) — recommended at 72,000 seconds (20 hours), leaving a margin below the stream's own 24-hour ceiling so a record is never still "eligible for retry" per this setting at a point where it has already aged out of the stream itself, which would otherwise manifest as a confusing, silent processing gap rather than a clean failure-destination delivery.
- **EventBridge retry configuration, plane (i) (a new, explicit selection — Round 1 did not address this):** `RetryPolicy.MaximumRetryAttempts: 10`, `RetryPolicy.MaximumEventAgeInSeconds: 3600` (1 hour). Reasoning specific to this plane: an EventBridge-to-Lambda delivery failure here is most plausibly caused by Lambda throttling under a burst of near-simultaneous S3 Lifecycle deletions (a single daily Lifecycle evaluation cycle can produce many "Object Deleted" events in a short window), which typically resolves within EventBridge's own retry backoff well inside an hour; capping at 10 attempts within 1 hour avoids holding a failed delivery indefinitely while still tolerating a transient Lambda concurrency-limit event during the daily Lifecycle batch window. This is a reasoned but explicitly flagged-for-confirmation judgment call, distinct in kind from the DynamoDB-path numbers above, which mirror this design's own established retry-count convention.
- **Lambda asynchronous-invoke retry configuration, plane (ii) (a new, explicit selection — Round 1 did not address this):** `RetryAttempts: 2` (the maximum AWS permits for Lambda's own asynchronous-invocation retry configuration — a 0–2 range, not independently extendable), `MaximumEventAgeInSeconds: 21600` (6 hours — also AWS's own maximum for this setting). Selecting the maximum permitted value in both cases is the correct choice here, not an arbitrary pick: AWS caps this specific mechanism's configurable range well below EventBridge's own (0–2 retries versus EventBridge's much larger range), so there is no headroom to reason about beyond "use what AWS allows," and using the maximum gives this plane the most resilience against transient invocation failure (e.g., transient throttling on the recovery-bucket-adjacent or `DisposalRepository` calls) that this configuration surface can provide. These specific numeric ranges (0–2 retries; a 60–21600 second age window) are AWS-configurable-range platform facts, flagged, per this document's convention, for confirmation at implementation time.
- **Poison-record isolation, same-batch redelivery, and same-shard blocking — corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 8; shard-blocking claim further corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 8):** Round 1's text asserted that a poison record "does not block later batches"; Round 2 corrected the redelivery scenario but retained an inaccurate implication about blocking, which is corrected here. DynamoDB Streams processing is ordered **per shard**; with `ReportBatchItemFailures` enabled, Lambda checkpoints at the **lowest failed sequence number** reported within a batch and the mapping retries from that point forward — meaning any records **after** that sequence number in the *same* batch that the function had already processed successfully **may be redelivered** on the retry, even though they were not the record that failed. This is a real, correctness-relevant redelivery scenario, not merely a retry-count detail, and it is exactly why §22.2's deterministic identity and §22.3's duplicate-conflict verification exist: a redelivered, already-successfully-recorded record recomputes the identical `disposal_id`, hits `ConditionalCheckFailedException` on the redundant `PutItem` attempt, and resolves via §22.3's read-back as a confirmed idempotent duplicate (§22.7 category (e)) — never a second write, never an error.

  **Same-shard blocking, corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 8):** with `ParallelizationFactor: 1`, processing of a given shard is **strictly sequential** — a failed checkpoint on that shard **blocks processing of later records on that same shard** until the checkpoint advances, i.e., until the failed sub-batch is either successfully retried (a subsequent invocation returns a well-formed response covering it) or exhausts its retry budget (`MaximumRetryAttempts`/`MaximumRecordAgeInSeconds`) and is routed to the failure destination. This corrects any prior implication that later records on the same shard continue processing independently while a poison record's own retry is still in progress — they do not; the shard's checkpoint position is what gates forward progress on that shard, and a stalled checkpoint stalls every subsequent record on that shard until it resolves one way or the other. **What is correctly retained from the prior text:** this blocking is scoped to the affected shard only — DynamoDB Streams' other shards (a table's stream is partitioned across multiple shards) are polled and checkpointed independently, so a poison record on one shard does not block records on a *different* shard, and once the poison record's own sub-batch is finally routed to the failure destination (exhausting its retry budget), the now-unblocked shard's checkpoint advances past it and subsequent records on that same shard resume processing normally. Once `MaximumRetryAttempts`/`MaximumRecordAgeInSeconds` is exhausted for the remaining sub-batch containing the poison record, it is delivered to the failure destination (§22.8 plane (iii), now the recovery bucket per §22.9.1) rather than retried indefinitely.
- **Redrive procedure:** defined concretely at §22.9.3 — an operator locates the failed batch via the recovery-bucket notification path (§22.9.2) or `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ`'s plane-(i)/(ii) messages as applicable, and invokes `rcp retention disposal-recorder redrive` (§22.9.3), which reads the recovery object and re-submits it through the recorder's own shared processing logic. One redrive procedure serves both this section's poison-record/partial-batch case and §22.9's full-batch-failure case, since both ultimately land the same way (a recovery-bucket object, optionally correlated with a notification message).

### 22.11 Dedicated Least-Privilege IAM (ADR Decision 13, Invariant 47)

**Confirmed defect:** §12's existing IAM bullets are explicitly self-described as "logical, not currently IAM-enforced," and characterize `evidenceDisposalRecorder` only as "the strongest candidate in this design for a dedicated per-function role... if added to the shared role instead, it inherits every other action already granted there." Product Strategy has now decided this, not merely identified it as a candidate: `evidenceDisposalRecorder` receives a dedicated role. This section describes that role's required shape — as documentation only; no `infra/resources/` file is created by this correction.

**A new, not-yet-created `infra/resources/evidence-disposal-recorder-iam.yml`**, following the exact `AuditFinalizationLambdaRole`/`AuditAggregationLambdaRole` shape already established in `infra/resources/phase4-aggregation-iam.yml` (an `AWS::IAM::Role` with a `lambda.amazonaws.com` `AssumeRolePolicyDocument` and one or more scoped `PolicyDocument` statements — not an expansion of the shared `provider.iam.role.statements` block), defines the **live recorder's own execution role** (`EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole`):

1. **CloudWatch Logs**, scoped to this function's own log group only: `logs:CreateLogGroup`/`CreateLogStream`/`PutLogEvents`, `Resource: arn:aws:logs:${self:provider.region}:*:log-group:/aws/lambda/${self:service}-${self:provider.stage}-evidenceDisposalRecorder:*` — mirroring `AuditFinalizationLambdaRole`'s identical log-group-scoping pattern (`phase4-aggregation-iam.yml:19-25`).
2. **DynamoDB Streams read, scoped to `MetadataTable`'s stream ARN — three actions in this statement (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 10):** `dynamodb:GetRecords`/`GetShardIterator`/`DescribeStream`, `Resource` referencing the stream ARN via a direct, same-stack `Fn::GetAtt: [MetadataTable, StreamArn]` reference (**corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 10(a) — this IAM statement's own `Resource` continues to resolve via this same-stack reference, unchanged; it never consumes a CloudFormation Output**; §22.4/§22.14 above). **Note (A1.4a.0 Round 5, item 1) — this statement's own same-stack resolution is distinct from, and unaffected by, the `MetadataTableStreamArn` Output now reinstated in `infra/resources/dynamodb.yml` for a different consumer (the operator CLI's own `StageConfig.metadata_table_stream_arn` field, §22.9.5): this role's own `PolicyDocument` never reads that Output either — it is same-stack IAM-to-resource scoping, using the identical `Fn::GetAtt` intrinsic Round 3 already established here, not a cross-stack or external reference of any kind.** Never the bare table ARN for these three actions, since they are Streams-specific operations.

   **`dynamodb:ListStreams` is restored as its own, separately scoped statement (corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 4 — reverses Round 2's/Round 3's omission, which is wrong).** Round 2 omitted this action, reasoning that the function's own runtime stream polling does not require it. Product Strategy corrects this: AWS's own documented, managed IAM policy for a Lambda DynamoDB-Streams execution role — the `AWSLambdaDynamoDBExecutionRole` managed policy (`docs.aws.amazon.com/aws-managed-policy/latest/reference/AWSLambdaDynamoDBExecutionRole.html`) and AWS's own DynamoDB-Streams-event-source-mapping documentation (`docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/services-dynamodb-eventsourcemapping.html`) — grant all **four** actions together (`DescribeStream`, `GetRecords`, `GetShardIterator`, **and** `ListStreams`) as the documented, expected permission set for this exact use case; omitting a documented, expected runtime permission merely because it cannot be scoped as tightly as its siblings is not a defensible narrowing. `ListStreams` is restored to `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole`, but the technical fact Round 2/Round 3 correctly identified is retained honestly, not discarded: `ListStreams` genuinely cannot be scoped to a single stream ARN the way `GetRecords`/`GetShardIterator`/`DescribeStream` can — it is an account/table-level discovery action (enumerating which streams exist for a table), not a stream-specific read. It is therefore granted as its **own, separate policy statement**, `Resource: "*"` (the achievable scope for this action — AWS does not support a `dynamodb:ListStreams` resource-level restriction to a single stream or table ARN; a table-level `Resource` restriction is not offered by this action either, confirmed by direct review of AWS's own `ListStreams` API/IAM reference, which documents it as requiring `Resource: "*"`), never folded into the ARN-scoped statement above as if it shared that statement's tighter scope. This restores a documented, expected permission rather than falsely narrowing this role below AWS's own reference shape, while stating precisely, rather than hiding, why this one action's scope is broader than its three siblings'.
3. **`DisposalRecord` `PutItem` and `GetItem`**, scoped to `MetadataTable`'s table ARN (not the stream ARN) — `PutItem` for §22.2's disposal write, `GetItem` for §22.3's duplicate-conflict read-back. No `Query`, `UpdateItem`, or `DeleteItem` action is granted — the recorder never queries, updates, or deletes anything.
4. **Failure-destination `sqs:SendMessage`**, scoped to `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ`'s ARN — **planes (i)/(ii) only (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 2)** — used by the Lambda's own async-invoke `DestinationConfig.OnFailure` (§22.8 plane (ii)), if that plane targets this queue directly rather than solely through EventBridge's own delivery mechanism. This is never used for the DynamoDB Streams plane (iii) any longer — that plane's `OnFailure` destination is the recovery bucket (below), not this queue.
5. **The recovery bucket's write permission, with the responsible IAM principal researched and stated precisely (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 10 — corrects Round 1's undifferentiated "payload-preservation permission" grant; corrected further, A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 1 — `s3:PutObject` alone is insufficient):** `s3:PutObject` **and `s3:ListBucket`** (corrected — see below), scoped to the dedicated recovery bucket (§22.9.1/§22.9.2), granted to **this execution role**. This requires stating precisely which mechanism actually performs the write, since the answer determines which IAM principal needs the grant: for a DynamoDB (or Kinesis) Streams event-source mapping's `OnFailure` destination, AWS's own documented behavior is that the destination write is performed **using the function's own execution role's credentials** — not a separate, AWS-internal service-linked role, and not the same resource-based-permission mechanism EventBridge invocation uses (item below). This means the write is AWS-managed (the recorder's own application code never calls `PutObject` for this path — §22.9.1), but the **credentials** used to perform it are this execution role's own, so the grant belongs here. This is stated as this correction's best-reasoned understanding of current AWS behavior for stream event-source-mapping `OnFailure` destinations, flagged for staging-time confirmation per this document's established convention, not treated as repository-verified. **`s3:GetObject` is explicitly never granted on the recovery bucket to this role, under any justification** — the corrected rule from Round 1's undifferentiated grant (which bundled `PutObject` and `GetObject` together "for redrive," a rationale Product Strategy rejected): the live recorder needs only the ability to fail, never the ability to read back what happens after it fails. Recovery-object read access belongs exclusively to the `rcp retention disposal-recorder redrive` command's own operator-CLI credential resolution (below, corrected A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 6) — never this execution role. **Explicit non-grant retained:** `PutObject`/`ListBucket` never expand into `GetObject`, `DeleteObject`, or any other evidence-content-shaped action.
6. **`s3:ListBucket` IS required and IS granted on the recovery bucket — corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 1; reverses Round 2's omission, which is wrong).** Round 2 omitted this grant, reasoning that a single, deterministic `PutObject` per failed batch does not require enumerating the bucket's contents. This is corrected: per AWS's own documented IAM requirements for a DynamoDB event-source-mapping S3 failure destination (`docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/services-dynamodb-errors.html`), the execution role requires **both** `s3:PutObject` scoped to the recovery-bucket object ARN (`arn:aws:s3:::{recovery-bucket-name}/*`) **and** `s3:ListBucket` scoped to the recovery-bucket ARN itself (`arn:aws:s3:::{recovery-bucket-name}`) — this is AWS's documented requirement for this specific destination mechanism, not a speculative broadening of this role's own scope, and not in tension with the existing, unchanged prohibition on `s3:GetObject` (item 5 above) — `PutObject`/`ListBucket` are the write-and-locate actions the delivery mechanism itself needs; `GetObject` (content read) remains never granted to this role under any justification.

   **`s3:ResourceAccount` condition, added (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 1 — new same-account protection, stated with its reason, not left as an unexplained detail; field name corrected A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 5 — Round 3's own text here originally, and incorrectly, named this condition key `aws:ResourceAccount`):** the `s3:PutObject`/`s3:ListBucket` grant above additionally carries a `Condition: { StringEquals: { "s3:ResourceAccount": "${aws:accountId}" } }`. **The condition key is `s3:ResourceAccount`, not `aws:ResourceAccount`** — the valid, AWS-documented condition key for this exact purpose (protecting an S3-destination grant against a bucket being deleted and recreated under a different account) is the service-specific `s3:ResourceAccount` key, not a generic `aws:`-prefixed key; there is no supported `aws:ResourceAccount` alias, and no other occurrence of this condition key anywhere in this document or the companion ADR may use the `aws:`-prefixed spelling (reference basis: `docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/services-dynamodb-errors.html`). **The reason for the condition, stated explicitly:** this is AWS's own documented pattern for protecting against a bucket being deleted and later recreated — by a *different* AWS account — reusing the identical bucket name; without a same-account condition, an IAM policy that grants access purely by bucket *name* (an ARN pattern) could, in that narrow but real scenario, silently grant access to a same-named bucket now owned by an unrelated account. The condition ties the grant to the bucket's own AWS account identity, not merely its name, closing that gap. This is stated here as AWS's own documented recommendation for this destination-configuration pattern specifically (§22.9.1's same-account destination-restriction constraint is a related but distinct AWS mechanism-level restriction; this condition is this role's own IAM-policy-level defense reinforcing it), flagged for staging-time confirmation per this document's established convention.

**No second, separate IAM principal for the redrive actor — corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 6; reverses Round 2/Round 3's design, which Product Strategy found self-contradictory).** Round 2/Round 3 described the `rcp retention disposal-recorder redrive` command (§22.9.3) as operating under "its own IAM principal, distinct from `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole`," while simultaneously stating (§22.9.3) that redrive "uses the existing operator-CLI credential-resolution boundary, the same mechanism every other `rcp` command uses, with no new per-command role." These two statements are incompatible as written: a genuinely new IAM principal/role is itself a broader security design — its own trust policy, its own CLI `AssumeRole` flow — that this correction never actually designed, only asserted alongside the contradictory "no new role" claim. **Product Strategy's explicit resolution, applied here: the redrive command operates under the operator's own existing, already-configured AWS credentials — the exact same credential-resolution mechanism every other `rcp` command in this platform already uses (the CLI's own `AwsClientFactory`/stage-config-driven credential resolution), not a per-command role, and not a second IAM principal this correction provisions.** Those operator credentials must, as an **operational/deployment-time prerequisite** (not a new IAM resource this correction creates), hold the narrowly-scoped `s3:GetObject` permission on the recovery bucket and the `DisposalRecord` `GetItem`/`PutItem` permissions on `MetadataTable` needed to exercise the shared processing logic (§22.9.3) — this document states the narrow permissions the operator's own credentials must hold; it does not define, provision, or otherwise scope what that operator identity's own broader permissions boundary looks like, which is outside this design's own resource inventory. **These operator credentials remain structurally separate from `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole`** — never the recorder Lambda's own execution role, and never merged with it — but that separation is expressed as "two different credential sources" (the recorder's own execution role, versus whatever IAM user/role/SSO identity the operator's CLI session already resolves to), not as "two IAM resources this correction provisions." The component that can cause a recovery object to exist (the live recorder's execution role, via the AWS-managed `OnFailure` write) remains structurally never the same component that can read one back (the operator's own pre-existing credentials) — the separation Round 2/Round 3 intended is preserved; only how it is expressed changes.

**Distinct, resource-based mechanisms — not IAM role statements, stated precisely so the two are never conflated:**

- **EventBridge's permission to invoke this Lambda** is an `AWS::Lambda::Permission` resource (`Action: lambda:InvokeFunction`, `Principal: events.amazonaws.com`, `SourceArn` scoped to the specific EventBridge rule's own ARN) — a resource-based policy attached to the Lambda function itself, structurally identical in kind to `AuditAggregationInvokePermissionFromFinalization` (`phase4-aggregation-iam.yml:109-118`), which already establishes this exact pattern (a `Principal` other than another IAM role, scoped via `SourceArn`) for `auditFinalization`'s invocation of `auditAggregation`. This is not a statement inside `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole`'s own `PolicyDocument` — it is a separate resource governing who may *call* the function, not what the function itself may *do*.
- **An EventBridge-rule-scoped DLQ resource policy**, required only if the EventBridge rule's own target-level `DeadLetterConfig` (§22.8 plane (i)) is used: the target `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ` needs a resource policy (an `AWS::SQS::QueuePolicy`, or an inline `Policy` on the queue) granting `sqs:SendMessage` to `events.amazonaws.com`, scoped by `aws:SourceArn` to the specific rule's own ARN and by account — never a blanket policy admitting any EventBridge rule in the account. **This same resource-policy discipline now extends to the recovery bucket too (A1.4a.0 Round 2, item 10):** the recovery-bucket resource policy (§22.9.2's secure-transport deny statement) is a bucket-level resource policy, kept structurally separate from `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole`'s own `PolicyDocument`, exactly as this discipline already applies to the DLQ.

**Explicit non-grant, stated as a rule this role must never violate, not merely an omission:** `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole` must never be granted `s3:GetObject`, `s3:PutObject`, `s3:DeleteObject`, or any other evidence-content action on `raw-results/*`, `intelligence/*`, `reports/*`, or `integrity/*` — under any justification, including a future convenience refactor. This Lambda's entire input is DynamoDB Streams records and S3 event *notifications* (metadata about a deletion that already occurred, carrying no object content), plus its own §22.9 recovery-bucket write; it has no legitimate reason to ever read or write evidence content itself, and (per this correction) no legitimate reason to read its own failure-destination content either.

### 22.12 Parser/Validation Boundary Correction (ADR Decision 13, Invariant 48)

**The A1.4a read-only investigation's own prior finding, corrected before being adopted into this document:** an early framing of this investigation proposed that `audit_lifecycle/finalization_gate.py::_extract_run_id_from_s3_key` (`finalization_gate.py:272-281`) is straightforwardly unsafe for the disposal-recorder boundary because it "fails open" — returning `""` on any key with fewer than four `/`-delimited segments rather than raising. Direct re-read of the function and its two confirmed call sites (`finalization_gate.py:200-201` and `:224-225`, both following the identical pattern: `s3_run_id = _extract_run_id_from_s3_key(s3_key); matching_runs = all_run_id_map.get(s3_run_id, [])`) shows this framing overstates the case: the caller's own `dict.get(s3_run_id, [])` treats an empty-string `s3_run_id` as "no matching run," which — at both call sites — correctly produces a `CHECK_EVERY_EVIDENCE_MAPS_TO_ONE_RUN` failure rather than silently accepting a malformed key. **The function's own empty-string-on-malformed-input return is not, by itself, proof that every caller of it fails open** — a caller can, and this one does, validate and reject the empty-string result.

**Why this function is still the wrong tool for the disposal-recorder boundary, precisely stated rather than left as a restated overclaim:** `_extract_run_id_from_s3_key` is a **positional, index-based parser** (`parts[3]` after a plain `/`-split) purpose-built for one specific, already-validated key shape (`raw-results/{client_id}/{audit_id}/{run_id}/results.json`) within a context (`finalization_gate.py`'s consistency checks) where a second, independent validation step downstream is what actually enforces correctness — the parser itself has no opinion about whether its output is meaningful, only about whether the split produced enough segments to index into. `evidenceDisposalRecorder` needs to derive `client_id`, `audit_id`, and `evidence_class` from **any** of four structurally different evidence-class key shapes (`raw-results/{client_id}/{audit_id}/{run_id}/results.json`; `intelligence/{client_id}/{audit_id}/{audit_execution_id}/...`; `reports/{client_id}/{audit_id}/.../{report_job_id}/artifact.json`; `integrity/{client_id}/{audit_id}/.../{certjob_id}/artifact.json`) — a single positional parser assuming one fixed shape cannot correctly serve all four, and this boundary has no downstream caller performing the kind of independent validation `finalization_gate.py`'s caller happens to perform. Reusing `_extract_run_id_from_s3_key` here would silently rely on a validation discipline this boundary does not have, for a function that was never designed to be relied on for fail-closed behavior on its own.

**Correction:** `evidenceDisposalRecorder` implements its own dedicated key-parsing and validation logic — prefix-dispatched (per §22.6's four-prefix restriction, checked *before* any positional parsing is attempted), validating segment count and non-emptiness for every extracted component, and **raising**, not returning a sentinel, on any malformed or unexpected shape. A malformed key that passes §22.6's prefix check but fails this dedicated parser's own validation is classified as a **malformed target-provenance event** (§22.7 category (c)), never silently treated as if `client_id`/`audit_id` were empty strings and processing continued. This is structurally the same "own the parsing, fail closed by construction, do not depend on a downstream caller's separate discipline" principle `_assert_disposal_sk()`/`_assert_retention_sk()`/`_assert_custody_field_only_update()`/`_assert_marker_key()` already establish for every other write-capable component in this design (§5.2, §19.5.9) — applied here to a read/parse boundary rather than a write boundary, but the same discipline.

### 22.13 Future QA Acceptance Matrix (A1.4a Implementation Subphase)

Documented here as required future test coverage; no test file is created by this correction. Expected ownership: a new `tests/unit/evidence_retention/test_disposal_recorder.py` (no such file exists today, confirmed), mirroring the naming convention already established for this module (`test_hold_transitions.py`, `test_retention_service.py`); a new `tests/unit/evidence_retention/test_disposal_recorder_redrive.py` for the redrive command (§22.9.3) specifically, mirroring `commands.py`'s existing per-command-group test convention.

1. **Deterministic-ID retry, corrected test assertions (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 6 — Round 2's item 1 test description asserted an incorrect equivalence and is corrected below):** the same DynamoDB TTL `REMOVE` event, and separately the same S3 `Object Deleted` event, processed twice — assert an identical `disposal_id` both times and exactly one persisted `DisposalRecord` (§22.2). Three distinct assertions replace Round 2's single, incorrectly-stated one:
   - **(a) Identical raw event redelivery is deterministic:** redelivering the *same* underlying event — bit-for-bit identical raw `detail.object.key` bytes, whatever encoding those bytes happen to be in — produces the identical `disposal_id` both times. This is the correct, valid test of §22.2's determinism guarantee and is retained.
   - **(b) Differently-encoded representations of a "same" key must NOT be asserted to collide, and this is corrected as a genuine test-description defect, not a refinement:** Round 2's text asserted that "a redelivery test with a key containing a percent-encodable character should still produce the same `disposal_id` both times regardless of how it is encoded, since the raw bytes received are what is hashed either way" — this is wrong and is removed. §22.2 hashes the raw EventBridge key exactly as received (correct, unchanged); two *differently-encoded* raw byte strings representing what a human might consider "the same" key (e.g., one containing a literal space, one containing `%20`) are, precisely because the raw bytes differ, **different hash inputs** and are expected and required to produce **different** `disposal_id` values — this is correct behavior per §22.2's own raw-key-hashing design, not a bug this test suite should guard against. No test in this matrix may assert that two differently-encoded key representations produce the same `disposal_id`.
   - **(c) Key-parsing/decoding is tested entirely separately from identity-hash determinism, never conflated:** the decoding/parsing logic §22.12's dedicated parser uses to extract `client_id`/`audit_id`/`evidence_class` for business-identity purposes has its own, independent test coverage, asserting correct extraction across encoded and unencoded key variants — this coverage must never be merged into, or used to justify an assertion within, the identity-hash determinism test in (a)/(b) above. The two concerns (deterministic hashing of raw bytes; correct parsing of human-meaningful identity fields) are tested as structurally separate test cases.
2. **Unequal-collision:** two different source-identity inputs contrived (in test fixtures only) to produce the same `disposal_id` — assert the integrity-failure disposition (§22.7 category (f)) is reached, not a silent duplicate-acceptance (§22.7 category (e)), and assert the persisted source-identity fields (§7.3, ADR Invariant 49) are what the mismatch is actually detected against, not only the disposal facts (§22.3).
3. **DynamoDB provenance — every branch, positive and negative, with the corrected disposition boundary (item 5):** wrong stream identity (assert **unknown/incompatible**, category (d) — not valid-but-irrelevant); `eventName != REMOVE` (assert valid-but-irrelevant, category (b)); non-service-origin `userIdentity` (assert valid-but-irrelevant, category (b)); missing `OLD_IMAGE`; `OLD_IMAGE` missing/invalid `evidence_class`; and the full positive case — assert each resolves to the disposition §22.4 specifies (§22.4).
4. **Real S3 EventBridge fixtures, corrected field name and added coverage (A1.4a.0 Round 3, items 3/4):** test fixtures built from §22.5's actual `Object Deleted` envelope shape (not the superseded `s3:LifecycleExpiration:*` naming), including `account`, `detail["event-version"]` (corrected field name — not `detail.version`), and `detail.requester` fields — with dedicated positive/negative cases for the MAJOR/MINOR semver-style compatibility rule (a higher-minor-version fixture accepted; a different-major-version and a lower-minor-version fixture both rejected as unknown/incompatible) and for the combined `reason`+`requester` provenance rule (a `reason == "Lifecycle Expiration"` fixture with a non-`"s3.amazonaws.com"` or missing `requester` rejected as unknown/incompatible, not recorded) — including a case explicitly noting the fixture must be reconciled against a real staging-environment capture before this test is trusted as ground truth (§22.5.1's confirmation requirement) (§22.5).
5. **Permanently-deleted versus delete-marker, and the corrected disposition boundary (item 3, item 5):** `deletion-type == "Permanently Deleted"` produces a `DisposalRecord`; `deletion-type == "Delete Marker Created"` resolves to valid-but-irrelevant (category (b)); an unrecognized `deletion-type` value fails closed as unknown/incompatible (category (d)), not valid-but-irrelevant (§22.5.1).
6. **Marker-prefix and unknown-prefix exclusion, now with different dispositions (item 5 — corrects Round 1's test description, which grouped both as the same outcome):** a `retention-markers/` disposal event resolves to valid-but-irrelevant (category (b)); an event under an unrecognized prefix resolves to **unknown/incompatible** (category (d)) — assert these two are distinguishable dispositions, not the same outcome reached two different ways (§22.6).
7. **EventBridge two-layer filtering (item 4 — new):** an event that would fail the rule-level `EventPattern` (§22.5.2) if actually delivered is nonetheless independently rejected by the handler's own defense-in-depth validation (§22.5.3) when constructed directly as a test fixture bypassing the rule — proving the handler never assumes the rule-level filter already did its job.
8. **Malformed-event failure:** an event matching a recordable shape but with unparseable fields, or missing `version-id`, resolves to malformed target-provenance (§22.7 category (c)), routed for retry/recovery, not silently dropped as valid-but-irrelevant (§22.7).
9. **Failure-plane wiring, one test per plane, with plane (iii) corrected (item 2):** (i) EventBridge rule `DeadLetterConfig` fires on simulated delivery failure, landing on `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ`; (ii) the Lambda's own async-invoke `DestinationConfig.OnFailure` fires on simulated S3-path invocation failure, landing on `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ`; (iii) the DynamoDB event-source mapping's `DestinationConfig.OnFailure` fires on simulated Streams-path retry exhaustion, landing on the **recovery bucket**, never `evidenceDisposalRecorderDLQ` — each asserted independently, not inferred from one shared mock, and (iii) specifically asserted to land on the correct destination type (§22.8). **A fifth, distinct plane, added (new, A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 7):** the recovery bucket's own `Object Created` notification rule (`EvidenceDisposalRecoveryBucketObjectCreatedRule`, §22.9.2) is its own failure plane, independent of (i)–(iii) — a simulated EventBridge delivery failure for this specific rule must be asserted to fire `EvidenceDisposalRecoveryNotificationFailedInvocationsAlarm` (§22.9.2), distinct from the plane-(i) alarm, proving this notification path's own delivery failure is observable and not merely inferred from the absence of a DLQ message.
10. **Recovery-bucket notification-rule wiring, consistently scoped (new, A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 7):** an infra-configuration-level assertion (§22.13 item 12's test category, below) confirming `EvidenceDisposalRecoveryBucketObjectCreatedRule`'s own `RetryPolicy` (`MaximumRetryAttempts: 10`, `MaximumEventAgeInSeconds: 3600`), its inclusion as one of exactly two rule ARNs authorized in `evidence-retention-dlq.yml`'s `QueuePolicy` (§22.11/§22.14), and `EvidenceDisposalRecoveryNotificationFailedInvocationsAlarm`'s own scoping to this rule's `RuleName` are all mutually consistent — not merely each individually present.
11. **Native payload-preserving destination and redrive (item 1/2/11 — replaces Round 1's "payload-preserving redrive" test, which tested the now-removed application-level capture):** a simulated failed DynamoDB Streams batch, delivered via the native `OnFailure` mechanism to the recovery bucket, is read by a simulated `rcp retention disposal-recorder redrive` invocation and successfully reprocessed, producing the correct `DisposalRecord`(s) via the same shared processing function the live handler uses; a case where some records in the redriven batch were already successfully recorded before the original failure asserts §22.3's duplicate-verification path resolves them as confirmed duplicates, not re-writes (§22.9, §22.9.3).
12. **Recovery-bucket security configuration (item 2, item 9 — new):** an infra-configuration-level test (mirroring `tests/unit/test_infra_configuration.py`'s existing convention of asserting on `infra/resources/*.yml`'s raw content, confirmed to already exist in this repository) asserting the recovery bucket's `PublicAccessBlockConfiguration`, `aws:SecureTransport`-deny bucket policy, and Lifecycle rule structure (no numeric duration, no tag-filter) are all present.
13. **Partial-batch behavior, same-batch redelivery, same-shard blocking, and actual remaining-invocation-payload behavior (item 8; corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 8; test assertions restated exactly, A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 2, to match §22.10's simplified, directly-stated bisection contract):** a batch containing one or more permanently unprocessable ("poison") records and several valid records. This test validates exactly five assertions, matching §22.10's corrected contract precisely:
    - **(a)** the function's own returned lowest-failed sequence number becomes the retry/checkpoint boundary directly — assert the event-source mapping checkpoints at that exact sequence number, not at some other point;
    - **(b)** the records **from that boundary onward** are exactly what the **next invocation's own batch** contains — not an assumption that a poison record is always cleanly isolated as its own one-record unit; a batch may contain multiple genuinely bad records, and this test must assert against the actual remaining-payload content the next invocation receives, never a simplified single-poison-record model;
    - **(c)** the test validates the **actual remaining payload content** of the next invocation directly (record identities/sequence numbers, not merely a count), consistent with (b);
    - **(d)** same-shard records remain blocked: while a shard's checkpoint is stalled on an unresolved failed sub-batch, later records on that **same** shard do not advance past it, **until either** the checkpoint advances (a subsequent invocation returns a well-formed response covering the remaining sub-batch) **or** the remaining sub-batch exhausts its retry budget (`MaximumRetryAttempts`/`MaximumRecordAgeInSeconds`) and is routed to the failure destination — assert both resolution paths, and assert the shard's checkpoint advances past the poison sub-batch once it is routed to the failure destination;
    - **(e)** other shards remain completely unaffected and continue processing independently — assert a record on a **different** shard is processed and checkpointed with no dependency on the first shard's stalled state.

    **And**, unchanged from Round 3: a simulated redelivery of an already-successfully-recorded later record in the same batch resolves to a confirmed idempotent duplicate (category (e)), not a duplicate write or an error (§22.10).
14. **IAM containment, corrected scope (item 10; corrected further, A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 1; corrected further, A1.4a.0 Round 4, items 4/6):** a policy-scope assertion test (or, if infeasible to automate, a documented manual-review requirement) confirming `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole` grants no `s3:GetObject`/`DeleteObject` on any evidence-class prefix or on the recovery bucket (§22.11's explicit non-grant, unchanged). **Corrected to a positive assertion for `s3:PutObject`/`s3:ListBucket` (Round 3, item 1 reverses Round 2's negative assertion here):** `s3:PutObject` **and** `s3:ListBucket` **are** granted on the recovery bucket, each scoped to the correct ARN shape (`PutObject` to the object ARN, `ListBucket` to the bucket ARN itself), and the grant carries the `s3:ResourceAccount` (not `aws:ResourceAccount` — Round 4, item 5) same-account condition. **Corrected to a positive assertion for `dynamodb:ListStreams` (Round 4, item 4 reverses Round 2/Round 3's negative assertion here):** `dynamodb:ListStreams` **is** granted, as its own separate statement scoped to `Resource: "*"`, distinct from the ARN-scoped `GetRecords`/`GetShardIterator`/`DescribeStream` statement — not omitted. **Redrive-credential assertion, corrected (Round 4, item 6 — replaces the prior "separate IAM principal" assertion, which described a resource this design no longer provisions):** a separate assertion (or documented manual-review requirement, since this is an operational credential-configuration fact rather than a deployable resource this test suite can render and inspect the way it inspects `infra/resources/*.yml`) confirming `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole` itself is never granted `s3:GetObject` on the recovery bucket, and confirming the redrive command's own dependency-construction path (`operator_cli/main.py`'s `disposal-recorder redrive` dispatch branch) constructs its S3 client from the operator's own resolved credentials (the CLI's existing `AwsClientFactory` mechanism), never from `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole`'s own credentials or a second, dedicated IAM role.
15. **Recursion prevention:** `DisposalRepository.put_disposal_record`'s own `PutItem` must never itself be misclassified as a new TTL-driven `REMOVE` event by a subsequent invocation — already structurally guaranteed by §22.4's exact `eventName == "REMOVE"` filter (a `PutItem` never generates a `REMOVE` stream record), but listed here as an explicit required regression test rather than left as an implicit structural property no test directly proves. **A second recursion-prevention test, new (item 2, item 9):** a recovery-bucket object deletion (via its own Lifecycle rule, simulated) must never be delivered to `evidenceDisposalRecorder`'s S3-path event source — assert this via the EventBridge rule's own bucket-name constraint (§22.5.2) rejecting a fixture event whose `detail.bucket.name` is the recovery bucket's name.
16. **Duplicate deliveries, general case:** ordinary at-least-once redelivery of a single already-recorded event resolves to a confirmed idempotent duplicate (§22.7 category (e)), composing §22.2's determinism and §22.3's verification.
17. **End-to-end happy path, both sources:** a genuine TTL disposal and a genuine S3 Lifecycle permanent-deletion disposal each produce exactly one correctly-populated `DisposalRecord`, from raw event through to the persisted item, with no manual mocking of the identity computation itself.
18. **Deployment-to-CLI configuration ordering (new, A1.4a.0 Round 5, item 1, §22.9.5; reconfirmed to cover the strengthened validator, A1.4a.0 Round 6, item 2):** a test proving `validate_disposal_recorder_config` resolves and passes **before** the redrive command's `GetObject` call is ever reached — a malformed or placeholder stage configuration (e.g., a `disposal_recorder_function_arn` still containing `:000000000000:`, or a blank `metadata_table_stream_arn`) short-circuits with `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR` before `AwsClientFactory` is ever constructed and before any AWS credential is exercised — mirroring the ordering `validate_scheduler_config`'s own existing test coverage presumably proves for `rcp audit schedule` (cited as precedent by analogy to `operator_cli/services.py:232-236`'s confirmed call ordering, not independently re-verified by this correction). This test's fixtures must exercise the **strengthened** validator (item 19 below), not merely the Round 5 placeholder-only version — a fixture using a structurally malformed (not merely blank/placeholder) field must equally short-circuit before any AWS call.
19. **Structural validation, one positive and one negative case per rule (new, A1.4a.0 Round 6, item 2, §22.9.5; extended, A1.4a.0 Round 7, items 2/3/4):** for each of the four structural regex checks — `DISPOSAL_RECOVERY_BUCKET_NAME_PATTERN`, `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_EVENT_SOURCE_MAPPING_UUID_PATTERN`, `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_FUNCTION_ARN_PATTERN`, `METADATA_TABLE_STREAM_ARN_PATTERN` — one positive fixture (a well-formed value for that field, all other fields also well-formed) and at least one negative fixture (a non-empty, non-placeholder value that fails that field's own structural pattern specifically — e.g. a bucket name containing an uppercase letter or underscore; a bucket name formatted as an IPv4 address; a bucket name with two consecutive periods; a UUID missing a hyphen or containing a non-hex character; a Lambda ARN missing the `function:` segment or with a non-12-digit account segment; a DynamoDB stream ARN missing the `/stream/{label}` suffix) — asserting `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR` names exactly the malformed field and no other. Each negative fixture must be distinct from Round 5's existing placeholder/empty-value negative cases — these are non-empty, non-placeholder-looking values that are nonetheless structurally invalid, proving the strengthened validator catches what the Round 5 placeholder-only checks could not. **Additional required fixtures, A1.4a.0 Round 7:**
    - **Commercial-partition lock (item 2):** a negative fixture for `disposal_recorder_function_arn` and one for `metadata_table_stream_arn`, each using a non-commercial-partition ARN (e.g. `arn:aws-us-gov:lambda:us-gov-west-1:123456789012:function:evidenceDisposalRecorder`, `arn:aws-cn:dynamodb:cn-north-1:123456789012:table/x/stream/2024-01-01T00:00:00.000`) — asserting `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR` names the field, proving the literal `arn:aws:` prefix correctly rejects the `aws-us-gov`/`aws-cn` partition segments and that removing the region group's `(?:-gov)?` component did not narrow acceptance of any actually-supported commercial region.
    - **Variable-precision stream label (item 4):** for `metadata_table_stream_arn`'s `stream_label` component specifically, at least two distinct **positive** fixtures — one with a 3-fractional-digit stream label (e.g. `.291`, matching AWS's own illustrative example) and one with a different fractional-second digit count or no fractional component at all (e.g. `.1234567` or no trailing `.` at all) — both accepted, proving the corrected `(?:\.\d+)?` group's variable/optional precision is genuinely honored and not silently still expecting exactly three digits; retain a negative fixture where the stream-label segment is not date/time-shaped at all (e.g. a non-numeric or malformed segment after `/stream/`), proving the corrected pattern still rejects genuine structural malformation, not merely relaxed into accepting an arbitrary trailing string.
    - **Reserved-suffix rejection — two independently-documented AWS rules, each with its own required, explicitly-named negative fixture, never merged into one test case (new, A1.4a.0 Round 9, item 1):** **(i)** a negative fixture for `disposal_recovery_bucket_name` ending in `--x-s3` (AWS's directory-bucket-name reservation) — its own explicitly-named fixture, distinct from (ii); **(ii)** a separate, new negative fixture for `disposal_recovery_bucket_name` ending in the literal suffix `-an` (AWS's account-regional-namespace bucket-creation reservation — a genuinely separate AWS rule from `--x-s3`, newly added to the pattern by this correction). Both fixtures assert `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR` names `disposal_recovery_bucket_name`; neither fixture's data or assertions may be shared with or substituted for the other's, since they exercise two independently-documented AWS rules, not two instances of the same rule.
20. **Cross-field region consistency — bound to `StageConfig.region`, not merely pairwise (new, A1.4a.0 Round 6, item 2, §22.9.5; corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 7, item 1 — the single pairwise check is now three independently-named checks):**
    - **(a) Positive, all three checks passing:** a fixture where `disposal_recorder_function_arn` and `metadata_table_stream_arn` share the identical region, and that shared region equals the fixture's own `StageConfig.region` value (validation passes on all three region checks).
    - **(b) Negative — ARN-vs-ARN region mismatch (retained from Round 6):** the two ARNs' own regions differ from each other (e.g. `disposal_recorder_function_arn` in `us-west-2`, `metadata_table_stream_arn` in `us-east-1`), regardless of which, if either, matches `StageConfig.region` — asserting `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR` names the ARN-vs-ARN region-mismatch rule specifically.
    - **(c) Negative — "wrong-stage-region" (new, A1.4a.0 Round 7, item 1):** the two ARNs' own regions **agree with each other** (both `us-west-2`, for example) but **disagree with `StageConfig.region`** (e.g. the fixture's `StageConfig.region` is `us-east-1`) — asserting `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR` names the function-ARN-region-vs-`StageConfig.region` and stream-ARN-region-vs-`StageConfig.region` rules specifically, and does **not** name the ARN-vs-ARN region-mismatch rule from (b), since the two ARNs are internally consistent with each other in this fixture — proving this is a distinct, independently detected defect from (b), not the same check reached two different ways.
    - **(d) Negative — account mismatch (retained from Round 6, unaffected by this round's region-binding correction):** matching region, differing account-ID segments between the two ARNs — asserting `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR` names the account-mismatch rule specifically, distinct from every region-shaped rule above.
21. **Stream table-identity match against `StageConfig.audit_metadata_table`, positive and negative (new, A1.4a.0 Round 6, item 2, §22.9.5):** a positive fixture where `metadata_table_stream_arn`'s own `table/{table-name}/stream/...` segment equals the fixture's `audit_metadata_table` value; a negative fixture where the stream ARN is otherwise well-formed and same-account/same-region-consistent (passes item 20's check) but its table-name segment names a different table than `audit_metadata_table` — asserting `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR` names this specific mismatch, proving the check catches a wrong-table stream ARN that the region/account check alone cannot detect.
22. **Redrive identity-mismatch rejection, one test per configured identity check (new, A1.4a.0 Round 5, item 1, §22.9.5):** four dedicated negative tests, each holding the other three configured identity values correct and mismatching exactly one, asserting the redrive command rejects fail-closed with the "recovery object failed envelope/provenance validation" outcome (§22.9.3) before shared record processing is ever invoked: **(a)** a recovered object's key whose `aws/lambda/{esm-uuid}/...` prefix does not match the configured `disposal_recorder_event_source_mapping_uuid`; **(b)** a recovered object whose body `requestContext.functionArn` does not match the configured `disposal_recorder_function_arn`; **(c)** a recovered object whose `DDBStreamBatchInfo.streamArn` does not match the configured `metadata_table_stream_arn`; **(d)** a recovered object whose `DDBStreamBatchInfo.streamArn` matches but a per-record `eventSourceARN` within its `payload` does not.
