Extended with the identical two-layer coverage for `disposal-recorder redrive`: `dispatch_disposal_recorder_redrive` tested directly against fake collaborators (proving the construction-time recovery-bucket guarantee plus the six numbered runtime envelope/provenance checks, item 7, run before shared processing is ever invoked, and that a validation failure short-circuits cleanly), and `main()` end-to-end composition coverage proving `operator_cli/main.py`'s new dispatch branch constructs the correct dependencies — including, per §22.9.5 (A1.4a.0 Round 5, item 1), that `validate_disposal_recorder_config` resolves and passes before `AwsClientFactory` is constructed.
- `tests/unit/test_operator_cli_result.py` — **new modified-file entry (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 11):** already exists today (already covers `HoldOperationResult` rendering, confirmed present in this branch's current diff). Extended for `DisposalRedriveResult` rendering/sanitization coverage, including the envelope/provenance-validation-rejection outcome (item 7) rendering as a clear, non-leaking message.
- `tests/unit/test_handler_import_smoke.py` — **reclassified from "New tests" to "Modified tests" (A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 8 — this file already exists today, confirmed by direct inspection, and already establishes the "Startup import validation — fail fast on missing critical modules" pattern this repository's other handlers use — `apps/backend/handlers/aggregation_handler.py`, `audit_finalization_handler.py`, `orchestrator_handler.py`, `scheduled_execution_handler.py` all follow it; a file that already exists and is merely extended belongs here, never under "New tests," which Round 3's inventory incorrectly did).** A1.4a implementation adds `evidence_disposal_recorder_handler` to this file's existing import-smoke coverage, following the identical pattern already established for the other four handlers.
- `tests/unit/test_infra_configuration.py` — **reclassified from "New tests" to "Modified tests" (A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 8 — this file already exists today, confirmed by direct inspection, 603 lines, asserting on `infra/serverless.yml`/`infra/resources/*.yml`'s raw content; Round 3's inventory listed it under "New tests" while its own descriptive text already said "extended, not newly created," a self-contradiction corrected by this relocation).** A1.4a implementation extends this file with assertions for: the new recovery bucket and its co-located notification rule/logical-ID (item 10(c)), including the recovery-bucket-notification rule's own `RetryPolicy` and `EvidenceDisposalRecoveryNotificationFailedInvocationsAlarm` (Round 4, item 7, §22.9.2); the event-source mapping's `MetricsConfig: { Metrics: ["EventCount"] }` block (Round 4, item 1, §22.10); the new IAM role including the corrected `s3:PutObject`/`s3:ListBucket`/`s3:ResourceAccount`-condition grant (item 1) and the restored, separately-scoped `dynamodb:ListStreams` statement (Round 4, item 4, §22.11); the new function block; the new `evidence-retention-dlq.yml` `QueuePolicy` scoped to exactly two rule ARNs (item 10(d)); and the new `custom.custodyPeriodDays.disposal_recovery` rendering (item 9). §22.13 item 12's recovery-bucket security assertions belong here. **Also extended (new, A1.4a.0 Round 5, item 1, §22.9.5; corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 6, item 3; corrected again — genuine rendered-template validation required, not raw-text assertion, A1.4a.0 Round 7, item 5; render mechanism, terminology, and fragment-provenance framing all corrected again, A1.4a.0 Round 8, items 1/2/3):** assertions that `infra/resources/dynamodb.yml` renders the reinstated `MetadataTableStreamArn` Output (raw-text assertion, consistent with this file's own established convention, sufficient for this single same-file Output); and, for the two new Outputs specifically, a **genuine rendered-CloudFormation-template test, not a raw-text/string-presence assertion on `infra/resources/*.yml`'s YAML content.** Round 6's own description of this test — asserting the Outputs' `Fn::GetAtt`/`Ref` intrinsics are textually present in the fragment file, and that the import line is textually present in `infra/serverless.yml` — is corrected as insufficient: it does not prove either intrinsic actually names something present in a genuinely rendered template's resource graph; does not prove the target resource is the expected type; and does not prove the fragment is genuinely wired into the stack rather than merely textually adjacent to an import line that could itself be malformed or misindented.

  **Isolated render-harness contract, required precisely (corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 8, item 1; made concretely executable and repository-verified, A1.4a.0 Round 9, item 2 — replaces Round 8's own "copies the minimum necessary repository content ... plus whatever else Serverless Framework's `${file(...)}` resolution requires" framing, which Product Strategy correctly found too vague to implement):** this repository's real, checked-in `config/custody_periods.json` defines exactly six stage-value keys today, all empty `{}` objects (`raw_evidence`, `aggregate_metadata`, `intelligence`, `report`, `certificate`, `operational_durations.retention_marker`) — and `infra/serverless.yml`'s and `infra/resources/s3.yml`'s own existing comments already document, in this codebase's own words, that referencing any of these unconfigured keys via `${file(...)}` fails Serverless Framework variable resolution at package/deploy time with an unresolved-variable error. This correction's own new `operational_durations.disposal_recovery` key (Round 3, item 9) adds a seventh such key, but does not create this pre-existing condition — `sls package --stage dev` against the real, unmodified repository fails today, before A1.4a implementation even begins, for reasons entirely unrelated to this test's own intent. A direct render against the real repository is therefore never what this test performs. The required test instead uses an isolated render harness, defined completely below:

  - **(a) Exact repository content copied — every path named explicitly, each confirmed present by direct inspection of this repository as of this correction (A1.4a.0 Round 9, item 2):** `infra/serverless.yml`; `infra/package.json`; `infra/package-lock.json` (confirmed to be the actual, present lockfile — this repository uses npm, not yarn/pnpm; no `yarn.lock`/`pnpm-lock.yaml` exists in `infra/`); `infra/plugins/` (confirmed present, containing `stage-guard.js` — the local plugin `infra/serverless.yml`'s own `plugins:` block already references as `./plugins/stage-guard`, Node's own extension-resolution filling in `.js`); `infra/resources/` (all `*.yml` fragment files — confirmed present today: `dynamodb.yml`, `evidence-retention-dlq.yml`, `iam.yml`, `phase4-aggregation-iam.yml`, `s3.yml`, `scheduler.yml`, plus whichever of A1.4a's own new fragment files — `evidence-disposal-recorder-outputs.yml`, `evidence-disposal-recovery-bucket.yml`, `evidence-disposal-recorder-iam.yml` — exist in the working tree at the time the harness runs); `config/` (confirmed present; `infra/serverless.yml`'s `custom.custodyPeriodDays` block resolves values via `${file(../config/custody_periods.json):...}` specifically — copying the full `config/` directory, including `config/defaults/` and `config/stages/`, is simpler and harmless even though only `config/custody_periods.json` is directly consumed by this `${file(...)}` resolution); `apps/`; `packages/`; `src/` (all three confirmed required directly from `infra/serverless.yml`'s own `package.patterns` block, `infra/serverless.yml:143-167`, which references `../apps/__init__.py`, `../apps/backend/**`, `../packages/__init__.py` and multiple `../packages/<name>/**` entries, and multiple `../src/release_confidence_platform/<name>/**` entries); `apps/backend/requirements.txt` (confirmed present and referenced directly by `custom.pythonRequirements.fileName: ../apps/backend/requirements.txt`, `infra/serverless.yml:74`, consumed by the `serverless-python-requirements` plugin). **Beyond this named list, the harness must additionally copy every further path this repository's own `infra/serverless.yml` `package.patterns` block and/or Serverless Framework's own file-based variable resolution actually consumes, confirmed by direct inspection of that block's own content immediately before implementation, not assumed complete by this documentation-only correction's own enumeration alone** — this named list reflects direct inspection performed for this Round 9 correction and is not guessed, but `package.patterns` may itself change before A1.4a implementation begins. All of the above is copied into a **temporary directory**, never modifying the real, checked-in repository files.
  - **(a1) Temporary-directory strategy:** a fresh, uniquely-named temporary directory created via Python's `tempfile.mkdtemp()` (or `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()` as a context manager, guaranteeing cleanup on both success and exception paths — the harness's own implementation may choose either, provided cleanup, item (f) below, is unconditional). The repository content named in (a) is copied into this directory only, preserving the same relative path layout (`infra/`, `config/`, `apps/`, `packages/`, `src/` as siblings) the real repository uses, since `infra/serverless.yml`'s own `${file(../config/custody_periods.json):...}` and `package.patterns`'s own `../apps/**`-style relative paths depend on that exact sibling layout to resolve correctly.
  - **(b) Sentinel values — integer only, never a string (corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 9, item 3 — see the dedicated correction immediately following this contract):** within that temporary copy **only**, the harness injects a single, consistent, schema-valid positive integer sentinel (e.g. `9999`) into every stage/class key `${file(...)}` resolution requires in the temporary copy's `config/custody_periods.json`, including the new `operational_durations.disposal_recovery` key — never the string `"SENTINEL_TEST_VALUE_DO_NOT_USE"` Round 8 also allowed, which would produce a type-invalid CloudFormation template (below).
  - **(c) Node.js dependency provisioning — into the temporary copy only, never the real repository:** the harness runs `npm ci` inside the temporary copy's own `infra/` directory, using the `infra/package-lock.json` copied into that same temporary directory in step (a). `npm ci` installs the exact versions the lockfile pins (confirmed today: Serverless Framework `3.40.0`, `serverless-python-requirements` `6.1.2` — `infra/package-lock.json`), regardless of the caret ranges (`^3.38.0`, `^6.1.2`) `infra/package.json` itself declares, producing a `node_modules/` directory within the **temporary** copy only. The real repository's own `infra/node_modules/` (already present on disk today from prior local development) is never read, written, or otherwise touched by this step. **Prerequisite, stated explicitly (A1.4a.0 Round 10, item 4):** `npm ci` requires either npm-registry access or a complete, usable warm npm cache satisfying every package the lockfile pins — this is a controlled local/CI dependency-provisioning prerequisite the harness assumes the environment it runs in already satisfies, not something this documentation-only correction can make unconditionally offline by assertion.
  - **(d) Serverless Framework executable and version source:** the harness invokes the Serverless Framework binary installed into the temporary copy's own `node_modules/.bin/` by step (c) (e.g. `node_modules/.bin/serverless` or the equivalent `sls` alias) — **never** a globally-installed `serverless`/`sls` binary, an ambient `npx`-resolved version, or any other version not pinned by this repository's own `infra/package-lock.json`. This is a genuine, confirmed risk, not a theoretical one: a bare `npx serverless --version` on a typical development machine can resolve to a cached major version (e.g. `4.x`) entirely different from, and potentially behaviorally incompatible with, this repository's own pinned `3.40.0` — the harness must not be exposed to that ambiguity.
  - **(e) Exact invocation:** `node_modules/.bin/serverless package --stage dev --package <temporary-package-output-directory>`, run with the temporary copy's own `infra/` directory as the working directory — `--package` is Serverless Framework's own documented flag for directing package output to an explicit directory, used here specifically so nothing is ever written to the default `.serverless/` under the working directory (item (g) below). `<temporary-package-output-directory>` is itself a second, dedicated temporary directory (or a subdirectory of the one from (a1)), distinct from the temporary repository-content copy, created the same way (`tempfile.mkdtemp()`).
  - **(f) Generated CloudFormation template location, confirmed by direct inspection of this repository's own prior packaging output — not a guess:** Serverless Framework v3 (the pinned major version, `3.40.0`) writes `cloudformation-template-create-stack.json` and `cloudformation-template-update-stack.json` directly into the package-output directory named by `--package`; both file names are confirmed present today in this repository's own `infra/.serverless/` (a pre-existing, gitignored local artifact directory from prior manual `serverless package`/`deploy` use — `infra/.gitignore` line 27, `infra/.serverless/`). The harness inspects `cloudformation-template-update-stack.json` — the full, merged resources/outputs document — for the static-referential-integrity assertions this test requires (below).
  - **(g) Cleanup, unconditional:** both the temporary repository-content copy (a1) and the temporary package-output directory (e) are removed after the test completes, whether it passes or fails (e.g. via `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()`'s own context-manager cleanup, or an explicit `try`/`finally`) — no temporary artifact from this harness persists on disk after the test run ends.
  - **(h) Explicit assertion that the real, authoritative repository checkout is never touched:** the test asserts, both before and after it runs, that the real repository's own `infra/.serverless/` path's state is unchanged by this test run specifically — this repository's `infra/.serverless/` directory may already exist on disk today (a pre-existing, gitignored local artifact from unrelated prior manual packaging, confirmed above), so the assertion is a before/after content-and-mtime comparison of that path (or, if it is genuinely absent in a given environment, an assertion that it remains absent), never a bare "does not exist" assertion that would be false in an environment where it already legitimately exists for unrelated reasons. This is what proves the `--package`-redirected output in (e) genuinely prevented any write to the real, authoritative checkout, rather than merely asserting the flag was passed.
  - **(i) Python-dependency provisioning — reclassified from an unconditional "fully offline" claim to a stated, honest environment prerequisite (resolution h2 selected, A1.4a.0 Round 9, item 2):** `infra/serverless.yml`'s `custom.pythonRequirements` configuration (`fileName: ../apps/backend/requirements.txt`, `slim: true`, `dockerizePip: non-linux`, `infra/serverless.yml:73-76`) means the `serverless-python-requirements` plugin runs during **every** `sls package` invocation, including this harness's, and its own dependency-layer-building step is host-environment-dependent, not something this documentation-only correction can make unconditionally offline by assertion: on a **Linux** host (the typical CI environment), `dockerizePip: non-linux` means the plugin builds `apps/backend/requirements.txt`'s dependencies using the host's own native Python/pip directly — no Docker daemon is invoked — but this still requires network access to install those dependencies from PyPI (or an already-warm local pip cache) unless one is pre-populated; on a **non-Linux** host (e.g. this repository's own local macOS development environment), the identical configuration instead invokes Docker, requiring a pre-available Docker daemon. **Prerequisite, stated explicitly (A1.4a.0 Round 10, item 4):** on non-Linux hosts, this Docker-based Python packaging step requires a running Docker daemon and either the required build image already cached locally or container-registry access to pull it — the harness cannot proceed on a non-Linux host with Docker unavailable, no image cached, and no registry access. This correction therefore states the boundary precisely rather than glossing over it: the harness's **no-AWS-credentials/no-AWS-call/no-AWS-mutation** guarantee (item (j) below) is unconditional and unaffected by this; its **Python-dependency-provisioning** step, and the Node.js-dependency-provisioning prerequisite stated in item (c) above, are controlled local/CI dependency-provisioning prerequisites this harness assumes the environment it runs in already satisfies (a pre-available Docker daemon plus a cached image or registry access on non-Linux hosts; network/cache access to PyPI on Linux hosts; npm-registry access or a warm npm cache for item (c)) — never folded into, or described as satisfying, an unconditional "fully offline" claim, and never weakening the unconditional prohibition on AWS credentials, AWS API calls, AWS mutation, or deployment stated elsewhere in this contract.
  - **(j) No AWS interaction, unconditional and unaffected by (i):** no AWS deployment, no AWS mutation, and no AWS credentials are required or used anywhere in this harness, regardless of host platform — this guarantee concerns AWS specifically and is independent of item (i)'s Python-dependency-provisioning prerequisite.
  - **(k) Required assertions:** **(i)** the real, checked-in `config/custody_periods.json` is byte-for-byte identical before and after the test runs (a before/after hash or content comparison — the harness must never write to the real file); **(ii)** a **separate, additional** test proves that ordinary packaging against the real, unmodified, authoritative `config/custody_periods.json` still fails closed exactly as documented (corrected command shape, A1.4a.0 Round 9, item 4 — see the dedicated correction immediately following item 3, below); **(iii)** the sentinel value exists only within the temporary test workspace and is asserted, explicitly, to never be written to, copied into, or otherwise leak into the real `config/custody_periods.json` or any other checked-in file — a hard testing invariant, not an incidental property; **(iv)** item (h)'s real-repository-`infra/.serverless`-unchanged assertion; **(v)** item (g)'s cleanup has genuinely occurred — neither temporary directory from this test run remains on disk after the test completes.

  **Integer-only sentinel value, corrected (A1.4a.0 Round 9, item 3 — removes Round 8's string-sentinel option entirely):** Round 8 allowed the sentinel injected in bullet (b) above to be either the string `"SENTINEL_TEST_VALUE_DO_NOT_USE"` or the integer `9999`. The string option is removed outright. Every one of the seven custody/operational-duration values the harness's temporary `config/custody_periods.json` copy needs (`raw_evidence`, `aggregate_metadata`, `intelligence`, `report`, `certificate`, `operational_durations.retention_marker`, `operational_durations.disposal_recovery`) must be injected as the identical, single, schema-valid, positive, non-Boolean integer sentinel — `9999`, retained from Round 8's own already-proposed value — never a string. **Reason:** `CustodyPeriodConfigLoader.resolve()`'s own existing validation rules (documented elsewhere in this ADR/TD) already reject `null`, Boolean, string, float, zero, and negative values for these exact fields, and the rendered CloudFormation template's own `LifecycleConfiguration.Rules[].Expiration.Days` / `NoncurrentVersionExpiration.NoncurrentDays` properties are **integer-typed** CloudFormation properties — injecting a string value there would produce a **type-invalid CloudFormation template** as this test's own fixture, which is not acceptable test design: the test must validate real, schema-shaped rendering, not deliberately construct a type-invalid template and then work around that self-inflicted invalidity. **The sentinel's non-production status was never established by its string-versus-integer shape, and remains established by the same five properties already required since Round 8, unaffected by this correction:** temporary-directory isolation (the sentinel is never written to the real, checked-in file, bullet (a1) above); explicit test-only naming/documentation in the test code itself (a comment/docstring stating this is a test sentinel, never a real custody decision); the required before/after hashing of the real, authoritative `config/custody_periods.json` (bullet (k)(i) above, proving the real file was never touched); zero leakage into any tracked file (bullet (k)(iii) above); and cleanup of the temporary output afterward (bullet (g) above). The integer-only correction here is made purely because a string value would be schema-invalid CloudFormation input, not because the sentinel's string shape was ever what made it safe.

  **Authoritative-config-failure invocation, corrected command shape (A1.4a.0 Round 9, item 4 — completes bullet (k)(ii) above, which Round 8 required but did not fully specify to match this round's corrected harness contract):** the separate, additional test proving `sls package --stage dev` against the real, unmodified, authoritative `config/custody_periods.json` still fails closed must **also** use the `--package <temporary-output-directory>` flag, exactly as the sentinel-injected harness run does (bullet (e) above) — using a **different** temporary output directory than the sentinel-injected run's own (the two invocations are logically independent test cases and should not share mutable output-directory state; reusing the same directory after its own cleanup would also be acceptable, since the flag's redirection is what matters, not directory identity, but a fresh, dedicated `tempfile.mkdtemp()` per invocation is simpler to reason about and is the required approach) — so that even this expected-to-fail invocation never writes a `.serverless`/package artifact into the real, authoritative repository checkout, consistent with bullet (h)'s same requirement applying here too. **Exact command shape:** `node_modules/.bin/serverless package --stage dev --package <second-temporary-output-directory>`, run against a temporary copy of the repository content that — unlike the sentinel-injected harness run — copies the real, unmodified, authoritative `config/custody_periods.json` verbatim, injecting no sentinel value into it at all (copying the real file is necessary here only to exercise Serverless Framework's own file-based variable resolution in a directory Serverless Framework can write package output outside of; it is not otherwise modified). This test must assert: **(a)** the command exits non-zero / raises the expected unresolved-variable Serverless Framework error, exactly as this ADR/TD's own existing text already documents this behavior in general terms; **(b)** the error implicates all seven of the currently-unresolved duration paths that exist once `operational_durations.disposal_recovery` is added as a seventh key alongside the six that already exist today (five evidentiary classes plus `retention_marker`) — stated precisely, since this document already establishes the "six today, seven once this correction's own key is added" count; **(c)** no `.serverless` directory or package artifact is created in the real, authoritative repository checkout by this failing invocation either — the identical bullet (h) assertion, applied to this second invocation.

  **What the harness's render can prove, corrected from "fully resolved" to "generated/merged," with a precise static referential-integrity contract (corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 8, item 2 — replaces Round 7's "fully-resolved CloudFormation template"/"intrinsics resolve without error" framing, which overclaimed what a local Serverless Framework render actually produces):** a Serverless-Framework-rendered template is a **generated/merged** template — Serverless-level `${self:...}`/`${file(...)}` variable substitutions applied, and every imported `resources:` fragment merged into one document — never a "fully resolved" template in the sense CloudFormation's own intrinsic-function resolution provides, which happens only at actual stack deployment/update time, against real, deployed resource physical IDs. A local render can prove only **static referential integrity**: that a reference is syntactically well-formed and names something that exists in the template's own `Resources:` section — never that AWS itself would successfully resolve it at deploy time. Against the harness's second-pass render (the two-pass sequence, §22.9.5), the test must prove, together, not merely each in isolation:

  - **(a)** both `EvidenceDisposalRecorderFunctionArn` and `EvidenceDisposalRecorderEventSourceMappingUuid` exist as real `Outputs:` entries in the rendered template;
  - **(b)** each Output's own intrinsic (`Fn::GetAtt`/`Ref`) names an **exact** logical ID that is genuinely present as a top-level key in the rendered template's own `Resources:` section — a static lookup, not a resolution;
  - **(c)** the function-ARN Output's target resource has `Type: AWS::Lambda::Function` in the rendered template;
  - **(d)** the ESM-UUID Output's target resource has `Type: AWS::Lambda::EventSourceMapping` in the rendered template;
  - **(e)** no target logical ID referenced by either Output is missing from `Resources:` — a `Resources:` block is a uniquely-keyed dict, so a correct static lookup under (b) already structurally guarantees this; it is not a separate check beyond (b), and must not be treated or scored as one.

  **This local, static check does not and cannot prove AWS would successfully deploy this template, or that the intrinsics would resolve to correct runtime values once deployed** — that remains a genuinely separate, staging-time concern this design continues to flag as requiring confirmation, never claimed as proven by the local render.

  **Fragment-import validation, split into a source-level check and a rendered-template-level check (corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 8, item 3 — removes the prior framing's implication that a merged template itself shows "which fragment contributed which resource" or exposes a "resolved resources list" the fragment "appears in"; that provenance information does not survive Serverless Framework's merge of imported `resources:` fragments into one generated/merged template and is not a real property of the rendered output):** two genuinely distinct proofs together establish that the fragment is genuinely wired in, never one alone:

  - **(a) source-level assertion** — operating on `infra/serverless.yml`'s own raw YAML text directly (the same raw-text/string-presence style this file already uses elsewhere for simpler cases) — proving the `resources:` list literally contains the import line `- ${file(resources/evidence-disposal-recorder-outputs.yml)}`;
  - **(b) rendered-template assertions** — the (a)–(e) static-referential-integrity assertions immediately above — proving the two Outputs the fragment is supposed to contribute are genuinely present, with correctly-typed targets, in the actual generated/merged template produced by packaging the full `infra/serverless.yml`, including this new import.

  (a) and (b) together prove the fragment is genuinely wired in and contributes real content, because (a) confirms the import exists in source and (b) confirms the resulting template contains the expected content — never because the rendered template itself retains or exposes any notion of a "resolved resources list" the fragment "appears in."

  **No actual AWS deployment is required for this validation — a local, isolated template-render/package step is sufficient and is what this test requires.** The exact Serverless-Framework-rendered logical IDs both Outputs' intrinsics target (`Fn::GetAtt`'s `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaFunction`-shaped target, `Ref`'s event-source-mapping target) are, per §22.9.5's own corrected two-pass sequence (Round 8, item 4), themselves confirmed via this identical isolated harness's first-pass render as a required implementation-time first-pass render before the Output fragment is finalized (phrasing corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 9, item 5) — not left open for this test to merely assume; the implementation-time first-pass render (two-pass sequence step 2/3, wording corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 10, item 3) and this test's own final render (two-pass sequence step 5) use the same isolated-harness mechanism and reinforce each other, one confirming the logical IDs to hard-code into the Outputs' own intrinsics, the other proving those intrinsics statically reference present, correctly typed resources once their targets are recorded (phrasing corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 9, item 5 — "resolve correctly once hard-coded" was exactly the kind of overclaiming language Round 8, item 2 was supposed to have eliminated everywhere; this instance was missed). This is a materially stronger requirement than Round 6's raw-text assertion and supersedes it entirely for these two Outputs; `infra/resources/dynamodb.yml`'s single, same-file `MetadataTableStreamArn` Output assertion (above) is unaffected by this correction and remains a raw-text assertion, consistent with this file's own existing convention for that simpler, non-cross-file case.

**New tests:**
- `tests/unit/config/test_stage_config.py` — **new (new, A1.4a.0 Round 5, item 1, §22.9.5 — no such file exists today, confirmed by direct inspection; `tests/unit/config/` today contains only `test_custody_period_config.py` and `__init__.py`; coverage scope expanded, A1.4a.0 Round 6, item 2).** Extended/created with `validate_disposal_recorder_config`'s own positive/negative coverage, mirroring `test_custody_period_config.py`'s existing negative-test conventions (§22.14's own established citation pattern for that file, above): a positive case where all four new fields resolve cleanly; one negative case per field for a missing/blank value; one negative case per ARN-shaped field (`disposal_recorder_function_arn`, `metadata_table_stream_arn`) for a `:000000000000:` placeholder-account value; §22.13 items 19–21's structural, cross-field-consistency, and stream-table-identity coverage (one positive and at least one negative case per structural regex; region-mismatch and account-mismatch negative cases; a wrong-table stream-ARN negative case); and §22.13 item 18's ordering assertion — `validate_disposal_recorder_config` raising `DISPOSAL_RECORDER_CONFIG_ERROR` before `AwsClientFactory` construction is ever reached, now proven against both a placeholder-shaped and a structurally-malformed fixture.
- `tests/unit/evidence_retention/test_disposal_recorder.py` — §22.13's full matrix (unit-level, mirroring this module's existing `tests/unit/evidence_retention/` convention — this repository's existing test suite does distinguish `tests/unit/` from `tests/integration/`, confirmed by directory inspection, §22.14 item 12's explicit check).
- `tests/unit/evidence_retention/test_disposal_recorder_redrive.py` — the redrive command's own core-logic tests (§22.9.3), including the construction-time recovery-bucket guarantee plus the six numbered runtime envelope/provenance checks (item 7) as its own dedicated positive/negative coverage — including the four identity-mismatch rejection tests **§22.13 item 22** requires (A1.4a.0 Round 5, item 1; cross-reference corrected, A1.4a.0 Round 7, item 6(b) — the prior "item 19" citation predated Round 6's insertion of new items 18–21 ahead of the identity-mismatch tests, which shifted them to their current, re-verified position at item 22; this citation is re-derived directly against §22.13's own actual final numbering after this round's edits, not assumed) — distinct from `test_operator_cli_retention.py`'s CLI-composition-level coverage above.
- No integration-test-category equivalent is proposed by this correction beyond what `tests/integration/` (confirmed to exist as a distinct directory from `tests/unit/`) may already establish as a convention for Lambda-handler-level tests elsewhere in this repository; A1.4a implementation should follow whatever convention `tests/integration/` already establishes for the other four handlers, if any, rather than inventing a new one.

**Reclassification note (A1.4a.0 Round 4, item 8):** Round 3's inventory listed `tests/unit/test_handler_import_smoke.py` and `tests/unit/test_infra_configuration.py` under "New tests" even though each entry's own text stated the file already exists and is merely extended — an internal contradiction (a file that already exists and is extended is a modified file, never a new one). Both entries are relocated above to "Modified tests," with their existing descriptive text retained and only supplemented with the Round 4 additions (items 1/4/7) each file must now also cover. After this move, "Modified tests" and "New tests" are each fully recalculated above: no file appears in both lists, and no file is mischaracterized as new when it already exists today.

**Docs:** `docs/backend/a1_4a_disposal_recorder_implementation_plan.md`, `docs/backend/a1_4a_disposal_recorder_implementation_report.md`, `docs/qa/a1_4a_disposal_recorder_test_plan.md`, `docs/qa/a1_4a_disposal_recorder_test_report.md`.

No numeric custody-duration value, deployment action, or AWS mutation is introduced or implied by this section. Judgment calls made explicit rather than silently finalized, flagged for confirmation before A1.4a implementation adopts them verbatim: the exact disposal-identity canonicalization field list/order within the canonical-JSON contract (§22.2); the choice of a canonical source-event reference over a persisted digest for the new `DisposalRecord` fields (§7.3, §22.3); the exact native `OnFailure`-destination key-naming convention the redrive command's input contract deliberately does not assume (§22.9.3); the batch size of 25 and the EventBridge/Lambda-async retry parameters for planes (i)/(ii) (§22.10); and the decision to include the redrive command within A1.4a's own scope rather than a separately authorized, blocking subphase (§22.9.3). The exact partial-batch-failure strategy (§22.10, `ReportBatchItemFailures` plus `BisectBatchOnFunctionError` together) remains a lower-risk judgment call, since it composes two independently AWS-documented, already-supported mechanisms rather than introducing new infrastructure. **No longer a judgment call (A1.4a.0 Round 3, item 10(a); partially superseded, A1.4a.0 Round 5, item 1):** for the event-source-mapping's own `EventSourceArn` property and `EvidenceDisposalRecorderLambdaRole`'s own IAM statement — the two same-stack consumers Round 3 addressed — resolving the stream ARN via a same-stack `Fn::GetAtt` reference rather than a CloudFormation Output remains a firm instruction, not reopened. **This is no longer the complete picture:** §22.9.5 identifies a categorically different, external consumer (the operator CLI's own `StageConfig.metadata_table_stream_arn` field, needed for §22.9.3's redrive-validation checks 4/6) for which a same-stack reference is structurally unavailable, and reinstates the `MetadataTableStreamArn` Output in `infra/resources/dynamodb.yml` for that reason alone — see §22.4, §22.11, and §22.14's corrected inventory above, which now lists `infra/resources/dynamodb.yml` again, restored for this new, external-consumer reason and not as a reversal of Round 3's own, still-correct reasoning for its own consumer.
